package main

// Go-native Smithers lowering, executed inside the pinned TypeScript fork.
//
// This file is compiled into the fork's `cmd/tsc` package through the build
// overlay, so it can drive the fork's own parser, binder, checker, node
// factory, and printer directly. It replaces the identity transform for
// `LoweringInternal` requests: authored `.sm` statements are rewritten into
// Result variant constructions and nominal error dispatch on the real
// AST, and the fork's printer emits the lowered TypeScript together with an
// authored -> lowered source map.
//
// Nothing here inspects source text. Every decision is made from a resolved
// symbol or a checker type.

import (
	"context"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"fmt"
	"path"
	"sort"
	"strings"
	"unicode/utf16"

	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/ast"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/checker"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/collections"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/compiler"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/core"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/printer"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/scanner"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/sourcemap"
	"github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/tsc/internal/tspath"
)

const (
	// preludeLogicalPath is the compiler-owned prelude injected into every
	// internally lowered project. It declares the runtime representation of
	// `Result`, publishes its type name globally, and
	// declares the nominal `Error.match` intrinsic, so authored `.sm` files
	// never import anything.
	preludeLogicalPath = "__smithers_prelude.ts"
	preludeVirtualName = "/src/" + preludeLogicalPath
	preludeModuleBase  = "__smithers_prelude.js"

	// tempPrefix names the temporaries introduced by postfix propagation, and
	// matchPrefix those introduced by nominal error matching.
	tempPrefix  = "__smithersUnwrapped"
	matchPrefix = "__smithersMatched"
)

// preludeImports is the fixed, ordered set of prelude exports the lowering may
// reference, together with the local aliases it imports them under. The local
// names are only ever produced by the lowering; authored code is never matched
// against them.
var preludeImports = []struct {
	exported string
	local    string
	// typeOnly marks a specifier the emitter erases. A type-only specifier adds
	// nothing to the emitted JavaScript, so a file that gains only one keeps its
	// previous runtime bytes exactly.
	typeOnly bool
}{
	{exported: "SmithersOk", local: "__smithersOk"},
	{exported: "SmithersErr", local: "__smithersErr"},
	{exported: "Panic", local: "__smithersPanic"},
	{exported: "smithersMatchFailed", local: "__smithersMatchFailed"},
	{exported: "smithersTry", local: "__smithersTry"},
	{exported: "smithersTryPromise", local: "__smithersTryPromise"},
	{exported: "smithersResultAll", local: "__smithersResultAll"},
	{exported: "smithersRegisterError", local: "__smithersRegisterError"},
	{exported: "smithersErrorIs", local: "__smithersErrorIs"},
	{exported: "SmithersNominalError", local: "__SmithersNominalError", typeOnly: true},
}

func preludeLocalName(exported string) string {
	for _, entry := range preludeImports {
		if entry.exported == exported {
			return entry.local
		}
	}
	panic("unknown prelude export " + exported)
}

// preludeText is the compiler-owned prelude. The classes are the runtime
// representation of a Result; the `declare global` block publishes the type
// name authored `.sm` files use without importing
// anything, and declares `Error.match` so nominal matching resolves to a
// compiler-owned declaration rather than to a spelling.
const preludeText = `export class Panic extends Error {
	constructor(readonly cause?: unknown) {
		super(cause instanceof Error ? cause.message : cause === undefined ? "panic" : String(cause));
		this.name = "Panic";
	}
}
export function panic(cause?: unknown): never {
	throw new Panic(cause);
}
export class SmithersExpectFailed extends Panic {
    constructor(message: string, readonly reason: unknown) {
        super(reason);
		this.message = message;
        this.name = "SmithersExpectFailed";
    }
}
const smithersContextFrames: Map<Function, unknown>[] = [];
export abstract class Context {
	static context<C extends abstract new (...args: never[]) => Context>(this: C): InstanceType<C> {
		for (let index = smithersContextFrames.length - 1; index >= 0; index--) {
			// Bound to a local rather than read twice. compatibility.mdx
			// §Mandatory makes noUncheckedIndexedAccess mandatory and warns
			// that it is noisy in index loops, because TypeScript cannot
			// prove a for-loop index is in bounds; the narrowing it asks
			// for is this one.
			const frame = smithersContextFrames[index];
			if (frame === undefined) continue;
			const value = frame.get(this);
			if (value !== undefined) return value as InstanceType<C>;
		}
		throw new Panic("unsatisfied Context requirement");
	}
}
export interface SmithersLayer<P> {
	readonly __smithersLayer: { readonly provides: P };
	readonly providers: Map<Function, unknown>;
}
export type Layer<P> = SmithersLayer<P>;
export const Layer = {
	succeed<C extends abstract new (...args: never[]) => Context>(capability: C, implementation: InstanceType<C>): SmithersLayer<C> {
		return { __smithersLayer: { provides: capability }, providers: new Map([[capability, implementation]]) };
	},
	merge<const L extends readonly SmithersLayer<unknown>[]>(...layers: L): SmithersLayer<L[number] extends SmithersLayer<infer P> ? P : never> {
		const providers = new Map<Function, unknown>();
		for (const layer of layers) for (const [capability, implementation] of layer.providers) providers.set(capability, implementation);
		return { __smithersLayer: { provides: undefined as never }, providers };
	},
	provide<L extends SmithersLayer<unknown>, A>(layer: L, body: () => A): A {
		smithersContextFrames.push(layer.providers);
		try { return body(); } finally { smithersContextFrames.pop(); }
	},
};
function smithersCall<A>(fallback: A | (() => A)): A {
    return typeof fallback === "function" ? (fallback as () => A)() : fallback;
}
export class SmithersOk<A> {
    readonly ok: true = true;
    constructor(readonly value: A) { }
    isOk(): boolean { return true; }
    isError(): boolean { return false; }
    unwrap(): A { return this.value; }
    unwrapOr(fallback: A | (() => A)): A { return this.value; }
    expect(message: string): A { return this.value; }
    match<R>(handlers: { ok: (value: A) => R; error: (error: never) => R }): R { return handlers.ok(this.value); }
    map<B>(mapper: (value: A) => B): SmithersOk<B> { return new SmithersOk(mapper(this.value)); }
    mapError<F>(mapper: (error: never) => F): SmithersOk<A> { return this; }
    andThen<B, F>(mapper: (value: A) => SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<F>): SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<F> { return mapper(this.value); }
	// flatten collapses exactly one level of nesting and COMBINES the two
	// failure channels, as poc/src/runtime/result.ts does.
	//
	// Unlike every other member here, the signature is written over the whole
	// Ok|Err union through an explicit "this" parameter, and the two classes
	// declare it IDENTICALLY on purpose. A .flatten() call is made on the union,
	// and the checker forms a union call signature by INTERSECTING the
	// constituents' "this" types; two different ones would intersect into a type
	// the receiver is not assignable to, and every authored call site would fail
	// with TS2684 instead. One shared signature intersects with itself, so the
	// receiver is accepted and B/F/G are inferred from it: an outer Ok holding
	// Ok<B> | Err<F>, or an Err<G>, gives back Ok<B> | Err<F | G> — the
	// reference's Result<B, E | F>, with both failure channels combined rather
	// than one of them dropped. Calling it on a Result that is not nested is an
	// ordinary type error at the authored call site, exactly as it is on the
	// reference, whose declaration carries the same "this" constraint.
	flatten<B, F, G>(this: SmithersOk<SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<F>> | SmithersErr<G>): SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<F | G> {
		if (this instanceof SmithersErr) return this;
		const inner = this.value;
		if (!(inner instanceof SmithersOk || inner instanceof SmithersErr)) panic("Result.flatten requires a nested Result");
		return inner;
	}
	recover<B>(mapper: (error: never) => B): SmithersOk<A> { return this; }
	// tap observes the success variant and MUST NOT replace the Result it
	// observes: specification/failures.mdx, "Matching and Transformation",
	// lists tap/tapError among the required operations, and the observation is
	// defined to leave the observed Result unchanged. The receiver itself is
	// returned, not a copy, so identity is preserved as well as value.
	tap(observer: (value: A) => void): SmithersOk<A> { observer(this.value); return this; }
	tapError(observer: (error: never) => void): SmithersOk<A> { return this; }
	// tapBoth observes whichever variant is active — exactly one handler runs —
	// and, like tap and tapError, returns the receiver rather than a copy.
	// The handler object is validated the way poc/src/runtime/result.ts
	// validates it, so a missing branch is a panic rather than a silently
	// skipped observation; an observer that is not there is a defect, not a
	// choice not to observe.
	tapBoth(handlers: { ok: (value: A) => unknown; error: (error: never) => unknown }): SmithersOk<A> {
		if (typeof handlers?.ok !== "function" || typeof handlers.error !== "function") panic("Result.tapBoth requires ok and error handlers");
		handlers.ok(this.value);
		return this;
	}
	// The runtime type tag, matching poc/src/runtime/result.ts. Both variants
	// answer "Result" and not their own class name: String(r) must not tell an
	// authored program which variant it holds, because isOk/isError/match are
	// the sanctioned discriminators and a stringified tag that leaked the
	// variant would be a second, untyped one.
	get [Symbol.toStringTag](): string { return "Result"; }
}
export class SmithersErr<E> {
    readonly ok: false = false;
    constructor(readonly error: E) { }
    isOk(): boolean { return false; }
    isError(): boolean { return true; }
    unwrap(): never { throw this.error; }
    unwrapOr<A>(fallback: A | (() => A)): A { return smithersCall(fallback); }
    expect(message: string): never { throw new SmithersExpectFailed(message, this.error); }
    match<R>(handlers: { ok: (value: never) => R; error: (error: E) => R }): R { return handlers.error(this.error); }
    map<B>(mapper: (value: never) => B): SmithersErr<E> { return this; }
    mapError<F>(mapper: (error: E) => F): SmithersErr<F> { return new SmithersErr(mapper(this.error)); }
    andThen<B, F>(mapper: (value: never) => SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<F>): SmithersErr<E> { return this; }
	// The identical twin of SmithersOk.flatten, and identical DELIBERATELY: see
	// the comment there. The union call signature intersects the two "this"
	// types, so the two spellings must be the same one or no call site
	// type-checks. compiler/fork_result_member_surface_test.go asserts they
	// stay identical.
	flatten<B, F, G>(this: SmithersOk<SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<F>> | SmithersErr<G>): SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<F | G> {
		if (this instanceof SmithersErr) return this;
		const inner = this.value;
		if (!(inner instanceof SmithersOk || inner instanceof SmithersErr)) panic("Result.flatten requires a nested Result");
		return inner;
	}
	// recover handles a recoverable failure and MUST NOT intercept the
	// distinguished panic channel: specification/failures.mdx, "Panic", makes a
	// panic a defect only an explicit boundary may observe, and
	// poc/src/runtime/result.ts passes it through unchanged. A Panic therefore
	// survives the call as the error variant of the result.
	//
	// The mapper parameter stays E rather than Exclude<E, Panic>: .recover is
	// called on the Ok|Err union, whose combined signature intersects the two
	// parameter types, and a conditional type there collapses the mapper's
	// parameter to never at every call site. The runtime guard, not the
	// parameter type, is what keeps the mapper from seeing a Panic.
	recover<B>(mapper: (error: E) => B): SmithersOk<B> | SmithersErr<Extract<E, Panic>> {
		if (this.error instanceof Panic) return this as unknown as SmithersErr<Extract<E, Panic>>;
		return new SmithersOk(mapper(this.error));
	}
	// tap fires only on success and tapError only on failure; neither replaces
	// the Result. Unlike recover, tapError observes a Panic as well: observing
	// is not handling, so passing the distinguished channel to an observer does
	// not convert it into a success.
	tap(observer: (value: never) => void): SmithersErr<E> { return this; }
	tapError(observer: (error: E) => void): SmithersErr<E> { observer(this.error); return this; }
	// tapBoth on the failure variant runs the error handler and only the error
	// handler. Like tapError it observes a Panic as well: observing is not
	// handling. The handler object is validated before either branch is chosen,
	// so a malformed object is a panic on BOTH variants rather than only on the
	// one whose handler is missing.
	tapBoth(handlers: { ok: (value: never) => unknown; error: (error: E) => unknown }): SmithersErr<E> {
		if (typeof handlers?.ok !== "function" || typeof handlers.error !== "function") panic("Result.tapBoth requires ok and error handlers");
		handlers.error(this.error);
		return this;
	}
	// The same tag as SmithersOk, for the reason stated there.
	get [Symbol.toStringTag](): string { return "Result"; }
}
type SmithersAnyResult = SmithersOk<any> | SmithersErr<any>;
type SmithersOkValue<R> = R extends SmithersOk<infer A> ? A : never;
type SmithersErrError<R> = R extends SmithersErr<infer E> ? E : never;
// smithersResultAll collects every success and STOPS at the first error. The
// left-to-right scan is the contract, not an implementation detail: with two
// failing entries the collected Result must carry the first, so a right-to-left
// fold would be wrong.
export function smithersResultAll<const R extends readonly SmithersAnyResult[]>(
	results: R,
): SmithersOk<SmithersOkValue<R[number]>[]> | SmithersErr<SmithersErrError<R[number]>>;
export function smithersResultAll(results: readonly SmithersAnyResult[]): SmithersAnyResult {
	const values: unknown[] = [];
	for (const result of results) {
		if (!result.ok) return result;
		values.push(result.value);
	}
	return new SmithersOk(values);
}
type SmithersErrorConstructor<E extends Error = Error> = abstract new (...args: never[]) => E;
// The native instanceof, taken from Function.prototype so a class that installs
// its own Symbol.hasInstance cannot decide whether OTHER classes' instances are
// its own. specification/failures.mdx, "Error Prototype": "Handler selection
// MUST use compiler-stable nominal identity, not a forgeable user '_tag' or
// minifier-sensitive constructor name in compiled artifacts." A user-installed
// Symbol.hasInstance is exactly such a forgeable hook: with a bare instanceof,
// 'genuine.is(Liar)' and 'genuine.matches(Liar)' answer whatever Liar says.
function smithersNativeInstanceOf(value: unknown, type: Function): boolean {
	try {
		return Boolean(Function.prototype[Symbol.hasInstance].call(type, value));
	} catch {
		return false;
	}
}
/**
 * The compiler's one handler-selection predicate.
 *
 * specification/failures.mdx, "Error Prototype": "Handler selection MUST use
 * compiler-stable nominal identity, not a forgeable user '_tag' or
 * minifier-sensitive constructor name in compiled artifacts." Every selection
 * the compiler emits — 'Error.prototype.is'/'.matches', the lowered
 * 'Error.match'/'.matchPartial' chain, and the declared '@throws {T}' channel a
 * foreign call is admitted into — routes through here, so a class that installs
 * its own Symbol.hasInstance cannot decide whether OTHER classes' instances are
 * its own at any one of them.
 *
 * It is exported because the lowering emits calls to it; nothing an author can
 * name reaches it, and it constructs nothing.
 */
export function smithersErrorIs<E extends Error>(error: unknown, type: SmithersErrorConstructor<E>): error is E {
	if (!smithersNativeInstanceOf(error, Error) || typeof type !== "function") return false;
	return smithersNativeInstanceOf(error, type as unknown as Function);
}
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The declaration-level nominal brand.
//
// Two 'class X extends Error {}' declarations with the same fields are the same
// STRUCTURAL type, so a type predicate that narrows by assignability subtracts
// both of them in its else branch and the surviving sibling collapses to
// 'never'. 'instanceof' narrowing is weaker — it filters by the declared base
// chain — which is why the lowered match chain could use it while
// smithersErrorIs could not. Merging
// 'interface X extends SmithersNominalError<"<identity>">' beside each authored
// Error class makes siblings nominally distinct, so the predicate narrows both
// branches correctly and handler selection can run on the unforgeable one.
//
// The brand is a phantom TYPE-ONLY member: it is never read, written,
// enumerated, or encoded, so runtime object shapes, the derived codec, and the
// emitted JavaScript are all untouched. The brand identity is the same string
// smithersRegisterError receives, so the nominal key and the wire key cannot
// drift apart.
//
// Exactly one level of an inheritance chain may carry a brand: TypeScript
// requires a merged interface's inherited members to be IDENTICAL to the base
// class's, so a differently branded subclass is TS2320. The compiler therefore
// brands the top of each chain and leaves a subclass to inherit it, which is
// also what the reference frontend does.
declare const smithersNominalErrorBrand: unique symbol;
export interface SmithersNominalError<Identity extends string> {
	readonly [smithersNominalErrorBrand]: { readonly [Key in Identity]: void };
}
function smithersErrorMatches(error: unknown, ...types: readonly SmithersErrorConstructor[]): boolean {
	return types.some((type) => smithersErrorIs(error, type));
}
function smithersRootCause(error: Error): unknown {
	let current: unknown = error;
	const seen = new Set<object>();
	while (typeof current === "object" && current !== null && !seen.has(current)) {
		seen.add(current);
		const descriptor = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(current, "cause");
		if (!descriptor || !("value" in descriptor) || descriptor.value === undefined) return current;
		current = descriptor.value;
	}
	return current;
}
for (const [name, method] of Object.entries({
	is(this: Error, type: SmithersErrorConstructor): boolean { return smithersErrorIs(this, type); },
	matches(this: Error, ...types: readonly SmithersErrorConstructor[]): boolean { return smithersErrorMatches(this, ...types); },
	rootCause(this: Error): unknown { return smithersRootCause(this); },
})) {
	if (Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(Error.prototype, name) === undefined) {
		Object.defineProperty(Error.prototype, name, {
			value: method,
			enumerable: false,
			configurable: false,
			writable: false,
		});
	}
}
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Nominal Error identity and cross-realm transport.
//
// specification/failures.mdx, "Error Classes": "Authors MUST NOT need a
// 'TaggedError("Name")' factory, '_tag' declaration, or separate
// error-declaration syntax. The compiler MUST provide stable nominal identity,
// matching metadata, serialization evidence, and cross-realm transport metadata
// while preserving ordinary 'Error' behavior." Three of those four were already
// provided by constructor-keyed dispatch; identity, serialization evidence, and
// transport metadata are supplied here.
//
// specification/failures.mdx, "Error Prototype": "Handler selection MUST use
// compiler-stable nominal identity, not a forgeable user '_tag' or
// minifier-sensitive constructor name in compiled artifacts." The identity is
// therefore minted by the compiler from the module path and the class name and
// is never written by an author; there is no author-facing spelling for it.
//
// specification/durable-execution.mdx, "Durable Boundary": "Plain data SHOULD
// derive the contract automatically. Functions, capabilities, process handles,
// and other ephemeral values MUST be rejected unless they define an explicit
// durable representation." The codec is therefore DERIVED at registration and
// every non-data field is refused by smithersAssertJson with its exact path,
// which is the "MUST be rejected" half delivered as a diagnostic rather than a
// silent drop.
//
// specification/durable-execution.mdx, "Security": "Messages crossing
// coordinator and worker boundaries MUST be validated against derived codecs."
// decode therefore re-derives the canonical wire form and refuses anything that
// does not reproduce it byte for byte, refuses an unknown identity, and refuses
// a codec that returns the wrong prototype.
type SmithersJsonValue = null | boolean | number | string | SmithersJsonValue[] | { [key: string]: SmithersJsonValue };
export class ErrorCodecError extends Error {
	constructor(message: string, options?: { readonly cause?: unknown }) {
		super(message, options);
		this.name = "ErrorCodecError";
	}
}
type SmithersErrorPayloadCodec = {
	readonly encode: (error: Error) => SmithersJsonValue;
	readonly decode: (payload: SmithersJsonValue) => Error;
};
type SmithersErrorRegistration = {
	readonly id: string;
	readonly type: SmithersErrorConstructor;
	codec?: SmithersErrorPayloadCodec;
	// True when 'codec' was derived by the compiler hook rather than supplied
	// with the class. A derived codec is a default an explicit one replaces;
	// two explicit codecs still collide.
	derived?: boolean;
};
const smithersErrorByType = new WeakMap<Function, SmithersErrorRegistration>();
const smithersErrorByPrototype = new WeakMap<object, SmithersErrorRegistration>();
const smithersErrorById = new Map<string, SmithersErrorRegistration>();
// A stable Error identity: an ECMAScript identifier alphabet plus the module
// path punctuation the compiler mints identities from, bounded at 256 UTF-16
// units. The letter classes are Unicode because failures.mdx requires ANY named
// class extending Error to be usable as a nominal error, and 'class Café extends
// Error {}' is one.
const SMITHERS_ERROR_IDENTITY = /^[\p{ID_Start}0-9$][\p{ID_Continue}$._/@:+-]{0,255}$/u;
const SMITHERS_MAX_WIRE_BYTES = 1048576;
const SMITHERS_MAX_JSON_DEPTH = 64;
function smithersIsLocalError(value: unknown): value is Error {
	return smithersNativeInstanceOf(value, Error);
}
function smithersIsErrorConstructor(type: unknown): boolean {
	if (typeof type !== "function") return false;
	const prototype: unknown = (type as { prototype?: unknown }).prototype;
	if (typeof prototype !== "object" || prototype === null) return false;
	return (type as unknown) === Error || smithersNativeInstanceOf(prototype, Error);
}
function smithersValidateIdentity(id: string): void {
	if (!SMITHERS_ERROR_IDENTITY.test(id)) {
		throw new TypeError("invalid stable Error identity: " + JSON.stringify(id));
	}
}
function smithersRegisterErrorType(type: SmithersErrorConstructor, id: string): SmithersErrorRegistration {
	if (!smithersIsErrorConstructor(type)) {
		throw new TypeError("Error identity requires a class extending Error");
	}
	smithersValidateIdentity(id);
	const priorType = smithersErrorByType.get(type as unknown as Function);
	const priorId = smithersErrorById.get(id);
	if (priorType !== undefined && priorType.id !== id) {
		throw new TypeError("Error constructor is already registered as " + priorType.id);
	}
	if (priorId !== undefined && priorId.type !== type) {
		throw new TypeError("stable Error identity " + id + " is already registered");
	}
	if (priorType !== undefined) return priorType;
	const registration: SmithersErrorRegistration = { id, type };
	smithersErrorByType.set(type as unknown as Function, registration);
	smithersErrorByPrototype.set(type.prototype as object, registration);
	smithersErrorById.set(id, registration);
	return registration;
}
// The compiler-derived transport payload: 'message' plus every own enumerable
// data property. That set is exactly what ordinary class syntax produces — a
// 'readonly path: string' constructor parameter, a field initializer, and a
// 'this.name = "X"' assignment. 'message' is added by name because JavaScript
// defines it non-enumerably; 'stack' is excluded by name because it is host
// text, not data; 'cause' supplied through the Error options bag is
// non-enumerable and so is excluded for free.
//
// Nothing is validated here: smithersEncodeError runs smithersAssertJson over
// the result, so a field holding a function, a capability, a handle, a cycle,
// or a symbol key is refused with its exact path.
function smithersDerivedPayload(error: Error): SmithersJsonValue {
	const payload: { [key: string]: SmithersJsonValue } = { message: error.message };
	for (const key of Object.keys(error)) {
		if (key === "message" || key === "stack") continue;
		const descriptor = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(error, key);
		if (descriptor === undefined || !("value" in descriptor) || !descriptor.enumerable) continue;
		payload[key] = descriptor.value as SmithersJsonValue;
	}
	return payload;
}
// Rebuild without running the constructor. A constructor's parameter list is
// not part of the wire contract — an author may spell '(path, reason)' or take
// an options object — so calling it would make transport depend on a signature
// the payload does not carry. Object.create keeps instanceof, the prototype
// identity decode re-checks, and every inherited method.
function smithersDerivedInstance(type: SmithersErrorConstructor, payload: SmithersJsonValue): Error {
	const record = smithersParseRecord(payload, "$.payload");
	if (typeof record.message !== "string") throw new ErrorCodecError("$.payload.message must be a string");
	const decoded = Object.create(type.prototype as object) as Error;
	Object.defineProperty(decoded, "message", { value: record.message, writable: true, enumerable: false, configurable: true });
	for (const key of Object.keys(record)) {
		if (key === "message") continue;
		Object.defineProperty(decoded, key, { value: record[key], writable: true, enumerable: true, configurable: true });
	}
	// A decoded error crossed a realm boundary, so it has no local call site to
	// report. 'stack' stays a string, which is what ordinary Error behavior
	// gives a reader, rather than becoming undefined only for decoded values.
	Object.defineProperty(decoded, "stack", { value: decoded.name + ": " + record.message, writable: true, enumerable: false, configurable: true });
	return decoded;
}
function smithersRegisterErrorCodec(type: SmithersErrorConstructor, id: string, codec: SmithersErrorPayloadCodec): void {
	const registration = smithersRegisterErrorType(type, id);
	if (registration.codec !== undefined && registration.derived !== true) {
		throw new TypeError("Error codec for " + id + " is already registered");
	}
	registration.codec = Object.freeze({ encode: codec.encode, decode: codec.decode });
	registration.derived = false;
}
/**
 * The trusted compiler hook emitted once after each named '.sm' Error class.
 * It supplies the stable nominal identity and, because failures.mdx also
 * requires serialization evidence and cross-realm transport metadata, the
 * derived codec durable-execution.mdx asks the compiler to derive.
 */
export function smithersRegisterError<E extends Error>(
	type: abstract new (...args: never[]) => E,
	id: string,
): abstract new (...args: never[]) => E {
	const registration = smithersRegisterErrorType(type, id);
	if (registration.codec === undefined) {
		registration.codec = Object.freeze({
			encode: (error: Error): SmithersJsonValue => smithersDerivedPayload(error),
			decode: (payload: SmithersJsonValue): Error => smithersDerivedInstance(registration.type, payload),
		});
		registration.derived = true;
	}
	return type;
}
function smithersRegistrationForError(error: Error): SmithersErrorRegistration | undefined {
	try {
		const prototype = Object.getPrototypeOf(error) as object | null;
		return prototype === null ? undefined : smithersErrorByPrototype.get(prototype);
	} catch {
		return undefined;
	}
}
/** The stable nominal identity of a local Error, or undefined for a value that has none. */
export function smithersErrorIdentity(error: unknown): string | undefined {
	if (!smithersIsLocalError(error)) return undefined;
	const registration = smithersRegistrationForError(error);
	return registration === undefined ? undefined : registration.id;
}
function smithersAssertJson(value: unknown, path: string, depth: number, seen: Set<object>): void {
	if (depth > SMITHERS_MAX_JSON_DEPTH) throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " exceeds the codec depth limit");
	if (value === null || typeof value === "boolean" || typeof value === "string") return;
	if (typeof value === "number") {
		if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " contains a non-finite number");
		return;
	}
	if (typeof value !== "object") throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " is not JSON data");
	if (seen.has(value)) throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " contains a cycle");
	seen.add(value);
	try {
		if (Array.isArray(value)) {
			for (let index = 0; index < value.length; index++) {
				const descriptor = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(value, String(index));
				if (descriptor === undefined) throw new ErrorCodecError(path + "[" + index + "] is a sparse array hole");
				if (!("value" in descriptor) || !descriptor.enumerable) {
					throw new ErrorCodecError(path + "[" + index + "] is not an enumerable data property");
				}
				smithersAssertJson(descriptor.value, path + "[" + index + "]", depth + 1, seen);
			}
			const extras = Reflect.ownKeys(value).filter((key) => key !== "length" && !(typeof key === "string" && /^(0|[1-9]\d*)$/.test(key)));
			if (extras.length > 0) throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " has non-JSON array properties");
			return;
		}
		const prototype = Object.getPrototypeOf(value) as object | null;
		if (prototype !== Object.prototype && prototype !== null) {
			throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " is not a plain JSON object");
		}
		for (const key of Reflect.ownKeys(value)) {
			if (typeof key !== "string") throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " has a symbol key");
			const descriptor = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(value, key);
			if (descriptor === undefined || !("value" in descriptor) || !descriptor.enumerable) {
				throw new ErrorCodecError(path + "." + key + " is not an enumerable data property");
			}
			smithersAssertJson(descriptor.value, path + "." + key, depth + 1, seen);
		}
	} finally {
		seen.delete(value);
	}
}
// Canonical JSON: object keys sorted, -0 written as 0. decode re-derives this
// form from what it parsed and refuses a wire that does not reproduce itself,
// so one error has exactly one encoding and a re-encoded message is comparable.
function smithersStringifyJson(value: SmithersJsonValue): string {
	if (value === null || typeof value === "boolean" || typeof value === "string") return JSON.stringify(value);
	if (typeof value === "number") return Object.is(value, -0) ? "0" : JSON.stringify(value);
	if (Array.isArray(value)) {
		const items: string[] = [];
		for (let index = 0; index < value.length; index++) {
			const descriptor = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(value, String(index));
			if (descriptor === undefined || !("value" in descriptor)) {
				throw new ErrorCodecError("$.payload[" + index + "] changed during encoding");
			}
			items.push(smithersStringifyJson(descriptor.value as SmithersJsonValue));
		}
		return "[" + items.join(",") + "]";
	}
	const record = value as { [key: string]: SmithersJsonValue };
	return "{" + Object.keys(record).sort().map((key) => {
		const descriptor = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(record, key);
		if (descriptor === undefined || !("value" in descriptor)) {
			throw new ErrorCodecError("$.payload." + key + " changed during encoding");
		}
		return JSON.stringify(key) + ":" + smithersStringifyJson(descriptor.value as SmithersJsonValue);
	}).join(",") + "}";
}
function smithersParseRecord(value: SmithersJsonValue, path: string): { [key: string]: SmithersJsonValue } {
	if (value === null || Array.isArray(value) || typeof value !== "object") {
		throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " must be an object");
	}
	return value;
}
function smithersRequireExactKeys(value: { [key: string]: SmithersJsonValue }, expected: readonly string[], path: string): void {
	const actual = Object.keys(value).sort();
	const wanted = [...expected].sort();
	if (actual.length !== wanted.length || actual.some((key, index) => key !== wanted[index])) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " has unexpected fields");
	}
}
// The wire limit is a byte budget, so it is measured in UTF-8 bytes. The count
// is computed here rather than taken from a host API because the prelude runs
// in every JavaScript environment Smithers targets and Buffer is Node's alone.
function smithersUtf8Length(text: string): number {
	let total = 0;
	for (let index = 0; index < text.length; index++) {
		const code = text.charCodeAt(index);
		if (code < 0x80) {
			total += 1;
		} else if (code < 0x800) {
			total += 2;
		} else if (code >= 0xD800 && code <= 0xDBFF && index + 1 < text.length &&
			text.charCodeAt(index + 1) >= 0xDC00 && text.charCodeAt(index + 1) <= 0xDFFF) {
			total += 4;
			index++;
		} else {
			total += 3;
		}
	}
	return total;
}
/**
 * A field smithersRequireExactKeys has already proved present, as a
 * SmithersJsonValue.
 *
 * noUncheckedIndexedAccess is mandatory (compatibility.mdx §Mandatory), so an
 * index read into a { [key: string]: SmithersJsonValue } is
 * SmithersJsonValue | undefined and the exact-keys check above is a narrowing
 * the checker cannot see. §Mandatory's own guidance is that such a read "MUST be
 * narrowed, or read through an extraction helper"; this is the helper, and it is
 * the same one requiredField is in poc/src/runtime/errors.ts.
 */
function smithersRequiredField(record: { [key: string]: SmithersJsonValue }, key: string, path: string): SmithersJsonValue {
	if (!Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(record, key)) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError(path + " is missing the '" + key + "' field");
	}
	return record[key] as SmithersJsonValue;
}
/** Encode a local Error into the canonical cross-realm wire form. */
export function smithersEncodeError(error: Error): string {
	if (!smithersIsLocalError(error)) throw new ErrorCodecError("only local Error instances can be encoded");
	const registration = smithersRegistrationForError(error);
	if (registration === undefined || registration.codec === undefined) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError("Error has no registered transport codec");
	}
	let payload: SmithersJsonValue;
	try {
		payload = registration.codec.encode(error);
	} catch (cause) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError("Error codec " + registration.id + " failed to encode", { cause });
	}
	try {
		smithersAssertJson(payload, "$.payload", 0, new Set<object>());
	} catch (cause) {
		if (cause instanceof ErrorCodecError) throw cause;
		throw new ErrorCodecError("Error codec " + registration.id + " returned unsafe data", { cause });
	}
	const wire = "{\"version\":1,\"identity\":" + JSON.stringify(registration.id) + ",\"payload\":" + smithersStringifyJson(payload) + "}";
	if (smithersUtf8Length(wire) > SMITHERS_MAX_WIRE_BYTES) throw new ErrorCodecError("encoded Error exceeds the wire limit");
	return wire;
}
/** Decode a wire form produced by smithersEncodeError back into a local Error. */
export function smithersDecodeError(wire: string): Error {
	if (typeof wire !== "string") throw new ErrorCodecError("encoded Error must be a string");
	if (smithersUtf8Length(wire) > SMITHERS_MAX_WIRE_BYTES) throw new ErrorCodecError("encoded Error exceeds the wire limit");
	let parsed: unknown;
	try {
		parsed = JSON.parse(wire);
	} catch (cause) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError("encoded Error is not valid JSON", { cause });
	}
	smithersAssertJson(parsed, "$", 0, new Set<object>());
	const envelope = smithersParseRecord(parsed as SmithersJsonValue, "$");
	smithersRequireExactKeys(envelope, ["version", "identity", "payload"], "$");
	if (envelope.version !== 1 || typeof envelope.identity !== "string") {
		throw new ErrorCodecError("encoded Error has an unsupported envelope");
	}
	const canonical = "{\"version\":1,\"identity\":" + JSON.stringify(envelope.identity) + ",\"payload\":" + smithersStringifyJson(smithersRequiredField(envelope, "payload", "$")) + "}";
	if (wire !== canonical) throw new ErrorCodecError("encoded Error is not canonical JSON");
	const registration = smithersErrorById.get(envelope.identity);
	if (registration === undefined || registration.codec === undefined) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError("unknown Error identity " + envelope.identity);
	}
	let decoded: Error;
	try {
		decoded = registration.codec.decode(smithersRequiredField(envelope, "payload", "$"));
	} catch (cause) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError("Error codec " + registration.id + " failed to decode", { cause });
	}
	if (!smithersIsLocalError(decoded) ||
		!smithersNativeInstanceOf(decoded, registration.type as unknown as Function) ||
		Object.getPrototypeOf(decoded) !== registration.type.prototype) {
		throw new ErrorCodecError("Error codec " + registration.id + " returned the wrong Error type");
	}
	return decoded;
}
function smithersMessagePayload(error: Error): SmithersJsonValue {
	return { message: error.message };
}
function smithersDecodeMessage(payload: SmithersJsonValue): string {
	const record = smithersParseRecord(payload, "$.payload");
	smithersRequireExactKeys(record, ["message"], "$.payload");
	if (typeof record.message !== "string") throw new ErrorCodecError("$.payload.message must be a string");
	return record.message;
}
for (const [type, id] of [
	[Error, "javascript:Error@1"],
	[EvalError, "javascript:EvalError@1"],
	[RangeError, "javascript:RangeError@1"],
	[ReferenceError, "javascript:ReferenceError@1"],
	[SyntaxError, "javascript:SyntaxError@1"],
	[TypeError, "javascript:TypeError@1"],
	[URIError, "javascript:URIError@1"],
] as ReadonlyArray<readonly [new (message?: string) => Error, string]>) {
	smithersRegisterErrorCodec(type, id, {
		encode: smithersMessagePayload,
		decode: (payload: SmithersJsonValue): Error => new type(smithersDecodeMessage(payload)),
	});
}
smithersRegisterErrorCodec(Panic, "smithers:Panic@1", {
	encode: smithersMessagePayload,
	decode: (payload: SmithersJsonValue): Error => new Panic(smithersDecodeMessage(payload)),
});
smithersRegisterErrorCodec(ErrorCodecError, "smithers:ErrorCodecError@1", {
	encode: smithersMessagePayload,
	decode: (payload: SmithersJsonValue): Error => new ErrorCodecError(smithersDecodeMessage(payload)),
});
export function smithersMatchFailed(error: never): never {
    throw new TypeError("non-exhaustive Error match");
}
export function smithersTry<A>(body: () => A): SmithersOk<A> | SmithersErr<Panic>;
export function smithersTry<A, E extends Error>(body: () => A, expected: abstract new (...args: never[]) => E): SmithersOk<A> | SmithersErr<E | Panic>;
export function smithersTry<A, E extends Error>(body: () => A, expected?: abstract new (...args: never[]) => E): SmithersOk<A> | SmithersErr<E | Panic> {
	// The admission test is smithersErrorIs, not a bare instanceof, for the same
	// reason handler selection is: 'expected' is the class an author named in a
	// '@throws {T}' contract or handed to Result.try, and a class that installs
	// Symbol.hasInstance would otherwise admit ANY thrown value into the declared
	// recoverable channel — a Result failure that never came from that class.
	try { return new SmithersOk(body()); }
	catch (cause) { return new SmithersErr(expected !== undefined && smithersErrorIs(cause, expected) ? cause : new Panic(cause)); }
}
export async function smithersTryPromise<A>(body: () => PromiseLike<A>): Promise<SmithersOk<A> | SmithersErr<Panic>>;
export async function smithersTryPromise<A, E extends Error>(body: () => PromiseLike<A>, expected: abstract new (...args: never[]) => E): Promise<SmithersOk<A> | SmithersErr<E | Panic>>;
export async function smithersTryPromise<A, E extends Error>(body: () => PromiseLike<A>, expected?: abstract new (...args: never[]) => E): Promise<SmithersOk<A> | SmithersErr<E | Panic>> {
	try { return new SmithersOk(await body()); }
	catch (cause) { return new SmithersErr(expected !== undefined && smithersErrorIs(cause, expected) ? cause : new Panic(cause)); }
}
declare global {
    type Result<A, E> = SmithersOk<A> | SmithersErr<E>;
    // The author-facing Result namespace carries only the documented
    // boundary adapters. Result.ok/err are deliberately absent: ordinary
    // return and throw lifting construct the variants.
    const Result: {
        try: typeof smithersTry;
        tryPromise: typeof smithersTryPromise;
        all: typeof smithersResultAll;
    };
    // Reflect.panic is the ambient spelling of the same distinguished channel
    // that panic(...) from smithers:exceptions charges. It is declared, never
    // emitted: every reachable use is rewritten into the error variant, and a
    // use in any other position is rejected, so the runtime Reflect object is
    // never asked for a member it does not have.
    namespace Reflect {
        function panic(cause?: unknown): never;
    }
    interface Error {
        is<E extends Error>(type: SmithersErrorConstructor<E>): this is E;
        matches<const Types extends readonly SmithersErrorConstructor[]>(...types: Types): boolean;
        match<R>(handlers: Record<string, (error: never) => R>): R;
        matchPartial<R, F>(handlers: Record<string, (error: never) => R>, fallback: (error: Error) => F): R | F;
        rootCause(): unknown;
    }
}
`

var compilerModuleSpecifiers = []string{
	"smthrs/context",
	"smthrs/provider",
	"smithers:exceptions",
	"smithers:comptime",
	"smithers:flows",
}

func configureInternalModules(options *core.CompilerOptions) {
	paths := collections.NewOrderedMapWithSizeHint[string, []string](len(compilerModuleSpecifiers))
	for _, specifier := range compilerModuleSpecifiers {
		if durableModuleSpecifiers[specifier] {
			// Durable declarations have a distinct module identity: resolving an
			// export from another compiler-owned module must not make it durable.
			paths.Set(specifier, []string{durableModuleAlias})
			continue
		}
		if specifier == comptimeModuleSpecifier {
			// The comptime intrinsic has its own compiler-owned module, so its
			// exported bindings cannot be reached through any other specifier.
			paths.Set(specifier, []string{comptimeModuleAlias})
			continue
		}
		paths.Set(specifier, []string{"./" + preludeLogicalPath})
	}
	options.Paths = paths
	options.PathsBasePath = "/src"
}

// preludeSymbols holds the declaration nodes of the compiler-owned prelude.
// Recognition is by node identity against these declarations, never by name
// spelling at the use site: a user-declared `Result` resolves to a different
// symbol and is left alone.
type preludeSymbols struct {
	checker *checker.Checker
	module  *ast.Symbol
	error   *ast.Symbol
	panic   *ast.Symbol
	context *ast.Symbol

	resultAlias  *ast.Node // the `Result` TypeAliasDeclaration in the prelude
	resultSymbol *ast.Symbol

	okClass  *ast.Node // the `SmithersOk` ClassDeclaration
	errClass *ast.Node // the `SmithersErr` ClassDeclaration

	resultUnwrap      map[*ast.Node]struct{} // retired `unwrap` members on the Result variants
	matchDecls        map[*ast.Node]struct{} // the global `Error.match` MethodSignature
	matchPartialDecls map[*ast.Node]struct{} // the global `Error.matchPartial` MethodSignature
	errorRuntimeDecls map[*ast.Node]struct{} // global Error helpers implemented by prelude side effects
	expectDecls       map[*ast.Node]struct{} // `expect` on the Result variants
	// resultConsumerDecls contains every receiver method that discharges a
	// Result must-consume obligation. The use-site member is recognized by its
	// prelude declaration identity, never by its spelling.
	resultConsumerDecls map[*ast.Node]struct{}
	// resultInspectionDecls is the compiler-owned discriminant and payload
	// properties of the two Result variants: `ok`, and the `value`/`error` the
	// readonly constructor parameters become. It is a PRELUDE SHAPE assertion —
	// the completeness check below requires exactly four — and nothing else
	// reads it.
	//
	// It used to feed a `resultInspection` predicate that let an authored read
	// of one of these DISCHARGE a must-consume obligation, which was a
	// fail-open: `const r = f(); if (r.ok) { … }` compiled and ran here while
	// the reference refused it with SMITHERS1302. Removed 2026-08-28. See the
	// arm it fed in mustconsume.go.txt for the specification sentence, and
	// conformance/corpus/07-must-consume/reading-a-results-runtime-tag-does-not-consume-it
	// for the case that pins it. Do not reconnect it: whether the language
	// gains an error-extraction form at all is an Open decision
	// (specification/failures.mdx, "Authorability").
	resultInspectionDecls map[*ast.Node]struct{}
	contextDecls          map[*ast.Node]struct{} // the compiler-owned `Context.context`
	panicDecls            map[*ast.Node]struct{} // the compiler-owned `panic(...)` function
	// reflectPanicDecls is the ambient `Reflect.panic` declaration. It is a
	// separate set from panicDecls only so the prelude completeness check can
	// count each spelling once; both reach the same channel.
	reflectPanicDecls map[*ast.Node]struct{}
	layerDecls        map[string]map[*ast.Node]struct{}
	// resultNamespaceDecls are the members of the global `Result` value:
	// `try`/`tryPromise`, the two documented adapters that turn a throwing (or
	// rejecting) body into a Result whose failure channel retains the
	// distinguished Panic case, and `all`, the collecting combinator. None of
	// the three has a runtime existence under the `Result` spelling, so the
	// lowering replaces the callee with the prelude export that implements it.
	resultNamespaceDecls map[string]map[*ast.Node]struct{}
	// promiseCombinatorDecls are the ambient Promise constructor's own static
	// collection methods. Capturing their declaration identity here prevents a
	// same-spelled authored object from acquiring structured-concurrency trust.
	promiseCombinatorDecls map[string]map[*ast.Node]struct{}
}

// loadPreludeSymbols locates the prelude's declarations in the checked Program
// and resolves their symbols through the fork's checker.
func loadPreludeSymbols(program *compiler.Program, typeChecker *checker.Checker) (*preludeSymbols, error) {
	var preludeFile *ast.SourceFile
	for _, file := range program.SourceFiles() {
		if file.FileName() == preludeVirtualName {
			preludeFile = file
			break
		}
	}
	if preludeFile == nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("the compiler-owned prelude %q is missing from the program", preludeLogicalPath)
	}

	symbols := &preludeSymbols{
		checker:               typeChecker,
		resultUnwrap:          make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		matchDecls:            make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		matchPartialDecls:     make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		errorRuntimeDecls:     make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		expectDecls:           make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		resultConsumerDecls:   make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		resultInspectionDecls: make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		contextDecls:          make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		panicDecls:            make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		reflectPanicDecls:     make(map[*ast.Node]struct{}),
		layerDecls: map[string]map[*ast.Node]struct{}{
			"succeed": {},
			"merge":   {},
			"provide": {},
		},
		resultNamespaceDecls: map[string]map[*ast.Node]struct{}{
			"try":        {},
			"tryPromise": {},
			"all":        {},
		},
		promiseCombinatorDecls: map[string]map[*ast.Node]struct{}{
			"all": {}, "allSettled": {}, "race": {}, "any": {},
			"allKeyed": {}, "allSettledKeyed": {},
		},
	}
	var errorInterface *ast.Node
	var panicClass *ast.Node
	var contextClass *ast.Node
	var walk func(node *ast.Node) bool
	walk = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
		switch {
		case ast.IsTypeAliasDeclaration(node):
			if declarationName(node) == "Result" {
				symbols.resultAlias = node
			}
		case ast.IsClassDeclaration(node):
			switch declarationName(node) {
			case "SmithersOk":
				symbols.okClass = node
				collectResultInspectionMembers(node, symbols.resultInspectionDecls)
				for _, name := range resultConsumerMembers {
					collectMembers(node, ast.IsMethodDeclaration, name, symbols.resultConsumerDecls)
				}
				collectMembers(node, ast.IsMethodDeclaration, "unwrap", symbols.resultUnwrap)
				collectMembers(node, ast.IsMethodDeclaration, "expect", symbols.expectDecls)
			case "SmithersErr":
				symbols.errClass = node
				collectResultInspectionMembers(node, symbols.resultInspectionDecls)
				for _, name := range resultConsumerMembers {
					collectMembers(node, ast.IsMethodDeclaration, name, symbols.resultConsumerDecls)
				}
				collectMembers(node, ast.IsMethodDeclaration, "unwrap", symbols.resultUnwrap)
				collectMembers(node, ast.IsMethodDeclaration, "expect", symbols.expectDecls)
			case "Panic":
				panicClass = node
			case "Context":
				contextClass = node
				collectMembers(node, ast.IsMethodDeclaration, "context", symbols.contextDecls)
			}
			return false
		case ast.IsModuleDeclaration(node) && declarationName(node) == "Reflect":
			// The ambient `Reflect.panic` lives in its own namespace inside the
			// `declare global` block. Collecting it here rather than letting the
			// FunctionDeclaration case below claim it keeps the two authored
			// spellings — the imported `panic` and the ambient `Reflect.panic` —
			// countable one by one in the completeness check.
			collectMembers(moduleBodyOf(node), ast.IsFunctionDeclaration, "panic", symbols.reflectPanicDecls)
			return false
		case ast.IsFunctionDeclaration(node):
			if declarationName(node) == "panic" {
				symbols.panicDecls[node] = struct{}{}
			}
			return false
		case ast.IsInterfaceDeclaration(node):
			if declarationName(node) == "Error" {
				errorInterface = node
				collectMembers(node, isMethodSignature, "match", symbols.matchDecls)
				collectMembers(node, isMethodSignature, "matchPartial", symbols.matchPartialDecls)
				for _, name := range []string{"is", "matches", "rootCause"} {
					collectMembers(node, isMethodSignature, name, symbols.errorRuntimeDecls)
				}
			}
			return false
		case ast.IsVariableDeclaration(node):
			if declarationName(node) == "Result" && node.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer == nil {
				typeNode := node.AsVariableDeclaration().Type
				if typeNode != nil && typeNode.Kind == ast.KindTypeLiteral {
					for _, member := range typeNode.AsTypeLiteralNode().Members.Nodes {
						if declarations, ok := symbols.resultNamespaceDecls[declarationName(member)]; ok {
							declarations[member] = struct{}{}
						}
					}
				}
				return false
			}
			if declarationName(node) == "Layer" {
				initializer := node.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer
				if initializer != nil && ast.IsObjectLiteralExpression(initializer) {
					for _, member := range initializer.AsObjectLiteralExpression().Properties.Nodes {
						if declarations, ok := symbols.layerDecls[declarationName(member)]; ok {
							declarations[member] = struct{}{}
						}
					}
				}
				return false
			}
		}
		node.ForEachChild(walk)
		return false
	}
	preludeFile.AsNode().ForEachChild(walk)

	// Resolve Promise at a compiler-owned location where authored shadows are
	// out of scope, then retain the ambient constructor members by declaration
	// identity. The extended keyed names are optional library additions; the
	// standard `all` member must always be present in the embedded ES2022 libs.
	promiseSymbol := typeChecker.ResolveName("Promise", preludeFile.AsNode(), ast.SymbolFlagsValue, false)
	if promiseSymbol != nil {
		promiseType := typeChecker.GetTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(promiseSymbol, preludeFile.AsNode())
		for name, declarations := range symbols.promiseCombinatorDecls {
			property := typeChecker.GetPropertyOfType(promiseType, name)
			if property == nil {
				continue
			}
			for _, declaration := range property.Declarations {
				declarations[declaration] = struct{}{}
			}
		}
	}

	if symbols.resultAlias == nil || symbols.okClass == nil || symbols.errClass == nil ||
		len(symbols.resultUnwrap) != 2 ||
		len(symbols.matchDecls) != 1 || len(symbols.matchPartialDecls) != 1 || len(symbols.errorRuntimeDecls) != 3 ||
		len(symbols.expectDecls) != 2 || len(symbols.resultConsumerDecls) != 2*len(resultConsumerMembers) ||
		len(symbols.resultInspectionDecls) != 4 ||
		panicClass == nil || contextClass == nil || len(symbols.contextDecls) != 1 ||
		len(symbols.panicDecls) != 1 || len(symbols.reflectPanicDecls) != 1 ||
		errorInterface == nil || len(symbols.layerDecls["succeed"]) != 1 ||
		len(symbols.layerDecls["merge"]) != 1 || len(symbols.layerDecls["provide"]) != 1 ||
		len(symbols.resultNamespaceDecls["try"]) != 1 || len(symbols.resultNamespaceDecls["tryPromise"]) != 1 ||
		len(symbols.resultNamespaceDecls["all"]) != 1 || len(symbols.promiseCombinatorDecls["all"]) == 0 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("the compiler-owned prelude is incomplete")
	}
	symbols.module = symbols.mergedSymbolAt(preludeFile.AsNode())
	symbols.resultSymbol = symbols.mergedSymbolAt(symbols.resultAlias.Name())
	symbols.error = symbols.mergedSymbolAt(errorInterface.Name())
	symbols.panic = symbols.mergedSymbolAt(panicClass.Name())
	symbols.context = symbols.mergedSymbolAt(contextClass.Name())
	if symbols.module == nil || symbols.resultSymbol == nil ||
		symbols.error == nil || symbols.panic == nil || symbols.context == nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("the compiler-owned prelude's Result declarations have no symbol")
	}
	return symbols, nil
}

func isMethodSignature(node *ast.Node) bool { return node.Kind == ast.KindMethodSignature }

// moduleBodyOf returns the block a namespace declaration encloses, so member
// collection can walk the statements directly rather than the declaration.
func moduleBodyOf(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsModuleDeclaration(node) {
		return nil
	}
	return node.AsModuleDeclaration().Body
}

// collectMembers records the direct members of a declaration that match a kind
// predicate and a member name. The name is only ever read inside the prelude,
// which is a file the compiler itself wrote.
func collectMembers(declaration *ast.Node, matches func(*ast.Node) bool, name string, into map[*ast.Node]struct{}) {
	if declaration == nil {
		return
	}
	declaration.ForEachChild(func(child *ast.Node) bool {
		if matches(child) && declarationName(child) == name {
			into[child] = struct{}{}
		}
		return false
	})
}

// collectResultInspectionMembers indexes the Result variant's direct `ok`
// field and the readonly constructor parameter that becomes its `value` or
// `error` field. Names are consulted only inside the compiler-authored prelude.
//
// The four declarations it finds are counted by the completeness check and read
// by nothing else: an authored read of one of them is NOT a consumption. See
// the field's own comment for why that changed on 2026-08-28.
func collectResultInspectionMembers(declaration *ast.Node, into map[*ast.Node]struct{}) {
	if declaration == nil {
		return
	}
	declaration.ForEachChild(func(child *ast.Node) bool {
		if ast.IsPropertyDeclaration(child) && declarationName(child) == "ok" {
			into[child] = struct{}{}
			return false
		}
		if !ast.IsConstructorDeclaration(child) {
			return false
		}
		for _, parameter := range child.Parameters() {
			name := declarationName(parameter)
			if name == "value" || name == "error" {
				into[parameter] = struct{}{}
			}
		}
		return false
	})
}

func declarationName(node *ast.Node) string {
	name := node.Name()
	if name == nil || !ast.IsIdentifier(name) {
		return ""
	}
	return name.Text()
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) mergedSymbolAt(node *ast.Node) *ast.Symbol {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	symbol := s.checker.GetSymbolAtLocation(node)
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return s.checker.GetMergedSymbol(symbol)
}

// declaresAnyOf reports whether a resolved symbol was declared by one of the
// given prelude declaration nodes. Node identity is the strongest available
// form of "this is the compiler's own type", and it survives merging, aliasing
// and re-export.
func declaresAnyOf(symbol *ast.Symbol, declarations ...*ast.Node) bool {
	if symbol == nil {
		return false
	}
	for _, declaration := range symbol.Declarations {
		for _, candidate := range declarations {
			if candidate != nil && declaration == candidate {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) isAliasSymbol(symbol *ast.Symbol, canonical *ast.Symbol, declaration *ast.Node) bool {
	if symbol == nil {
		return false
	}
	if canonical != nil && s.checker.GetMergedSymbol(symbol) == canonical {
		return true
	}
	return declaresAnyOf(symbol, declaration)
}

// isResultSymbol reports whether a resolved symbol is the prelude's `Result`.
func (s *preludeSymbols) isResultSymbol(symbol *ast.Symbol) bool {
	return s.isAliasSymbol(symbol, s.resultSymbol, s.resultAlias)
}

// memberSelection gives dotted access and computed element access one semantic
// representation. In TypeScript, x.member and x["member"] select the same
// resolved property and execute the same call. Compiler-owned members must
// therefore never depend on which of those two authored spellings was used.
type memberSelection struct {
	receiver *ast.Node
	// name is the node that SPELLS the member. A symbol resolves here for the
	// dotted spelling and for a literal key, and for nothing else; ask
	// memberSymbol for the member's symbol rather than resolving this node.
	name *ast.Node
	// text is the member name itself, resolved through the checker. It is NOT
	// name.Text(): on `Clock[KEY]` the key node spells `KEY` and selects
	// `context`.
	text string
}

// selectMember answers the member a `.name` or a computed `[key]` access
// selects.
//
// The criterion is the one
// 05-context-rows/a-non-literal-computed-capability-access-has-no-statically-known-member
// states: a key is a member selection when it "resolves to the same property
// symbol" — and the CHECKER answers that, where `ast.IsStringLiteralLike`
// answers a question about SPELLING instead. The two are not the same question,
// and the gap was measured on this backend with a runtime oracle:
// `Clock["context" satisfies string]()`, `Clock[("context") as const]()`,
// `Clock[<"context">"context"]()`, and `Clock[KEY]()` for `const KEY = "context"`
// (and for an alias of that alias) all resolve to the same property symbol, all
// checked clean, and all PANICKED with `capability 'Clock' was not provided` —
// the exact program the corpus certifies as SMITHERS2102. This walk used to
// apply `ast.SkipParentheses` to the key, so it already saw `Clock[("context")]`
// and none of the other five.
//
// A key whose type is the WIDE `string` still selects no statically known member
// and is still not a selection: `Clock[key]()` for a `string` parameter,
// `Clock["cont" + "ext"]()` and `Clock["context" as string]()` (whose assertion
// widens the literal away) each stay TS7053 over the emitted module, because a
// compiler-owned receiver has no string index signature. Asking the checker for
// a string LITERAL type draws that line in the same place TypeScript draws it,
// rather than one wrapper away from it.
func (s *preludeSymbols) selectMember(node *ast.Node) (memberSelection, bool) {
	if node == nil {
		return memberSelection{}, false
	}
	if ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(node) {
		access := node.AsPropertyAccessExpression()
		name := access.Name()
		return memberSelection{receiver: access.Expression, name: name, text: name.Text()}, true
	}
	if ast.IsElementAccessExpression(node) {
		access := node.AsElementAccessExpression()
		if key := access.ArgumentExpression; key != nil {
			if text, static := s.staticKeyName(key); static {
				return memberSelection{receiver: access.Expression, name: key, text: text}, true
			}
		}
	}
	return memberSelection{}, false
}

// staticKeyName is THE ONE answer to "which member does this key name", shared
// by selectMember and by the destructuring half (bindingMemberNames).
//
// A string LITERAL type is the criterion, because that is exactly when
// TypeScript resolves the access to one property symbol; a widening `string`
// names nothing and must stay unknown, which is what keeps `Clock[key]()` a
// TS7053 rather than a silently recognized call. The literal-node test is kept
// ahead of it so a key inside a type-erased context still answers without a
// checker round trip, and so the two can never disagree about a plain
// `["context"]`.
func (s *preludeSymbols) staticKeyName(key *ast.Node) (string, bool) {
	if key == nil {
		return "", false
	}
	if ast.IsStringLiteralLike(key) {
		return key.Text(), true
	}
	t := s.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(key)
	if t == nil || !t.IsStringLiteral() {
		return "", false
	}
	text, ok := t.AsLiteralType().Value().(string)
	return text, ok
}

// calleeSelection is the member an invocation selects, seen THROUGH grouping.
//
// Parenthesising never detaches: `(Db.context)()` is the same member access,
// the same `this` binding and the same emitted call as `Db.context()`.
// ECMAScript detaches a receiver through an assignment, a comma, or an argument
// position — never through grouping. Reading the callee raw made the
// parenthesized spelling record NO requirement row while checkContextReferences
// refused it as "detached" and told the author to invoke it directly as
// `Capability.context()` — advice the program was already following (measured on
// this backend: SMITHERS2107, row empty). The row is recordable here, so it is
// recorded, and isZeroArgumentCallee looks through the same grouping so the two
// answers cannot disagree.
//
// A cast is deliberately NOT stripped: `(Db.context as F)()` genuinely stops the
// resolved signature, and with it the row.
func (s *preludeSymbols) calleeSelection(call *ast.Node) (memberSelection, bool) {
	if call == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(call) {
		return memberSelection{}, false
	}
	callee := call.AsCallExpression().Expression
	if callee == nil {
		return memberSelection{}, false
	}
	return s.selectMember(ast.SkipParentheses(callee))
}

// memberSymbol is the property symbol a selection actually resolves to.
//
// GetSymbolAtLocation answers the member only where the member is SPELLED — the
// `.name` of a property access, or a literal element-access key. On
// `Clock[KEY]` it answers the symbol of `KEY`, and on `Clock[("context")]` it
// answers nothing at all, so a rule that resolved the key node directly went
// blind at exactly the spellings selectMember now recognizes. Asking the
// RECEIVER's type for the member closes it, and closes it by symbol identity: a
// user object with a same-spelled member still resolves to its own declaration
// and can never stand in for a compiler-owned one. The spelled symbol is only
// trusted when it actually names this member, which is what keeps `Clock[KEY]`
// from being answered with `KEY`'s own binding symbol.
func (s *preludeSymbols) memberSymbol(selection memberSelection) *ast.Symbol {
	if spelled := s.checker.GetSymbolAtLocation(selection.name); spelled != nil && spelled.Name == selection.text {
		return spelled
	}
	if selection.receiver == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return s.checker.GetPropertyOfType(s.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(selection.receiver), selection.text)
}

// authoredConstructor reports a forbidden author-facing Result
// variant hook. The owner must resolve to the compiler prelude; spelling is
// consulted only after that identity proof to select the reserved hook.
func (s *preludeSymbols) authoredConstructor(node *ast.Node) (string, bool) {
	selection, selected := s.selectMember(node)
	if !selected {
		return "", false
	}
	if !ast.IsIdentifier(selection.receiver) {
		return "", false
	}
	symbol := s.checker.GetSymbolAtLocation(selection.receiver)
	name := selection.text
	if s.isResultSymbol(symbol) && (name == "ok" || name == "err" || name == "success" || name == "error") {
		return "Result." + name, true
	}
	return "", false
}

// resultNamespaceCall recognizes a call to a member of the compiler-owned
// global `Result` value through the prelude's own declaration nodes, so an
// unrelated object with a `try` or `all` method is never mistaken for it. It
// returns the member name, which selects the prelude export the call lowers to.
func (s *preludeSymbols) resultNamespaceCall(node *ast.Node) (string, bool) {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return "", false
	}
	selection, selected := s.calleeSelection(node)
	if !selected {
		return "", false
	}
	symbol := s.memberSymbol(selection)
	for _, member := range resultNamespaceMembers {
		if symbolDeclaredBy(symbol, s.resultNamespaceDecls[member]) {
			return member, true
		}
	}
	return "", false
}

// resultBoundaryCall narrows resultNamespaceCall to the members that ADOPT the
// caller's throw scope. `Result.all` is deliberately not one of them: its
// argument is an array of already-computed Results, not a body the adapter
// invokes inside its own try/catch, so a foreign call written there must still
// be charged and wrapped.
func (s *preludeSymbols) resultBoundaryCall(node *ast.Node) (string, bool) {
	member, matched := s.resultNamespaceCall(node)
	if !matched || !resultBoundaryMembers[member] {
		return "", false
	}
	return member, true
}

// isPanicCall recognizes the compiler-owned panic intrinsic under either of its
// two authored spellings — `panic(...)` imported from `smithers:exceptions` and
// the ambient `Reflect.panic(...)` — through the prelude's own declarations.
// docs/DECISIONS.md, "Typed failures" (Locked): "Reflect.panic and
// compiler/runtime invariant failures enter the same distinguished channel."
// A user function named `panic`, and a user object with a `panic` member, both
// resolve elsewhere and stay ordinary calls.
func (s *preludeSymbols) isPanicCall(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return false
	}
	expression := ast.SkipParentheses(node.AsCallExpression().Expression)
	if selection, selected := s.selectMember(expression); selected {
		// The member's own symbol carries the declaration, and memberSymbol is
		// what resolves it: on `Reflect[KEY]` for `const KEY = "panic"` the key
		// node spells `KEY`, so resolving that node answered `KEY`'s binding and
		// the panic went unrecognized — measured, on this backend, as a
		// SMITHERS1503 the reference reports and this one did not, and as a
		// `: string` function ending in a panic that stopped type-checking here.
		// Asking the RECEIVER's type for `panic` still proves which one was
		// selected: a user object with a `panic` member has a different receiver
		// type and resolves to its own declaration.
		return symbolDeclaredBy(s.resolveAliasSymbol(s.memberSymbol(selection)), s.reflectPanicDecls)
	}
	return symbolDeclaredBy(s.resolvedSymbolAt(expression), s.panicDecls)
}

// panicTemplateTagMessage is shared with poc/src/language/semantic.ts, which
// reports the same refusal at the same node.
const panicTemplateTagMessage = "panic(...) is a call, not a template tag; a tagged `panic` receives the template parts as an array, so the authored text becomes the panic's cause and the message degrades to the generic 'Smithers panic'. Write panic(`...`) instead"

// isPanicTag recognizes the panic intrinsic written as a TEMPLATE TAG.
//
// specification/failures.mdx writes the intrinsic as `panic(...)` and writes no
// tagged-template spelling, and the runtime says why: a tag hands `makePanic` a
// `TemplateStringsArray`, which is not a string, so the authored text is demoted
// into `cause` and the message degrades to the generic "Smithers panic".
// Measured on this backend before this rule: a panic tag over the template
// "authored message" checked clean and aborted with a Panic whose `cause` was
// the ARRAY [ 'authored message' ] rather than the authored string, so the two
// backends built structurally different Panic values for the same program (the
// reference's message degraded outright, to "Smithers panic"). The ambient
// spelling was worse: `Reflect.panic` in a tag position survived lowering
// untouched and the accepted program died with
// `TypeError: Reflect.panic is not a function`.
//
// The two leaf tests are `isPanicCall`'s own, so the tag spelling and the call
// spelling recognize the same intrinsic. What is added around them is the walk a
// TAG position needs and a callee position does not: `panic` reached through a
// type-only wrapper or a `const` value alias still IS `panic` at run time, and
// every one of those spellings degrades identically, so a recognizer that missed
// one would leave that spelling silently broken. The wrapper table is
// `typeOnlyWrapperOperand` — THE ONE TABLE — and the binding step is
// `constantInitializerOfSymbol`, the same `const`-only step `contextReceiverOf`
// uses and for the same reason: a reassigned `let` is no evidence about the
// value at the use.
//
// The refusal is SMITHERS1503, the diagnostic that already answers "this is the
// panic operation in a spelling the lowering does not support", reported at the
// whole tagged expression exactly as the call form is. The shape is
// SMITHERS1604's: refuse the OPERATION, leave the NAME resolvable — `panic`
// stays importable, callable and assignable, and `const p = panic; p("x")` still
// aborts with its authored message.
func (s *preludeSymbols) isPanicTag(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(node) {
		return false
	}
	return s.namesPanicIntrinsic(node.AsTaggedTemplateExpression().Tag, 0)
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) namesPanicIntrinsic(expression *ast.Node, depth int) bool {
	if expression == nil || depth > 16 {
		return false
	}
	if operand := typeOnlyWrapperOperand(expression); operand != nil {
		return s.namesPanicIntrinsic(operand, depth+1)
	}
	if selection, selected := s.selectMember(expression); selected {
		return symbolDeclaredBy(s.resolveAliasSymbol(s.memberSymbol(selection)), s.reflectPanicDecls)
	}
	if symbolDeclaredBy(s.resolvedSymbolAt(expression), s.panicDecls) {
		return true
	}
	if !ast.IsIdentifier(expression) {
		return false
	}
	return s.namesPanicIntrinsic(constantInitializerOfSymbol(s.resolvedSymbolAt(expression)), depth+1)
}

// resolvedSymbolAt follows an import alias to the symbol it names. `panic` is
// reached by importing it, so the binding at the call site is an alias and only
// the aliased symbol carries the prelude's own declaration.
func (s *preludeSymbols) resolvedSymbolAt(node *ast.Node) *ast.Symbol {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return s.resolveAliasSymbol(s.checker.GetSymbolAtLocation(node))
}

// resolveAliasSymbol is the alias/merge half of resolvedSymbolAt, shared with
// the callers that already hold a resolved symbol rather than a node.
func (s *preludeSymbols) resolveAliasSymbol(symbol *ast.Symbol) *ast.Symbol {
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if symbol.Flags&ast.SymbolFlagsAlias != 0 {
		if resolved := s.checker.GetAliasedSymbol(symbol); resolved != nil {
			symbol = resolved
		}
	}
	return s.checker.GetMergedSymbol(symbol)
}

// resultNamespaceMembers is iterated instead of the map so recognition order is
// fixed; a compiler whose answer depends on map iteration is not reproducible.
var resultNamespaceMembers = []string{"try", "tryPromise", "all"}

// resultConsumerMembers is the locked receiver surface that discharges a
// Result ownership obligation. The names are consulted only while indexing the
// compiler-owned prelude; authored calls are recognized from the declaration
// nodes collected there.
//
// This is the fork's own copy of the reference's RESULT_MEMBER_SIGNATURES
// (poc/src/language/semantic.ts), in the same order and naming the same
// members. Two hand-maintained walks over one member set is the defect shape
// that made `flatten` and `tapBoth` implemented, tested, documented, and
// unreachable; the reference collapsed its two copies into one table, and this
// copy cannot be collapsed into that one because it is Go source compiled into
// a foreign checkout. It is pinned to the reference instead:
// compiler/fork_result_member_surface_test.go reads BOTH files and fails when
// the name lists differ, so a member added on one side and not the other is a
// red test rather than a silent reachability gap.
//
// `unwrap` is deliberately not here. The two prelude classes still implement it
// as the runtime's missed-lowering fallback — `resultUnwrap` collects it, and
// the retired-syntax rule refuses the authored spelling as SMITHERS1206 — but a
// member that authored code may not write cannot be a member that discharges an
// obligation.
var resultConsumerMembers = []string{
	"isOk", "isError", "match", "map", "mapError", "andThen", "flatten",
	"recover", "tap", "tapError", "tapBoth", "unwrapOr", "expect",
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) resultConsumerCall(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return false
	}
	selection, selected := s.calleeSelection(node)
	if !selected {
		return false
	}
	symbol := s.memberSymbol(selection)
	return symbolDeclaredBy(symbol, s.resultConsumerDecls)
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) promiseCombinatorCall(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return false
	}
	selection, selected := s.calleeSelection(node)
	if !selected {
		return false
	}
	symbol := s.memberSymbol(selection)
	for _, name := range []string{"all", "allSettled", "race", "any", "allKeyed", "allSettledKeyed"} {
		if symbolDeclaredBy(symbol, s.promiseCombinatorDecls[name]) {
			return s.checker.GetPromisedTypeOfPromise(s.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(node)) != nil
		}
	}
	return false
}

// resultBoundaryMembers are the subset of the namespace whose first argument is
// a body the adapter invokes inside its own try/catch.
var resultBoundaryMembers = map[string]bool{"try": true, "tryPromise": true}

// resultNamespaceExports maps each member to the prelude runtime export that
// implements it.
var resultNamespaceExports = map[string]string{
	"try":        "smithersTry",
	"tryPromise": "smithersTryPromise",
	"all":        "smithersResultAll",
}

// isErrorRuntimeMember identifies one of the global Error helpers whose
// authored method call executes against the prelude-installed prototype
// implementation. The resolved declaration, not the property spelling, is the
// authority, so unrelated user methods do not pull in or depend on the prelude.
func (s *preludeSymbols) isErrorRuntimeMember(node *ast.Node) bool {
	selection, selected := s.selectMember(node)
	if !selected {
		return false
	}
	return symbolDeclaredBy(s.memberSymbol(selection), s.errorRuntimeDecls)
}

// isCompilerModuleSpecifier recognizes a compiler-owned virtual module by the
// module symbol the checker resolved it to. The authored spelling is never
// used to decide whether an import is intrinsic.
func (s *preludeSymbols) isCompilerModuleSpecifier(specifier *ast.Node) bool {
	if specifier == nil {
		return false
	}
	if durableResolvedModule(s.checker, specifier) {
		return true
	}
	if comptimeResolvedModule(s.checker, specifier) {
		return true
	}
	if s.module == nil {
		return false
	}
	symbol := s.checker.ResolveExternalModuleName(specifier)
	return symbol != nil && s.checker.GetMergedSymbol(symbol) == s.module
}

// isVariantUnion reports whether every constituent of a checker type was
// declared by one of the prelude's variant classes. This recognizes a Result
// after narrowing or instantiation has dropped the alias.
func isVariantUnion(t *checker.Type, classes ...*ast.Node) bool {
	if t == nil {
		return false
	}
	constituents := []*checker.Type{t}
	if t.IsUnion() {
		constituents = t.Types()
	}
	if len(constituents) == 0 {
		return false
	}
	for _, constituent := range constituents {
		if !declaresAnyOf(constituent.Symbol(), classes...) {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// isResultType reports whether a checker type is a Result: either it still
// carries the prelude's alias, or (after narrowing, instantiation or explicit
// variant typing) every constituent is one of the prelude's variant classes.
func (s *preludeSymbols) isResultType(t *checker.Type) bool {
	if t == nil {
		return false
	}
	if s.isResultSymbol(t.Alias().Symbol()) {
		return true
	}
	return isVariantUnion(t, s.okClass, s.errClass)
}

// unwrapKind distinguishes a retired Result `.unwrap()` call from an ordinary
// same-spelled method. It no longer denotes propagation.
type unwrapKind int

const (
	unwrapNone unwrapKind = iota
	unwrapResult
)

// unwrapCall reports whether a call expression is the retired
// `<result>.unwrap()` spelling, using the resolved property declaration and
// Result type identity rather than authored member text.
func (s *preludeSymbols) unwrapCall(node *ast.Node) (*ast.Node, unwrapKind) {
	selection, ok := s.zeroArgumentMethodCall(node)
	if !ok {
		return nil, unwrapNone
	}
	symbol := s.memberSymbol(selection)
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil, unwrapNone
	}
	result := false
	for _, declaration := range symbol.Declarations {
		if _, ok := s.resultUnwrap[declaration]; ok {
			result = true
		}
	}
	if !result {
		return nil, unwrapNone
	}
	receiverType := s.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(selection.receiver)
	if result && s.isResultType(receiverType) {
		return selection.receiver, unwrapResult
	}
	return nil, unwrapNone
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) zeroArgumentMethodCall(node *ast.Node) (memberSelection, bool) {
	if !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return memberSelection{}, false
	}
	call := node.AsCallExpression()
	if call.Arguments == nil || len(call.Arguments.Nodes) != 0 {
		return memberSelection{}, false
	}
	return s.calleeSelection(node)
}

type errorMatchKind int

const (
	errorMatchNone errorMatchKind = iota
	errorMatchExhaustive
	errorMatchPartial
)

// matchCall reports whether a call expression is a compiler-owned Error.match
// or Error.matchPartial operation, resolved through the prelude declarations.
// Arguments are returned unvalidated so lowering can issue the language's
// precise shape/fallback diagnostic before ordinary checker cascades escape.
func (s *preludeSymbols) matchCall(node *ast.Node) (receiver *ast.Node, handlers *ast.Node, fallback *ast.Node, kind errorMatchKind) {
	if !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return nil, nil, nil, errorMatchNone
	}
	selection, selected := s.calleeSelection(node)
	if !selected {
		return nil, nil, nil, errorMatchNone
	}
	call := node.AsCallExpression()
	symbol := s.memberSymbol(selection)
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil, nil, nil, errorMatchNone
	}
	kind = errorMatchNone
	for _, declaration := range symbol.Declarations {
		if _, exists := s.matchDecls[declaration]; exists {
			kind = errorMatchExhaustive
			break
		}
		if _, exists := s.matchPartialDecls[declaration]; exists {
			kind = errorMatchPartial
			break
		}
	}
	if kind == errorMatchNone {
		return nil, nil, nil, errorMatchNone
	}
	if call.Arguments != nil && len(call.Arguments.Nodes) > 0 {
		handlers = call.Arguments.Nodes[0]
	}
	if call.Arguments != nil && len(call.Arguments.Nodes) > 1 {
		fallback = call.Arguments.Nodes[1]
	}
	return selection.receiver, handlers, fallback, kind
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) expectCall(node *ast.Node) (*ast.Node, bool) {
	if !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return nil, false
	}
	call := node.AsCallExpression()
	selection, selected := s.calleeSelection(node)
	if call.Arguments == nil || len(call.Arguments.Nodes) != 1 || !selected {
		return nil, false
	}
