	if !symbolDeclaredBy(s.memberSymbol(selection), s.expectDecls) ||
		!s.isResultType(s.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(selection.receiver)) {
		return nil, false
	}
	return selection.receiver, true
}

// liftShape is the checked return contract of the function being lowered. It
// is derived from the declared return type annotation by resolved symbol
// identity, and it drives every lift decision inside the body.
type liftShape struct {
	async  bool // the declared return type is `Promise<...>` on an async function
	result bool // the (awaited) return type is the prelude's Result
	// panics reports whether the function's PUBLISHED failure row names the
	// distinguished `Panic` case — an authored `Result<A, Panic>` annotation, or
	// an inferred row that already carries Panic from a foreign boundary.
	//
	// It gates materialization only. specification/failures.mdx §Panic Does Not
	// Widen a Return Type: "An author MAY still annotate `Result<A, Panic>`
	// explicitly to materialize a panic as a value ... The prohibition is on the
	// compiler *forcing* that widening, not on an author choosing it." A
	// function whose channel is `Result<A, Missing>` publishes `Missing` as its
	// expected-error channel; turning a panic into that channel's error variant
	// would hand a `Panic` to a caller's exhaustive `match` over `Missing`.
	panics bool
}

func (s liftShape) lifts() bool { return s.result }

// loweringReport is a Smithers diagnostic raised by the lowering itself, at an
// authored position. The lowering never approximates: a construct it cannot
// lower soundly is left byte-for-byte intact and reported here.
type loweringReport struct {
	code     string
	category string
	message  string
	start    int
	length   int
}

func loweringReportCategory(report loweringReport) string {
	if report.category != "" {
		return report.category
	}
	return "error"
}

// errorRow is one member of the statically known error union at a match site,
// or one capability in a requirement row. Nothing about a row is ever encoded in
// a checker TypeFlags bit, which is a full uint32 the fork owns.
//
// A row has one of exactly two identities:
//
//   - NOMINAL. The row IS a checker-resolved class or type-parameter symbol.
//     Every failure row, and every capability a `Capability.context()` read
//     charges, is nominal: some declaration in the program produces it, so
//     there is a symbol to be identical to, and two spellings of one class are
//     one row because they resolve to one symbol.
//   - AMBIENT. The row is a capability charged by an operation the ambient
//     environment publishes, and NO declaration in the charging program produces
//     it, so there is no symbol at all. Identity is the NAME.
//     specification/compatibility.mdx §Determinism-Sensitive Members rows three
//     and five are the whole population: `Promise.race`/`any` charge
//     `Scheduler` and the ICU-backed members charge `Locale`, and neither
//     `Scheduler` nor `Locale` is a class the charging program declares or
//     imports.
//
// THIS USED TO BE A FAIL-OPEN, and the shape is worth keeping written down
// because it is the shape that hides. `rowSet` was `map[*ast.Symbol]errorRow`
// and `addRow` returned false — SILENTLY — for a row whose symbol was nil. So an
// ambient charge could be wired in, compile clean, and enforce nothing: MEASURED
// on 2026-09-01, the charge sites for rows three and five were added against the
// old table and every one of the five charging vectors in
// compiler/ambient-charge-vectors.json produced output byte-identical to the run
// with no wiring at all. Migration step 14 met the same wall and removed its own
// wiring rather than ship something that read as mirrored, which was the right
// call about the wrong half of the problem.
//
// `addRow` now FAILS CLOSED. A row it cannot represent panics instead of being
// discarded, because an unrepresentable row means the analysis lost a
// requirement, and losing a requirement quietly is the one outcome a row
// analysis must not have — every other failure of this table is loud. The
// precedent for a panic on an impossible state in this bridge is
// `preludeSymbols.exported`.
type errorRow struct {
	symbol *ast.Symbol
	name   string
	// ambient marks the row whose identity IS its name. It is an explicit flag
	// rather than `symbol == nil`, so that an UNRESOLVED nominal row — a bug in
	// a caller that skipped its nil guard — stays distinguishable from a
	// deliberately symbol-less one and still fails closed instead of silently
	// becoming a row named "Error".
	ambient bool
}

// rowKey is a row's identity inside a rowSet. Exactly one field is ever set:
// `symbol` for a nominal row, `name` for an ambient one.
//
// A nominal row is keyed by its symbol ALONE, with `name` left empty, which
// preserves the previous table's identity exactly: two rows for one symbol
// carrying different rendered names stay one row, as they always did, and
// `rowName`'s module qualification is unaffected because it never took part in
// identity.
//
// IDENTITY IN THIS TABLE IS NOT WHAT THE ROW PUBLISHES, and the distinction is
// load-bearing. A nominal `Locale` and an ambient `Locale` are two entries here
// and ONE published requirement, because the reference frontend's row is a
// `Set<string>` and a row is a set of capability names. `missingRowSet` and
// `formatRowSet` are where that collapse happens, and both say why; a table that
// only got the keys right published `{Locale, Locale}` where the reference
// published `Locale`, measured 2026-09-01.
type rowKey struct {
	symbol *ast.Symbol
	name   string
}

// key returns the row's identity, and false when it has none. A nominal row with
// no symbol has lost the identity it is supposed to carry; an ambient row with a
// symbol, or with no name, never had one. All three are analysis bugs rather
// than inputs, and addRow refuses them loudly.
func (row errorRow) key() (rowKey, bool) {
	if row.ambient {
		if row.symbol != nil || row.name == "" {
			return rowKey{}, false
		}
		return rowKey{name: row.name}, true
	}
	if row.symbol == nil {
		return rowKey{}, false
	}
	return rowKey{symbol: row.symbol}, true
}

// ambientRow is a capability charged by an ambient operation; see errorRow. It
// is the only constructor for an ambient row, so a caller cannot reach the
// unrepresentable shapes by accident.
func ambientRow(name string) errorRow {
	return errorRow{name: name, ambient: true}
}

// rowSet is the nominal-or-ambient row side table; TypeFlags remains entirely
// owned by the fork.
type rowSet map[rowKey]errorRow

func addRow(into rowSet, row errorRow) bool {
	key, representable := row.key()
	if !representable {
		// Fail closed. The alternative is the silent drop this replaced, which
		// loses a requirement and reports success — see errorRow.
		panic("smithers: unrepresentable row: " + unrepresentableRowDetail(row))
	}
	if _, exists := into[key]; exists {
		return false
	}
	into[key] = row
	return true
}

func unrepresentableRowDetail(row errorRow) string {
	if !row.ambient {
		return "a nominal row with no resolved symbol, rendered as " + strconvQuote(row.name)
	}
	if row.symbol != nil {
		return "an ambient row carrying a symbol, rendered as " + strconvQuote(row.name)
	}
	return "an ambient row with no name"
}

func addRows(into rowSet, from rowSet) bool {
	changed := false
	for _, row := range from {
		changed = addRow(into, row) || changed
	}
	return changed
}

// missingRowSet is the rows of `values` that `covered` does not discharge.
//
// COMPARED BY NAME, not by key, and that is deliberate rather than loose. This
// is the capability-discharge subtraction, and the reference frontend's row is a
// `Set<string>` of capability names, so its subtraction is a name subtraction.
// Keying identity by symbol here would make the two backends disagree the moment
// a program has a row whose identity is not a symbol: an ambient `Locale` charge
// and a `Locale.context()` read are ONE requirement to the reference and would
// be two to a symbol-keyed subtraction, so a layer providing `Locale` would
// discharge the row on one backend and leave it standing on the other.
// MEASURED on the reference 2026-09-01: a function that reads `Locale.context()`
// and calls `"a".localeCompare(b)` publishes `requirements: ["Locale"]` — one
// entry, not two.
//
// Names are safe to compare because buildRowNames makes them injective over the
// symbols that reach a row: two modules declaring a same-named capability get
// `Name@module/path`, which is the whole job of the qualifier, and
// conformance/identity/module-row-qualifier.json pins the two backends to one
// answer for it. So a name collision here is the same name collision the
// reference has, rather than a new one this table invents.
func missingRowSet(values rowSet, covered rowSet) rowSet {
	discharged := make(map[string]struct{}, len(covered))
	for _, row := range covered {
		discharged[row.name] = struct{}{}
	}
	missing := make(rowSet)
	for key, row := range values {
		if _, ok := discharged[row.name]; !ok {
			missing[key] = row
		}
	}
	return missing
}

type rowCallEdge struct {
	node                 *ast.Node
	callee               *functionRows
	propagatesFailure    bool
	instantiatedFailures rowSet
}

type rowProvideEdge struct {
	node      *ast.Node
	callback  *functionRows
	provided  rowSet
	complete  bool
	argument  *ast.Node
	callbacks *ast.Node
}

type foreignPolicy struct {
	kind        string // never, panic, or declared
	errorSymbol *ast.Symbol
	errorName   string
	async       bool
}

type functionRows struct {
	node               *ast.Node
	file               *ast.SourceFile
	symbol             *ast.Symbol
	name               string
	exported           bool
	explicitReturn     bool
	declaredShape      liftShape
	declaredFailures   rowSet
	directFailures     rowSet
	bodyFailures       rowSet
	failures           rowSet
	directRequirements rowSet
	requirements       rowSet
	calls              []rowCallEdge
	provides           []rowProvideEdge
	// accessorUses are the project accessors this body invokes by reading or
	// writing a property. `box.size` CALLS the getter and `box.first = 1` CALLS
	// the setter, so both are ordinary calls for row purposes; see
	// accessorInvocations.
	accessorUses []*functionRows
	// callbackValues are the checked function values this body hands across a
	// call or `new` argument boundary. The callee invokes them inside the call,
	// so their capability requirements ARE this body's requirements
	// (specification/requirements.mdx §Inference: "Requirement inference MUST be
	// transitive through ordinary calls"). Before this edge existed,
	// `xs.map(() => Db.context()...)` published `requirements: []` while reading
	// `Db` at run time, and the top-level unsatisfied-requirement check
	// certified the program because the row it consults was empty. See
	// crossingCallbacks for the value edge and its one exclusion.
	callbackValues   []*functionRows
	hasContractError bool
	genericTemplate  bool
	unconsumedParams map[int]bool
	// resultBoundaryBody marks the body argument of `Result.try` /
	// `Result.tryPromise`. Inside it the adapter owns the throw scope, so a
	// foreign call keeps its authored throw instead of being wrapped again.
	resultBoundaryBody bool
}

func (fn *functionRows) inferredFallible() bool {
	return !fn.explicitReturn && len(fn.bodyFailures) != 0
}

// rowAnalysis is the whole-project function graph and its fixed-point rows.
// Every edge and member is checker-resolved; import spelling never enters the
// graph, so aliases, namespace access, relative modules, and cycles all share
// the same representation.
type rowAnalysis struct {
	checker *checker.Checker
	prelude *preludeSymbols
	project *project
	// comptime owns the nodes the comptime frontend replaces or erases. Whole
	// program row rules do not apply to source that never reaches the emitted
	// artifact, so reports against those nodes are dropped rather than shown.
	comptime      *comptimeAnalysis
	functions     []*functionRows
	byNode        map[*ast.Node]*functionRows
	bySymbol      map[*ast.Symbol]*functionRows
	layerBindings map[*ast.Symbol]*ast.Node
	rowNames      map[*ast.Symbol]string
	reports       map[*ast.SourceFile][]loweringReport
	topProvides   []rowProvideEdge
	foreignCalls  map[*ast.Node]foreignPolicy
	// resultPropagations maps each valid postfix `!` AST node to its Result
	// operand. The parser already represents postfix `!` as NonNullExpression;
	// the `.sm` semantic pass removes that old meaning and owns every such node.
	resultPropagations map[*ast.Node]*ast.Node
	// A policy can be queried by row inference, provenance, top-level checks,
	// and foreign-boundary checks. Invalid annotation diagnostics are emitted
	// once per authored call even when several of those passes query it.
	foreignAnnotationReports map[*ast.Node]bool
	foreignOrderReports      map[*ast.Node]bool
	// Nodes at which the property/accessor rule already reported SMITHERS1506.
	// `for (const n of foreign.inner)` is BOTH a foreign property read and an
	// implicit iteration of what that read returned; collectFacts runs first and
	// takes the node, and checkImplicitForeignInvocation still owns it when the
	// property rule declined — a trusted getter handing back a foreign Iterable
	// is trusted for the READ and still foreign for the iteration.
	foreignEvaluationReports map[*ast.Node]bool
	// assetImports is keyed by the authored import/re-export declaration or
	// dynamic import call. Potential asset edges are recorded before validation
	// so neither a valid asset nor a fail-closed asset refusal can fall through
	// into foreign-module policy.
	assetImports          map[*ast.Node]assetImportEdge
	refusedForeignModules map[*ast.SourceFile]bool
	// staticTrustRoots and dynamicTrustRoots are the TRUSTED foreign modules one
	// authored `.sm` loads directly, each remembered with the authored edge that
	// reached it, so walkReachedForeignModules can ask the rest of the reached
	// closure for the same claim and report against a node the author can change.
	// They are kept apart only so the walk can visit them in the reference's
	// order — static edges in statement order, then dynamic ones — even though
	// checkDynamicModuleImports runs first.
	staticTrustRoots  map[*ast.SourceFile][]moduleTrustRoot
	dynamicTrustRoots map[*ast.SourceFile][]moduleTrustRoot
	// fileReferences indexes identifier occurrences by resolved symbol, one
	// entry per source file, built on first use by the must-consume ownership
	// walk. See identifierReferences.
	fileReferences map[*ast.SourceFile]map[*ast.Symbol][]*ast.Node
}

func symbolDeclaredBy(symbol *ast.Symbol, declarations map[*ast.Node]struct{}) bool {
	if symbol == nil {
		return false
	}
	for _, declaration := range symbol.Declarations {
		if _, ok := declarations[declaration]; ok {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (s *preludeSymbols) layerCall(node *ast.Node, method string) bool {
	if !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return false
	}
	selection, selected := s.calleeSelection(node)
	if !selected {
		return false
	}
	declarations := s.layerDecls[method]
	if len(declarations) == 0 {
		return false
	}
	symbol := s.memberSymbol(selection)
	return symbolDeclaredBy(symbol, declarations)
}

// isPreludeContextMember reports whether a member selection resolves to the
// compiler-owned `Context.context`. Identity, never spelling: an ordinary class
// with a `context` member resolves elsewhere and stays ordinary.
//
// The recognition of a capability READ lives in `rowAnalysis.contextRequirement`
// instead, because the receiver — not the member — is what decides whether the
// call has a recordable nominal key, and a receiver that pins nothing must be
// refused rather than fall through as "not a context call".
func (s *preludeSymbols) isPreludeContextMember(selection memberSelection) bool {
	return symbolDeclaredBy(s.memberSymbol(selection), s.contextDecls)
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) canonical(symbol *ast.Symbol) *ast.Symbol {
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if symbol.Flags&ast.SymbolFlagsAlias != 0 {
		if resolved := a.checker.GetAliasedSymbol(symbol); resolved != nil {
			symbol = resolved
		}
	}
	return a.checker.GetMergedSymbol(symbol)
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) symbolAt(node *ast.Node) *ast.Symbol {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return a.canonical(a.checker.GetSymbolAtLocation(node))
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) rowName(symbol *ast.Symbol) string {
	if symbol == nil {
		return "Error"
	}
	if name := a.rowNames[symbol]; name != "" {
		return name
	}
	return symbol.Name
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) row(symbol *ast.Symbol) errorRow {
	symbol = a.canonical(symbol)
	return errorRow{symbol: symbol, name: a.rowName(symbol)}
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) typeHasBaseSymbol(t *checker.Type, target *ast.Symbol, seen map[*checker.Type]bool) bool {
	if t == nil || target == nil || seen[t] {
		return false
	}
	seen[t] = true
	if symbol := a.canonical(t.Symbol()); symbol == target {
		return true
	}
	if t.Flags()&checker.TypeFlagsTypeParameter != 0 {
		constraint := a.checker.GetBaseConstraintOfType(t)
		return constraint != nil && constraint != t && a.typeHasBaseSymbol(constraint, target, seen)
	}
	if t.IsUnion() || t.IsIntersection() {
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			if a.typeHasBaseSymbol(part, target, seen) {
				return true
			}
		}
		return false
	}
	if t.Flags()&checker.TypeFlagsObject == 0 {
		return false
	}
	for _, base := range a.checker.GetBaseTypes(t) {
		if a.typeHasBaseSymbol(base, target, seen) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) isErrorType(t *checker.Type) bool {
	if t == nil {
		return false
	}
	if t.IsUnion() {
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			if !a.isErrorType(part) {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	return a.typeHasBaseSymbol(t, a.prelude.error, make(map[*checker.Type]bool))
}

// isNominalErrorClass reports whether a class declaration is one of the named
// Error classes specification/failures.mdx, "Error Classes", makes usable as a
// nominal recoverable error: "Any named class extending 'Error' MUST be usable
// as a nominal recoverable error."
//
// The three conditions are exactly the reference frontend's
// collectErrorDeclarations: a class DECLARATION (a class expression has no
// binding the registration could name), with a name, with a heritage clause,
// whose checker type is an Error type. Nothing reads the spelling of the base.
//
// It is the single predicate behind both halves of the identity contract: the
// SMITHERS1150 duplicate-name refusal and the emitted registration that gives
// the class its stable identity and derived codec. Keeping them on one
// predicate is what stops a class from being refused a duplicate identity it
// would never have been given, or given one whose duplicate went unrefused.
// The one asymmetry is deliberate and lives at the registration site: an
// ambient declaration reserves an identity but emits no binding to register.
func (a *rowAnalysis) isNominalErrorClass(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsClassDeclaration(node) || node.Name() == nil {
		return false
	}
	heritage := node.ClassLikeData().HeritageClauses
	if heritage == nil || len(heritage.Nodes) == 0 {
		return false
	}
	return a.isErrorType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(node.Name()))
}

// hasErrorClassBase reports whether a class extends another Error CLASS
// DECLARATION, as opposed to the ambient `Error` interface/var or a foreign
// declared type. It is the "exactly one level of an inheritance chain may carry
// a brand" test nominalErrorBrand applies, and it is deliberately the reference
// frontend's hasErrorClassBase, symbol for symbol: the base expression's symbol
// is unaliased, its class declaration found, and the declaration's own checker
// type asked whether it is an Error. Nothing reads the spelling of the base, so
// an import alias or a same-named class in another module answers the same.
func (a *rowAnalysis) hasErrorClassBase(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if node == nil || node.ClassLikeData() == nil {
		return false
	}
	heritage := node.ClassLikeData().HeritageClauses
	if heritage == nil {
		return false
	}
	for _, clause := range heritage.Nodes {
		if clause.AsHeritageClause().Token != ast.KindExtendsKeyword {
			continue
		}
		types := clause.AsHeritageClause().Types
		if types == nil {
			continue
		}
		for _, typeNode := range types.Nodes {
			base := classDeclarationOf(a.symbolAt(typeNode.Expression()))
			if base == nil || base.Name() == nil {
				continue
			}
			if a.isErrorType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(base.Name())) {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) extendsContext(symbol *ast.Symbol) bool {
	symbol = a.canonical(symbol)
	if symbol == nil {
		return false
	}
	return a.typeHasBaseSymbol(a.checker.GetDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol), a.prelude.context, make(map[*checker.Type]bool))
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) rowsOfType(t *checker.Type) (rowSet, bool) {
	rows := make(rowSet)
	if t == nil {
		return rows, false
	}
	if t.Flags()&checker.TypeFlagsNever != 0 {
		return rows, true
	}
	if t.IsUnion() {
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			values, ok := a.rowsOfType(part)
			if !ok {
				return rows, false
			}
			addRows(rows, values)
		}
		return rows, true
	}
	if !a.isErrorType(t) {
		return rows, false
	}
	symbol := a.canonical(t.Symbol())
	if symbol == nil && t.Alias() != nil {
		symbol = a.canonical(t.Alias().Symbol())
	}
	if symbol == nil {
		return rows, false
	}
	addRow(rows, a.row(symbol))
	return rows, true
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) resultArguments(t *checker.Type) (success *checker.Type, failure *checker.Type, ok bool) {
	if t == nil || !a.prelude.isResultType(t) {
		return nil, nil, false
	}
	if alias := t.Alias(); alias != nil && a.prelude.isResultSymbol(alias.Symbol()) {
		arguments := alias.TypeArguments()
		if len(arguments) == 2 {
			return arguments[0], arguments[1], true
		}
	}
	constituents := []*checker.Type{t}
	if t.IsUnion() {
		constituents = t.Types()
	}
	for _, part := range constituents {
		if !declaresAnyOf(part.Symbol(), a.prelude.errClass) {
			continue
		}
		arguments := a.checker.GetTypeArguments(part)
		if len(arguments) == 1 {
			return nil, arguments[0], true
		}
	}
	return nil, nil, false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) functionType(node *ast.Node) (*checker.Type, bool) {
	if node == nil || node.Type() == nil {
		return nil, false
	}
	t := a.checker.GetTypeFromTypeNode(node.Type())
	async := ast.HasSyntacticModifier(node, ast.ModifierFlagsAsync)
	// Failure-row contracts live in Result even when a plain function forwards
	// Promise<Result<...>> without using the async keyword. This analysis shape
	// is deliberately broader than lowerer.shapeOf: only async functions are
	// rewritten through their awaited value, but both spellings carry the same
	// statically declared failure row.
	if promised := a.checker.GetPromisedTypeOfPromise(t); promised != nil {
		t = promised
	}
	return t, async
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) declaredFunctionShape(node *ast.Node) (liftShape, rowSet, *checker.Type) {
	rows := make(rowSet)
	t, async := a.functionType(node)
	if t == nil {
		return liftShape{}, rows, nil
	}
	shape := liftShape{async: async}
	if _, failure, ok := a.resultArguments(t); ok {
		shape.result = true
		if failures, known := a.rowsOfType(failure); known {
			addRows(rows, failures)
		}
		return shape, rows, failure
	}
	return liftShape{}, rows, nil
}

const deferredRowFlags = checker.TypeFlagsTypeParameter | checker.TypeFlagsConditional |
	checker.TypeFlagsIndexedAccess | checker.TypeFlagsSubstitution

func hasDeferredRowType(t *checker.Type, seen map[*checker.Type]bool) bool {
	if t == nil || seen[t] {
		return false
	}
	seen[t] = true
	if t.Flags()&deferredRowFlags != 0 {
		return true
	}
	if t.IsUnion() || t.IsIntersection() {
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			if hasDeferredRowType(part, seen) {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	if alias := t.Alias(); alias != nil {
		for _, argument := range alias.TypeArguments() {
			if hasDeferredRowType(argument, seen) {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

func functionBinding(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if name := node.Name(); name != nil {
		return name
	}
	if node.Parent != nil && ast.IsVariableDeclaration(node.Parent) {
		return node.Parent.Name()
	}
	return nil
}

// bindingText is the display name of a function-like declaration's binding, and
// "" for a binding that has no source text to read.
//
// ast.Node.Text PANICS on any kind it does not render, and a class member name
// may be a ComputedPropertyName: `static [Symbol.hasInstance](value: unknown)`
// is ordinary TypeScript class syntax, which specification/compatibility.mdx
// keeps in `.sm`. Reading it unguarded took the whole bridge down with
// "Unhandled case in Node.Text: *ast.ComputedPropertyName" before any diagnostic
// existed, so the observation was a crashed backend rather than a verdict — the
// one outcome a differential harness cannot score. The name is only ever used
// to render a diagnostic, so a binding with no readable text is anonymous.
func bindingText(binding *ast.Node) string {
	if binding == nil {
		return ""
	}
	switch binding.Kind {
	case ast.KindIdentifier, ast.KindPrivateIdentifier,
		ast.KindStringLiteral, ast.KindNoSubstitutionTemplateLiteral, ast.KindNumericLiteral:
		return binding.Text()
	}
	return ""
}

func functionIsExported(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if ast.HasSyntacticModifier(node, ast.ModifierFlagsExport) {
		return true
	}
	if node.Parent == nil || !ast.IsVariableDeclaration(node.Parent) ||
		node.Parent.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer != node {
		return false
	}
	declarationList := node.Parent.Parent
	if declarationList == nil || !ast.IsVariableDeclarationList(declarationList) || declarationList.Parent == nil ||
		!ast.IsVariableStatement(declarationList.Parent) {
		return false
	}
	return ast.HasSyntacticModifier(declarationList.Parent, ast.ModifierFlagsExport)
}

func newRowAnalysis(typeChecker *checker.Checker, prelude *preludeSymbols, files []*ast.SourceFile, comptime *comptimeAnalysis, project *project) *rowAnalysis {
	analysis := &rowAnalysis{
		checker:                  typeChecker,
		prelude:                  prelude,
		project:                  project,
		comptime:                 comptime,
		byNode:                   make(map[*ast.Node]*functionRows),
		bySymbol:                 make(map[*ast.Symbol]*functionRows),
		layerBindings:            make(map[*ast.Symbol]*ast.Node),
		rowNames:                 make(map[*ast.Symbol]string),
		reports:                  make(map[*ast.SourceFile][]loweringReport),
		foreignCalls:             make(map[*ast.Node]foreignPolicy),
		resultPropagations:       make(map[*ast.Node]*ast.Node),
		foreignAnnotationReports: make(map[*ast.Node]bool),
		foreignOrderReports:      make(map[*ast.Node]bool),
		foreignEvaluationReports: make(map[*ast.Node]bool),
		assetImports:             make(map[*ast.Node]assetImportEdge),
		refusedForeignModules:    make(map[*ast.SourceFile]bool),
		staticTrustRoots:         make(map[*ast.SourceFile][]moduleTrustRoot),
		dynamicTrustRoots:        make(map[*ast.SourceFile][]moduleTrustRoot),
		fileReferences:           make(map[*ast.SourceFile]map[*ast.Symbol][]*ast.Node),
	}
	analysis.buildRowNames(files)
	analysis.checkAssetImports(files)
	analysis.checkDynamicModuleImports(files)
	analysis.checkForeignModuleTrust(files)
	analysis.walkReachedForeignModules(files)
	analysis.collectFunctions(files)
	analysis.checkNestedResultContracts()
	analysis.collectLayerBindings(files)
	for _, fn := range analysis.functions {
		analysis.collectFacts(fn)
	}
	analysis.checkForeignProvenanceEscapes(files)
	analysis.collectTopProvides(files)
	analysis.inferRows()
	analysis.collectInferredResultPropagations(files)
	analysis.checkContracts()
	analysis.checkBoundMustConsume(files)
	analysis.checkResultParameters(files)
	analysis.checkUnboundMustConsume(files)
	analysis.checkLayers()
	analysis.checkTopLevelRequirements(files)
	analysis.checkContextReferences(files)
	analysis.checkTopLevelCheckedChannels(files)
	analysis.checkPanicCatchBoundaries(files)
	analysis.checkCrossModuleEscapes(files)
	// Fail-closed frontend rules (smithershostrules.go). They run last because
	// none of them contributes a row: each one only decides whether a program
	// the language forbids may reach emit at all.
	analysis.checkHostGlobals(files)
	analysis.checkStaticBlocks(files)
	analysis.checkDuplicateErrorNames(files)
	analysis.checkForeignBoundaries(files)
	analysis.checkCallbackOwnership(files)
	analysis.checkRetiredTypeGrammar(files)
	analysis.checkRelativeModuleResolution(files)
	analysis.checkProjectImportBindings(files)
	analysis.checkCompilerResultConstructors(files)
	return analysis
}

// checkNestedResultContracts rejects a Result success channel that is itself
// the compiler-owned Result. This is a fact about the authored contract, not a
// failed assignment in its body, so the diagnostic belongs at the return type
// annotation where the implicit normalization policy would have to be chosen.
func (a *rowAnalysis) checkNestedResultContracts() {
	for _, fn := range a.functions {
		t, _ := a.functionType(fn.node)
		success, _, ok := a.resultArguments(t)
		if !ok || !a.prelude.isResultType(success) {
			continue
		}
		a.report(fn.node.Type(), "SMITHERS1203", "nested Result normalization is not specified; make the conversion explicit")
	}
}

// isRowQualifierUnit reports whether one UTF-16 code unit survives a module row
// qualifier verbatim: [A-Za-z0-9._/-].
//
// '+' is deliberately outside it, and that is the whole reason
// moduleRowQualifier is reversible: an unescaped '+' can never occur, so every
// '+' in a qualifier starts exactly one five-unit escape. Same withholding
// trick as isEscapedIdentityPathUnit, over a smaller alphabet — '@' and ':' are
// verbatim there and escaped here, because a row name is spelled
// `Name@qualifier` and keeping '@' out of the qualifier makes the join
// unambiguous on its own.
func isRowQualifierUnit(unit uint16) bool {
	return unit >= '0' && unit <= '9' || unit >= 'A' && unit <= 'Z' || unit >= 'a' && unit <= 'z' ||
		unit == '.' || unit == '_' || unit == '/' || unit == '-'
}

// moduleRowQualifier is the module half of a disambiguated nominal row name.
//
// A disambiguator that re-collides is not one. The predecessor of this function
// rewrote every character outside [A-Za-z0-9._/-] to '_', which is many-to-one
// on exactly the input it is reached for: `a b.sm` and `a_b.sm` both cleaned to
// `a_b`, so two distinct `Boom` classes were handed ONE row name by the very
// mechanism that exists because their bare names already collided.
//
// `+%04X` fixes it the same way stableErrorIdentity's escape does — a bijection
// onto its image, so distinct module paths cannot converge. A path spelled
// entirely in the alphabet is its own escape, so every qualifier this compiler
// has ever minted is unchanged; only the ones that were already colliding move.
//
// It walks UTF-16 CODE UNITS, not runes, and that is load-bearing across
// backends rather than a detail. The predecessor was `for _, character := range
// qualifier`, which walks runes and wrote one '_' per rune, while the reference
// frontend's `.replace(/…/g, "_")` had no `u` flag and walked code units. For an
// astral character in a module path the two therefore disagreed outright:
// `x😀.sm` minted `x_` here and `x__` there. stableErrorIdentity and
// durableFailureIdentity already encode through utf16 for precisely this
// reason; this is the third site.
func moduleRowQualifier(qualifier string) string {
	units := make([]uint16, 0, len(qualifier))
	for _, unit := range utf16.Encode([]rune(qualifier)) {
		if isRowQualifierUnit(unit) {
			units = append(units, unit)
			continue
		}
		units = append(units, utf16.Encode([]rune(fmt.Sprintf("+%04X", unit)))...)
	}
	return string(utf16.Decode(units))
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) buildRowNames(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	byName := make(map[string]map[*ast.Symbol]*ast.SourceFile)
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsClassDeclaration(node) && node.Name() != nil {
				symbol := a.symbolAt(node.Name())
				t := a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(node.Name())
				if symbol != nil && (a.isErrorType(t) || a.extendsContext(symbol)) {
					items := byName[symbol.Name]
					if items == nil {
						items = make(map[*ast.Symbol]*ast.SourceFile)
						byName[symbol.Name] = items
					}
					items[symbol] = file
				}
				return false
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
	for name, items := range byName {
		if len(items) < 2 {
			continue
		}
		for symbol, file := range items {
			qualifier := strings.TrimPrefix(file.FileName(), "/src/")
			qualifier = strings.TrimSuffix(qualifier, ".sm")
			a.rowNames[symbol] = name + "@" + moduleRowQualifier(qualifier)
		}
	}
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) collectFunctions(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsFunctionLikeDeclaration(node) && node.Body() != nil {
				shape, declared, failureType := a.declaredFunctionShape(node)
				binding := functionBinding(node)
				symbol := a.symbolAt(binding)
				name := "<anonymous>"
				if text := bindingText(binding); text != "" {
					name = text
				}
				fn := &functionRows{
					node:               node,
					file:               file,
					symbol:             symbol,
					name:               name,
					exported:           functionIsExported(node),
					resultBoundaryBody: a.isResultBoundaryBody(node),
					explicitReturn:     node.Type() != nil,
					declaredShape:      shape,
					declaredFailures:   declared,
					directFailures:     make(rowSet),
					bodyFailures:       make(rowSet),
					failures:           make(rowSet),
					directRequirements: make(rowSet),
					requirements:       make(rowSet),
					genericTemplate:    len(node.TypeParameters()) != 0 && hasDeferredRowType(failureType, make(map[*checker.Type]bool)),
					unconsumedParams:   make(map[int]bool),
				}
				if shape.result {
					addRows(fn.directFailures, declared)
					addRows(fn.failures, declared)
				}
				a.functions = append(a.functions, fn)
				a.byNode[node] = fn
				if symbol != nil {
					a.bySymbol[symbol] = fn
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

// collectLayerBindings records the layer value a binding names — and only when
// the binding is `const`.
//
// A `let`/`var` binding is resolved from its INITIALIZER forever, and a later
// assignment is invisible, so `let app = Layer.succeed(Db, db); app =
// Layer.succeed(Log, log); Layer.provide(app, body)` was judged COMPLETE with
// `provided = {Db}`, the missing set came out empty, the program compiled
// `ok: true` and then panicked at run time with `capability 'Db' was not
// provided`. Measured on this backend; the `var` twin and the
// reassigned-in-a-helper twin broke identically.
// specification/requirements.mdx §Satisfaction (Locked): "When the compiler
// knows the complete closure, an unsatisfied capability MUST be a compile
// error" — here the compiler BELIEVED it knew the closure and was wrong.
//
// This is the rule checkForeignValueBoundaries already applies for the same
// reason ("a mutable alias cannot retain foreign panic provenance in this
// POC"), and the rule constantInitializer applies for the same reason again: a
// reassignment makes the initializer no evidence at all about the value at the
// read. Everything not recorded here lands on the already-fail-closed
// SMITHERS2104 path, so a mutable layer binding is refused rather than resolved
// from a stale initializer — while a `const` layer that is genuinely MISSING a
// capability still draws the precise SMITHERS2101 that names it.
func (a *rowAnalysis) collectLayerBindings(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsVariableDeclaration(node) {
				declaration := node.AsVariableDeclaration()
				name := node.Name()
				if declaration.Initializer != nil && name != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(name) &&
					node.Parent != nil && ast.IsVariableDeclarationList(node.Parent) && ast.IsVarConst(node) {
					if symbol := a.symbolAt(name); symbol != nil {
						a.layerBindings[symbol] = declaration.Initializer
					}
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

// An INVOCATION is a call, a `new`, or a tagged template. All three name a
// callee in the syntax and RUN it, so all three are ordinary calls for row
// purposes.
//
// The call graph used to be keyed on ast.IsCallExpression alone, and every
// other invocation form dropped the callee's requirement row on the floor.
// Measured on this backend: `` tag`x` ``, `new C()`, an implicit `super()` and
// an explicit one, `` `${obj}` `` through an authored `toString` or
// `Symbol.toPrimitive`, `JSON.stringify(o)` through an authored `toJSON`, an
// authored thenable and an authored `@decorator` each published
// `requirements: []` for a function that reads a capability, compiled
// `ok: true`, and panicked at run time with `capability 'Db' was not
// provided`. specification/requirements.mdx §Inference (Locked): "Calling a
// function with unsatisfied requirements MUST add those capabilities to the
// caller's `R` row. … Requirement inference MUST be transitive through ordinary
// calls."
//
// This mirrors `InvocationExpression` in poc/src/language/semantic.ts; the two
// must agree or the backends diverge.
func isInvocationExpression(node *ast.Node) bool {
	return node != nil && (ast.IsCallExpression(node) || ast.IsNewExpression(node) ||
		ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(node))
}

// invocationArguments is the argument list an invocation hands its callee.
//
// A tagged template's arguments are its SUBSTITUTIONS: `` hostTag`x${cb}` `` is
// `hostTag(parts, cb)`, so `cb` crosses exactly the value boundary a call
// argument crosses and owes exactly the same contract. Reading `Node.Arguments()`
// is not an option — it PANICS on a tagged template — which is why this is one
// function rather than a widened kind test at each site.
func invocationArguments(node *ast.Node) []*ast.Node {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(node) {
		template := node.AsTaggedTemplateExpression().Template
		if template == nil || template.Kind != ast.KindTemplateExpression {
			return nil
		}
		spans := template.AsTemplateExpression().TemplateSpans
		if spans == nil {
			return nil
		}
		arguments := make([]*ast.Node, 0, len(spans.Nodes))
		for _, span := range spans.Nodes {
			arguments = append(arguments, span.AsTemplateSpan().Expression)
		}
		return arguments
	}
	if ast.IsCallExpression(node) || ast.IsNewExpression(node) {
		return node.Arguments()
	}
	return nil
}

// resolveInvokedDeclaration is the checked function a tagged template or a
// `new` invokes.
//
// The checker is asked, exactly as resolveCallee asks it for a call, so
// `new Derived()` over a class with no constructor of its own resolves to the
// BASE constructor it implicitly runs — the implicit-`super()` spelling needs no
// separate case. No symbol fallback is taken here: a `new` names a CLASS, not a
// function, and reading its symbol would resolve nothing a signature has not
// already answered.
func (a *rowAnalysis) resolveInvokedDeclaration(node *ast.Node) *functionRows {
	if node == nil || (!ast.IsNewExpression(node) && !ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(node)) {
		return nil
	}
	if signature := a.checker.GetResolvedSignature(node); signature != nil {
		if declaration := signature.Declaration(); declaration != nil {
			return a.byNode[declaration]
		}
	}
	return nil
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) resolveCallee(call *ast.Node) *functionRows {
	if call == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if ast.IsNewExpression(call) || ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(call) {
		return a.resolveInvokedDeclaration(call)
	}
	if !ast.IsCallExpression(call) {
		return nil
	}
	if signature := a.checker.GetResolvedSignature(call); signature != nil {
		if declaration := signature.Declaration(); declaration != nil {
			if fn := a.byNode[declaration]; fn != nil {
				return fn
			}
		}
	}
	expression := call.AsCallExpression().Expression
	location := expression
	if ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(expression) {
		location = expression.AsPropertyAccessExpression().Name()
	}
	return a.bySymbol[a.symbolAt(location)]
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) isForeignDeclaration(node *ast.Node) bool {
	file := ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(node)
	if file == nil || file.FileName() == preludeVirtualName || file.FileName() == durableVirtualName ||
		file.FileName() == comptimeVirtualName {
		return false
	}
	name := strings.ToLower(file.FileName())
	return strings.HasPrefix(name, "/src/") && !strings.HasSuffix(name, ".sm")
}

// attachedJSDocComment is the JSDoc block the PARSER attaches to one
// declaration, as source text, or "" when the parser attaches none.
//
// THE ONE PLACE that answers "which comment belongs to this declaration".
// Every `@throws` claim admitted at a foreign boundary is read out of the
// declaration `GetResolvedSignature(...).Declaration()` resolved to, so the
// block that is read has to be the block that declaration actually carries.
//
// Scanning the raw trivia between `declaration.Pos()` and the first real token
// is NOT that question, and answering it that way was a measured fail-open on
// this backend. TypeScript's parser collects a member's JSDoc from
// `GetJSDocCommentRanges`, which for every kind but a parameter, a type
// parameter, a function expression, an arrow, a parenthesis, a variable
// declaration and an export specifier is `GetLeadingCommentRanges` alone — and
// that iterator does not begin collecting until it has crossed a line break.
// So a `/** @throws {never} */` written on the SAME LINE as a class member is
// trailing trivia of the token before it and is attached to nothing:
//
//	class SameLineMarked { /** @throws {never} */ constructor(v: string) {…} }
//
// leaves `constructor.JSDoc(file)` empty, and the reference (which reads
// `ts.getJSDocTags`) reports the constructor as unannotated. The raw-trivia
// scan found the block anyway, so this backend believed a trust claim the
// checker does not attach to anything, accepted `new SameLineMarked("bad")`
// inside a function declared `: string` with zero diagnostics, and ran it —
// throwing a raw host `Error` with exit code 1 where the reference refuses
// with SMITHERS1504. Asking the parser is the only way to stay in step with a
// rule the parser owns.
//
// The block is returned as TEXT rather than as parsed tags on purpose: every
// caller below compares the marker with the exact case and exact brace syntax
// the specification prints (exactThrowsAnnotations, exactModuleMarker), and a
// JSDoc tag name that the fork's JSDoc parser has already normalized is not
// the thing those rules are written against.
func attachedJSDocComment(declaration *ast.Node) string {
	file := ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(declaration)
	if file == nil {
		return ""
	}
	documents := declaration.JSDoc(file)
	if len(documents) == 0 {
		return ""
	}
	// The LAST attached block, matching what the raw scan answered when the
	// parser and the scan agreed: a declaration carrying two blocks makes its
	// claim in the one nearest the declaration.
	last := documents[len(documents)-1]
	text := file.Text()
	// A JSDoc node spans from the host node's full start (or the previous
	// block's end) through `*/`, so the comment itself is the last `/** … */`
	// inside that span.
	start, end := last.Pos(), last.End()
	if start < 0 || end < start || end > len(text) {
		return ""
	}
	span := text[start:end]
	open := strings.LastIndex(span, "/**")
	if open < 0 {
		return ""
	}
	close := strings.Index(span[open+3:], "*/")
	if close < 0 {
		return ""
	}
	return span[open : open+3+close+2]
}

func throwsAnnotation(declaration *ast.Node) string {
	comment := attachedJSDocComment(declaration)
	if comment == "" {
		return ""
	}
	if exactModuleMarker(comment) {
		// A file-leading `@module` tag disambiguates the module initializer
		// trust claim from a function-level `@throws` claim on the first
		// declaration. It must never certify that declaration infallible.
		return ""
	}
	return exactThrowsAnnotation(comment)
}

func exactThrowsAnnotation(comment string) string {
	claims := exactThrowsAnnotations(comment)
	if len(claims) == 0 {
		return ""
	}
	return claims[0]
}

// exactThrowsAnnotations returns EVERY `@throws {…}` claim in one comment.
//
// Reading only the first was order-dependent in a way that reversed the verdict:
// `{never}` then `{TypeError}` trusted the binding and silently dropped the
// declared channel, while the identical pair in the opposite order refused it.
// The same two claims must not give opposite answers, and only one of the two
// orders failed closed, so the callers now see all of them and refuse a
// declaration that makes more than one distinct claim.
func exactThrowsAnnotations(comment string) []string {
	const marker = "@throws"
	var claims []string
	for offset := 0; offset < len(comment); {
		index := strings.Index(comment[offset:], marker)
		if index < 0 {
			break
		}
		index += offset
		position := index + len(marker)
		for position < len(comment) && isJSDocWhitespace(comment[position]) {
			position++
		}
		if position >= len(comment) || comment[position] != '{' {
			offset = index + len(marker)
			continue
		}
		position++
		close := strings.IndexByte(comment[position:], '}')
		if close < 0 {
			break
		}
		claims = append(claims, strings.TrimSpace(comment[position:position+close]))
		offset = position + close + 1
	}
	return claims
}

// throwsAnnotations returns every `@throws {…}` claim on ONE declaration.
//
// The block comes from `attachedJSDocComment` — the one place that answers
// which comment belongs to a declaration — and not from a second raw-trivia
// scan beside it. Two scans of the same trivia is how a claim the parser
// attaches to nothing came to be believed here in the first place.
func throwsAnnotations(declaration *ast.Node) []string {
	comment := attachedJSDocComment(declaration)
	if comment == "" {
		return nil
	}
	if exactModuleMarker(comment) {
		return nil
	}
	return exactThrowsAnnotations(comment)
}

// foreignThrowsAnnotation is the single place a `@throws` claim is admitted at a
// foreign boundary on this backend. It refuses two claims rather than believing
// them, and mirrors foreignThrowsAnnotation in poc/src/language/semantic.ts.
//
//   - A declaration carrying MORE THAN ONE distinct `@throws` makes no single
//     claim to honour.
//   - `@throws {never}` on an ASYNC or Promise-returning binding describes a
//     channel it does not cover. specification/compatibility.mdx, "Foreign
//     Boundary": "Calling an unannotated foreign runtime value MUST add the
//     checked `panic` case, because JavaScript and TypeScript may throw, REJECT,
//     or violate a declaration." An async function that fails does not throw at
//     the call — it returns and rejects later — so a marker about the call is
//     not about the failure. Believing it there erased the rejection channel,
//     while the UNTRUSTED spelling of the same binding correctly charges Panic
//     (09-foreign-calls/foreign-rejection-becomes-panic), which is what makes
//     the trusted direction the fail-open one.
//
// The marker is refused rather than silently ignored, so an author can tell a
// believed claim from a dropped one. The second return value is true when the
// claim was refused.
// carriesRejectionChannel reports whether a type carries a DEFERRED failure
// channel — a rejection.
//
// Separate from the `async` flag on foreignPolicy, deliberately: that flag drives
// lowering and its answer is part of the emitted program, while this predicate
// only decides whether a `@throws {never}` claim can be honoured, so it may be
// strictly wider without moving an emitted byte. The width that matters is a
// UNION with a Promise constituent (`string | Promise<string>`), which the
// narrow check answers nil for and which was fail-open.
func (a *rowAnalysis) carriesRejectionChannel(t *checker.Type) bool {
	if t == nil {
		return false
	}
	if a.checker.GetPromisedTypeOfPromise(t) != nil {
		return true
	}
	if t.IsUnion() {
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			if a.carriesRejectionChannel(part) {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) foreignThrowsAnnotation(declaration *ast.Node, async bool, boundary *ast.Node) (string, bool) {
	if declaration == nil {
		return "", false
	}
	claims := throwsAnnotations(declaration)
	distinct := make([]string, 0, len(claims))
	for _, claim := range claims {
		seen := false
		for _, kept := range distinct {
			if kept == claim {
				seen = true
				break
			}
		}
		if !seen {
			distinct = append(distinct, claim)
		}
	}
	if len(distinct) > 1 {
		if !a.foreignAnnotationReports[boundary] {
			a.foreignAnnotationReports[boundary] = true
			quoted := make([]string, 0, len(distinct))
			for _, claim := range distinct {
				quoted = append(quoted, "{"+claim+"}")
			}
			a.report(boundary, "SMITHERS1502",
				"this foreign declaration makes contradictory @throws claims ("+strings.Join(quoted, " and ")+
					"); a binding cannot both claim never and declare a channel — keep one @throws tag")
		}
		return "", true
	}
	annotation := ""
	if len(distinct) == 1 {
		annotation = distinct[0]
	}
	if isNeverThrowsAnnotation(annotation) && async {
		if !a.foreignAnnotationReports[boundary] {
			a.foreignAnnotationReports[boundary] = true
			a.report(boundary, "SMITHERS1502",
				"foreign @throws {never} cannot describe an async or Promise-returning binding: the marker removes the checked panic case for the CALL, and an async binding fails by rejecting afterwards; expose a synchronous trusted binding, or drop the marker and propagate the panic case")
		}
		return "", true
	}
	return annotation, false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) foreignPolicyForCall(call *ast.Node) (foreignPolicy, bool) {
	if call == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(call) {
		return foreignPolicy{}, false
	}
	if a.assetValueProvenance(call.AsCallExpression().Expression, make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool)) {
		// A loader result is a compiler-issued pure-data module. Calling a typed
		// value it exports is governed by that checked module contract, never by
		// the JavaScript foreign-call/panic fallback.
		return foreignPolicy{}, false
	}
	signature := a.checker.GetResolvedSignature(call)
	if signature == nil || signature.Declaration() == nil || !a.isForeignDeclaration(signature.Declaration()) {
		return foreignPolicy{}, false
	}
	// A refused module edge does NOT excuse the calls behind it.
	// specification/type-system.mdx:60 — "Every unannotated foreign call MUST add
	// the distinguished checked `panic` case, because JavaScript may throw,
	// reject, or violate a declaration" — and :56 — "An ignored Result MUST be a
	// compile error" — with specification/compatibility.mdx:22 stating the same
	// foreign-boundary rule. Neither MUST is conditioned on the trust of the
	// module edge the call arrived through, and the control proves it is not a
	// cascade: 09-foreign-calls/the-never-annotation-is-case-sensitive charges
	// SMITHERS1301 at the identical position over the identical program shape
	// with a genuine trust header, and passes on both backends. The second
	// diagnostic survives fixing the first, so suppressing it hands back one
	// error and hides another.
	returned := a.checker.GetReturnTypeOfSignature(signature)
	async := a.checker.GetPromisedTypeOfPromise(returned) != nil
	annotation, _ := a.foreignThrowsAnnotation(signature.Declaration(), a.carriesRejectionChannel(returned), call)
	callee := call.AsCallExpression().Expression
	// Two conditions, both about PROVENANCE. The third — "the checked result of
	// this call is used as a value here" — was about PLACEMENT: it existed
	// because a `Result.try(...)` wrapper had to be hoisted in front of the
	// statement, and the wrapper is an expression that stays where it was
	// written. It is retired with the postfix-`!` placement walk; see
	// DECISIONS.md §Typed failures and specification/failures.mdx §Refusal
	// Conditions. `foreignResultIsUsedAsValue` went with it.
	//
	// A same-position suppression went with it too, and that is worth recording
	// because deleting the branch without deleting the suppression opened a
	// hole. `makeCallable()("x")` charges ONE SMITHERS1507 on the reference, at
	// the outer call, because the outer call's callee is not a stable reference.
	// This backend used to charge it on the INNER call instead — through the
	// deleted "used as a value" branch, the inner call's result being read as a
	// callee — and then suppressed the outer one so the two would not double up
	// at the shared position. With the inner report gone the suppression left
	// the shape unreported entirely, MEASURED as `js [1510, 1301, 1507] / go
	// [1510, 1301]` on that program. The outer call answers the surviving
	// question on its own.
	orderUnsafe := !a.stableForeignCallee(callee) || a.foreignCalleeHasUncheckedResult(callee)
	if orderUnsafe && !a.foreignOrderReports[call] {
		a.foreignOrderReports[call] = true
		a.report(call, "SMITHERS1507", "this foreign callee is not a stable reference the compiler can read once, or its result is already an unchecked foreign value; bind the callee to a const and propagate the checked result with postfix !, or continue through an explicitly typed local adapter")
	}
	if annotation == "never" {
		return foreignPolicy{kind: "never", async: async}, true
	}
	if annotation != "" {
		symbol := a.canonical(a.checker.ResolveName(annotation, call, ast.SymbolFlagsType, false))
		if symbol != nil && a.isErrorType(a.checker.GetDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol)) {
			return foreignPolicy{kind: "declared", errorSymbol: symbol, errorName: annotation, async: async}, true
		}
		if !a.foreignAnnotationReports[call] {
			a.foreignAnnotationReports[call] = true
			a.report(call, "SMITHERS1502", "foreign @throws {"+annotation+"} has no checker-matching Error constructor value in scope; import that Error class (named, aliased, or through a namespace) or provide an adapter")
		}
	}
	return foreignPolicy{kind: "panic", async: async}, true
}

// stableForeignCallee reports whether a callee is a leaf read once at the point
// the call happens, so the foreign policy resolved for it is the policy the
// call will actually run.
//
// Type-only wrappers come from `typeOnlyWrapperOperand` (THE ONE TABLE, above).
// This walk knew the parenthesis, `as` and `<T>x` and not `satisfies`, and here
// the drift pointed the other way from the provenance walk's: a `@throws
// {never}` callee named through `satisfies` — still one leaf, still read once,
// with its trust marker intact because `satisfies` does not change the type —
// was refused SMITHERS1507 on this backend while the reference accepted it and
// ran it. An over-refusal is as much a defect as a fail-open, and the same
// table closes both directions at once.
func (a *rowAnalysis) stableForeignCallee(expression *ast.Node) bool {
	if expression == nil {
		return false
	}
	if operand := typeOnlyWrapperOperand(expression); operand != nil {
		return a.stableForeignCallee(operand)
	}
	switch expression.Kind {
	case ast.KindNonNullExpression:
		// In `.sm` this AST kind is the checked postfix propagation boundary,
		// never TypeScript's non-null assertion.
		return true
	case ast.KindIdentifier:
		return true
	case ast.KindPropertyAccessExpression:
		return a.stableForeignCallee(expression.AsPropertyAccessExpression().Expression)
	case ast.KindElementAccessExpression:
		return a.stableForeignCallee(expression.AsElementAccessExpression().Expression)
	}
	return false
}

// foreignCalleeHasUncheckedResult and stableForeignCallee are folded into ONE
// `orderUnsafe` decision at the call site above, so they must answer the same
// wrapper question. Type-only wrappers therefore come from the same
// `typeOnlyWrapperOperand` table; `!` keeps its own branch here, and its answer
// is deliberately the OPPOSITE of the one stableForeignCallee gives it, which
// is exactly why `!` may not live in a shared table.
func (a *rowAnalysis) foreignCalleeHasUncheckedResult(expression *ast.Node) bool {
	if expression == nil {
		return false
	}
	if operand := typeOnlyWrapperOperand(expression); operand != nil {
		return a.foreignCalleeHasUncheckedResult(operand)
	}
	switch expression.Kind {
	case ast.KindNonNullExpression:
		return false
	case ast.KindPropertyAccessExpression:
		return a.foreignCalleeHasUncheckedResult(expression.AsPropertyAccessExpression().Expression)
	case ast.KindElementAccessExpression:
		return a.foreignCalleeHasUncheckedResult(expression.AsElementAccessExpression().Expression)
	case ast.KindCallExpression:
		policy, foreign := a.foreignPolicyForCall(expression)
		return foreign && policy.kind != "never"
	case ast.KindIdentifier:
		symbol := a.canonical(a.checker.GetSymbolAtLocation(expression))
		declaration := variableLikeDeclaration(symbol)
		if declaration != nil && ast.IsVariableDeclaration(declaration) {
			initializer := declaration.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer
			if initializer != nil && ast.IsCallExpression(initializer) {
				policy, foreign := a.foreignPolicyForCall(initializer)
				return foreign && policy.kind != "never"
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) addForeignFailures(into rowSet, policy foreignPolicy) {
	if policy.kind == "never" {
		return
	}
	addRow(into, a.row(a.prelude.panic))
	if policy.kind == "declared" && policy.errorSymbol != nil {
		addRow(into, a.row(policy.errorSymbol))
	}
}

// forwardingParent is THE ONE TABLE, asked in the PARENT direction.
//
// A parent forwards its child's value unchanged exactly when
// typeOnlyWrapperOperand names that child as its operand — so this is literally
// the same table the operand-direction walks read, not a second table that
// happens to agree today. Three parent-direction walks each spelled their own
// `IsParenthesizedExpression || IsAsExpression || …` chain and they had drifted:
// none of them knew `satisfies`, and one did not know `<T>x` either, so
// SMITHERS1104 went silent at those spellings while its siblings fired and the
// refusal degraded to an unmapped stock-TypeScript TS2322 reported at 0:0 with
// internal lowering type names in an author-facing message.
//
// `await` is added here rather than to the shared table because it is a real
// change of value that the shared table deliberately excludes — every caller of
// THIS helper wants it, and each states so next to its own reason.
func forwardingParent(current *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if current == nil || current.Parent == nil {
		return nil
	}
	parent := current.Parent
	if typeOnlyWrapperOperand(parent) == current {
		return parent
	}
	if ast.IsAwaitExpression(parent) && parent.AsAwaitExpression().Expression == current {
		return parent
	}
	return nil
}

// callPropagatesFailure reports whether the value an invocation produces reaches
// the enclosing function's own channel, so that the callee's failures belong in
// this function's row.
//
// It is the UP direction of the same edge resultPropagationOperand walks DOWN,
// and the two must agree: the row charge and the propagation recognition are one
// fact about one program. The binding case is why. `const r = inferred("bad");
// const v = r!` reaches the channel exactly as `const v = inferred("bad")!`
// does — the reference runs both and charges both — and reading only the
// syntactic parent stopped at the VariableDeclaration. Recognizing `r!` as a
// propagation without also charging its row would have turned a refusal into a
// FAIL-OPEN: `outer(): Result<number, Calm>` would compile over a body that can
// only produce `Boom`.
func (a *rowAnalysis) callPropagatesFailure(call *ast.Node) bool {
	return a.propagatesToEnclosingChannel(call, make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool))
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) propagatesToEnclosingChannel(current *ast.Node, seen map[*ast.Symbol]bool) bool {
	for current != nil && current.Parent != nil {
		if forwarded := forwardingParent(current); forwarded != nil {
			current = forwarded
			continue
		}
		parent := current.Parent
		switch {
		case ast.IsNonNullExpression(parent) && parent.AsNonNullExpression().Expression == current:
			return true
		case ast.IsReturnStatement(parent):
			return true
		case ast.IsVariableDeclaration(parent) && parent.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer == current:
			return a.bindingPropagates(parent, seen)
		}
		return false
	}
	return false
}

// bindingPropagates asks whether any reference to a stored value carries it on
// into the enclosing function's channel.
//
// Only references in the SAME enclosing function count. A propagation inside a
// nested callback belongs to that callback's row, not to this one, and charging
// it here would be an over-refusal at a spelling nothing else refuses.
func (a *rowAnalysis) bindingPropagates(declaration *ast.Node, seen map[*ast.Symbol]bool) bool {
	name := declaration.AsVariableDeclaration().Name()
	if name == nil || !ast.IsIdentifier(name) {
		return false
	}
	symbol := a.symbolAt(name)
	if symbol == nil || seen[symbol] {
		return false
	}
	seen[symbol] = true
	owner := enclosingFunction(declaration)
	for _, reference := range a.identifierReferences(ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(declaration), symbol) {
		if reference == name || enclosingFunction(reference) != owner {
			continue
		}
		if a.propagatesToEnclosingChannel(reference, seen) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) instantiatedFailures(call *ast.Node, callee *functionRows) rowSet {
	if callee == nil || !callee.genericTemplate {
		return nil
	}
	signature := a.checker.GetResolvedSignature(call)
	if signature == nil {
		a.report(call, "SMITHERS1803", "the failure row of generic call '"+callee.name+"' cannot be resolved at this call site")
		return make(rowSet)
	}
	returned := a.checker.GetReturnTypeOfSignature(signature)
	if promised := a.checker.GetPromisedTypeOfPromise(returned); promised != nil {
		returned = promised
	}
	_, failure, ok := a.resultArguments(returned)
	if !ok || failure == nil || hasDeferredRowType(failure, make(map[*checker.Type]bool)) {
		a.report(call, "SMITHERS1803", "the failure row of generic call '"+callee.name+"' is a template over its type parameters and is still unresolved at this call site; supply concrete type arguments or wrap the call in an explicitly checked non-generic function")
		return make(rowSet)
	}
	rows, known := a.rowsOfType(failure)
	if !known {
		a.report(call, "SMITHERS1803", "the failure row of generic call '"+callee.name+"' cannot be determined soundly at this call site")
		return make(rowSet)
	}
	a.checkCallbackCoverage(call, callee, rows)
	return rows
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) nominalAncestry(symbol *ast.Symbol, seen map[*ast.Symbol]bool) rowSet {
	rows := make(rowSet)
	symbol = a.canonical(symbol)
	if symbol == nil || seen[symbol] {
		return rows
	}
	seen[symbol] = true
	addRow(rows, a.row(symbol))
	t := a.checker.GetDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol)
	if t != nil && t.Flags()&checker.TypeFlagsObject != 0 {
		for _, base := range a.checker.GetBaseTypes(t) {
			if baseSymbol := a.canonical(base.Symbol()); baseSymbol != nil {
				addRows(rows, a.nominalAncestry(baseSymbol, seen))
			}
		}
	}
	return rows
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) checkCallbackCoverage(call *ast.Node, callee *functionRows, instantiated rowSet) {
	// invocationArguments, not Arguments(): a generic tagged template hands its
	// substitutions to the tag, and Arguments() panics on one.
	for _, argument := range invocationArguments(call) {
		callback := a.byNode[argument]
		if callback == nil {
			location := argument
			if ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(location) {
				location = location.AsPropertyAccessExpression().Name()
			}
			callback = a.bySymbol[a.symbolAt(location)]
		}
		if callback == nil || !callback.explicitReturn || !callback.declaredShape.result {
			continue
		}
		uncovered := make([]string, 0)
		for key, row := range callback.declaredFailures {
			// `key.symbol` for a nominal row and nil for an ambient one, which
			// nominalAncestry answers with an empty set. A declared FAILURE row
			// is always nominal — an ambient row is only ever a requirement —
			// so the nil arm is unreachable here and is nonetheless the safe
			// answer: an unknown ancestry covers nothing.
			ancestry := a.nominalAncestry(key.symbol, make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool))
			covered := false
			for expected := range instantiated {
				if _, ok := ancestry[expected]; ok {
					covered = true
					break
				}
			}
			if !covered {
				uncovered = append(uncovered, row.name)
			}
		}
		if len(uncovered) != 0 {
			// Authored Error class names; the reference sorts the same list
			// with `compareText` (poc/src/language/semantic.ts:1355).
			sortUTF16(uncovered)
			a.report(call, "SMITHERS1806", "instantiating '"+callee.name+"' here publishes the failure row "+formatRowSet(instantiated)+", which a callback argument's declared "+strings.Join(uncovered, " | ")+" cannot produce; correct the type arguments or widen the row")
		}
	}
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Capability receiver resolution (SMITHERS2106)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

// contextReceiverKind is what a `Capability.context()` receiver identifies.
//
// specification/requirements.mdx §Context Access: "The receiver MUST identify a
// `Context` subclass strongly enough for the compiler to record its nominal
// key." `contextReceiverCapability` is that receiver.
// `contextReceiverAmbiguous` is a receiver that reaches the language's
// `Context.context` without pinning ONE key, and it is a REFUSAL, not an empty
// row: the runtime keys the lookup on the constructor the receiver evaluates
// to, so an unpinned receiver produces a checked program that reads a
// capability its row does not name. Silence there was the fail-open — measured
// on this backend, `(flag ? Db : Twin).context()` compiled `ok: true` with the
// row `Db`, a `Layer.provide` of `Db` satisfied that declared row, and the
// program panicked with `capability 'Log' was not provided`.
type contextReceiverKind int

const (
	contextReceiverNone contextReceiverKind = iota
	contextReceiverCapability
	contextReceiverAmbiguous
)

type contextReceiver struct {
	kind   contextReceiverKind
	symbol *ast.Symbol // the capability class symbol when kind is capability
}

var (
	noContextReceiver        = contextReceiver{kind: contextReceiverNone}
	ambiguousContextReceiver = contextReceiver{kind: contextReceiverAmbiguous}
)

const ambiguousContextReceiverMessage = "the receiver of context() must identify exactly one Context subclass; this receiver can evaluate to a different capability at runtime, so no nominal key can be recorded"

// valueBranches are the operands whose runtime value can BECOME this
// expression's value. A nil result means "this expression selects no operand" —
// it is a leaf as far as provenance is concerned, not an expression with zero
// branches.
//
// THE ONE TABLE. Every provenance walk over a selecting operator has to use
// this and only this. The two walks that own provenance —
// `contextReceiverOf` (which capability a `.context()` receiver names) and
// `foreignValueProvenance` (which module a value came from) — were written
// separately and drifted: the receiver walk learned `??`, `||`, `&&`, the comma
// and the assignment, and the foreign walk learned none of them. So
// `new (Untrusted ?? Other)("a")`, `(client ?? otherClient).dangerous` and
// `` (utag ?? otherTag)`x` `` were accepted with `failures: []` while the
// direct spelling of each was refused, and a raw host `Error` escaped a checked
// function through every one. SMITHERS1504, SMITHERS1506, SMITHERS1508 and the
// foreign `Panic` row were silenced at once by two characters. A copy of this
// table taught to one walk and not its sibling is exactly how that happened, so
// the sibling must CALL this and never restate it.
//
// The fold at each caller is the fail-closed direction — every operand that can
// be the value is folded in, because which one it will be is not decidable
// here.
//
// Why the syntax and not the checker type: TypeScript subtype-reduces a union
// of structurally identical constituents to its first member, so
// `(flag ? Db : Log).context()` — with `Db` and `Log` two distinct capabilities
// that happen to share a shape — has the checker type `typeof Db`, and NOTHING
// IN THE TYPE REMEMBERS `Log`. A purely type-directed resolution — which is
// what the retired `classReferenceSymbol` was, and what `resolveLayer`'s
// capability argument used until it was routed through `contextReceiverOf` —
// therefore cannot see the second arm, and records a MISATTRIBUTED row rather
// than an ambiguous one. The branches are
// still in the SYNTAX, so the syntax is asked first and the type is consulted
// only for expressions that select nothing.
//
// Its sibling is `typeOnlyWrapperOperand`, directly below — THE ONE TABLE for
// wrappers that change only the TYPE (the parenthesis, `as` including
// `as const`, `<T>x` and `satisfies`). The two answer different questions —
// "which operand can be the value" versus "is this a wrapper around the value"
// — and both were drifted apart by the same mechanism, one round apart: a copy
// taught to one walk and not to its sibling. A caller that needs both asks both.
//
// This mirrors `valueBranches` in poc/src/language/semantic.ts; the two
// backends must answer the same question the same way.
func valueBranches(expression *ast.Node) []*ast.Node {
	if expression == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if ast.IsConditionalExpression(expression) {
		conditional := expression.AsConditionalExpression()
		return []*ast.Node{conditional.WhenTrue, conditional.WhenFalse}
	}
	if ast.IsBinaryExpression(expression) {
		binary := expression.AsBinaryExpression()
		switch binary.OperatorToken.Kind {
		case ast.KindQuestionQuestionToken, ast.KindBarBarToken, ast.KindAmpersandAmpersandToken:
			return []*ast.Node{binary.Left, binary.Right}
		case ast.KindCommaToken, ast.KindEqualsToken:
			// The value of `(a, b)` and of `(c = Db)` is the right operand alone.
			return []*ast.Node{binary.Right}
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// typeOnlyWrapperOperand is the operand of a wrapper that changes only the
// TYPE, never the value.
//
// THE OTHER ONE TABLE, and the sibling of `valueBranches` directly above. The
// two answer different questions — `valueBranches` answers "which operand can
// BECOME this expression's value" (a runtime selection), this one answers "is
// this expression merely a wrapper AROUND the value" (a compile-time
// annotation) — and they were drifted apart by the same mechanism one round
// apart, so they are kept side by side and each names the other.
//
// A nil result means "this expression is not a type-level wrapper": it is a
// value as far as this table is concerned.
//
// Every walk that asks "what value is this really" has to use this and only
// this. Walks in this bridge each spelled their own
// `KindParenthesizedExpression, KindAsExpression, …` case list, and they
// drifted: when TypeScript 4.9 added `satisfies`, some learned it and some did
// not. `foreignValueProvenance` was one that did not, while
// `withoutTypeAssertions` — three functions away in the same file — did, so
// `(client satisfies T).dangerous` was accepted with zero diagnostics while
// `client.dangerous` was refused. Nine independently measured programs escaped
// through that hole on this backend, one per rule, each accepted with
// `failures: []` inside a function declared to return a plain type and each
// throwing a raw host `Error` at run time with exit code 1: SMITHERS1504 (a
// foreign constructor and a foreign tagged-template tag), SMITHERS1506 (a
// property read, an element access, an optional chain, iteration, spread and
// coercion), SMITHERS1508 (a callable handed to a higher-order call, stored
// through a mutable alias, or returned) and the foreign `Panic` row.
//
// `satisfies` is the purest laundering wrapper there is: unlike `as` it does
// not even change the expression's type, so nothing downstream can notice it
// was there. That is also why it is the one wrapper a TRUSTED foreign value
// survives — see foreignThrowsAnnotation: `as` and an explicit annotation
// replace the declaration the checker resolves and erase a `@throws {never}`
// marker, `satisfies` does not.
//
// `x as const` is an `AsExpression` too, so it rides the same entry.
//
// What is deliberately NOT in the table, and why each is left to its caller:
//
//   - `!` is this language's checked postfix propagation boundary, never
//     TypeScript's non-null assertion. It turns a checked `Result` into its
//     success value, so it is a real operation and the walks legitimately
//     disagree about it: `stableForeignCallee` accepts it outright,
//     `foreignCalleeHasUncheckedResult` stops at it, `withoutTypeAssertions`
//     must not cross it at all, and `foreignValueProvenance` walks it.
//   - `await` removes a Promise layer, which is likewise a real change of
//     value. `foreignValueProvenance` still walks it, in its own branch,
//     because the awaited value still came from wherever the promise did.
//
// A shared table may hold only what means the same thing to every caller;
// anything else stays a caller's own branch, spelled next to its own reason.
//
// This mirrors `typeOnlyWrapperOperand` in poc/src/language/semantic.ts; the
// two backends must answer the same question the same way.
func typeOnlyWrapperOperand(expression *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if expression == nil {
		return nil
	}
	switch expression.Kind {
	case ast.KindParenthesizedExpression, ast.KindAsExpression,
		ast.KindTypeAssertionExpression, ast.KindSatisfiesExpression:
		return expression.Expression()
	}
	return nil
}

// agreeOnCapability folds syntactic branch resolutions into one verdict.
//
// A branch carrying no capability evidence at all is skipped rather than
// treated as disagreement: the left operand of `flag && Db` is a boolean and
// the discarded arm of `c ?? Db` is `undefined`, and neither can be the
// receiver of a capability read. Two branches naming DIFFERENT capabilities are
// the ambiguity this rule exists to refuse.
func agreeOnCapability(parts []contextReceiver) contextReceiver {
	var found *ast.Symbol
	for _, part := range parts {
		switch part.kind {
		case contextReceiverNone:
			continue
		case contextReceiverAmbiguous:
			return ambiguousContextReceiver
		}
		if found == nil {
			found = part.symbol
			continue
		}
		if found != part.symbol {
			return ambiguousContextReceiver
		}
	}
	if found == nil {
		return noContextReceiver
	}
	return contextReceiver{kind: contextReceiverCapability, symbol: found}
}

// capabilityOfClassLike is the nominal row identity of a class-like declaration
// that extends `Context`.
func (a *rowAnalysis) capabilityOfClassLike(declaration *ast.Node) contextReceiver {
	if declaration == nil || !ast.IsClassLike(declaration) {
		return noContextReceiver
	}
	symbol := a.canonical(declaration.Symbol())
	if symbol == nil || !a.extendsContext(symbol) {
		return noContextReceiver
	}
	// A class EXPRESSION extending `Context` has a nominal key no `Layer` can
	// name, so a read through it can never be provided; that is a refusal, not
	// a row.
	if !ast.IsClassDeclaration(declaration) || declaration.Name() == nil {
		return ambiguousContextReceiver
	}
	if named := a.symbolAt(declaration.Name()); named != nil {
		symbol = named
	}
	return contextReceiver{kind: contextReceiverCapability, symbol: symbol}
}

// isContextConstructorType reports whether this type's construct signatures
// produce a `Context` instance. It is the bound of the language's own helper
// shape, `<C extends abstract new (...args: never[]) => Context>`, which names
// no single class at all.
func (a *rowAnalysis) isContextConstructorType(t *checker.Type) bool {
	if t == nil {
		return false
	}
	for _, signature := range a.checker.GetSignaturesOfType(t, checker.SignatureKindConstruct) {
		returned := a.checker.GetReturnTypeOfSignature(signature)
		if returned == nil {
			continue
		}
		if symbol := a.canonical(returned.Symbol()); symbol != nil && a.extendsContext(symbol) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) contextReceiverOfType(t *checker.Type, seen map[*checker.Type]bool) contextReceiver {
	if t == nil || seen[t] {
		return noContextReceiver
	}
	seen[t] = true
	if t.IsUnion() {
		parts := make([]contextReceiver, 0, len(t.Types()))
		anyCapability := false
		anyPlain := false
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			if part.Flags()&(checker.TypeFlagsUndefined|checker.TypeFlagsNull|checker.TypeFlagsVoid) != 0 {
				continue
			}
			resolved := a.contextReceiverOfType(part, seen)
			parts = append(parts, resolved)
			if resolved.kind == contextReceiverNone {
				anyPlain = true
			} else {
				anyCapability = true
			}
		}
		// Unlike a syntactic branch, a union constituent that is not a
		// capability IS evidence the receiver does not pin one key: it is a
		// value this receiver can actually hold.
		if anyCapability && anyPlain {
			return ambiguousContextReceiver
		}
		return agreeOnCapability(parts)
	}
	if t.IsIntersection() {
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			if a.contextReceiverOfType(part, seen).kind != contextReceiverNone {
				return ambiguousContextReceiver
			}
		}
		return noContextReceiver
	}
	if t.Flags()&checker.TypeFlagsTypeParameter != 0 {
		constraint := a.checker.GetBaseConstraintOfType(t)
		if constraint == nil || constraint == t {
			return noContextReceiver
		}
		// A type parameter bounded by `typeof Db` is still substitutable by a
		// SUBCLASS of `Db`, whose nominal key is a different one, so a bound
		// never pins the key even when it names exactly one class.
		if a.contextReceiverOfType(constraint, seen).kind != contextReceiverNone {
			return ambiguousContextReceiver
		}
		if a.isContextConstructorType(constraint) {
			return ambiguousContextReceiver
		}
		return noContextReceiver
	}
	return a.capabilityOfClassLike(classDeclarationOf(a.canonical(t.Symbol())))
}

// contextReceiverOf resolves the capability a `.context()` receiver identifies.
//
// Syntax first (see valueBranches, THE ONE TABLE), type second, and that
// ordering is load-bearing. A type assertion is asked about its OPERAND first,
// because `as`
// changes the type and never the value: `(Db as any).context()` and
// `(Db as unknown as { context(): Db }).context()` both call `Db`'s inherited
// static at runtime, and both recorded an empty row while doing it. The
// asserted type stays the fallback for an operand carrying no capability
// evidence of its own, which is what keeps `(x as typeof Db).context()` — an
// author's explicit claim about an opaque value — recording `Db` rather than
// becoming a blanket refusal of every cast.
func (a *rowAnalysis) contextReceiverOf(receiver *ast.Node, depth int) contextReceiver {
	if receiver == nil {
		return noContextReceiver
	}
	if depth > 16 {
		return ambiguousContextReceiver
	}
	if branches := valueBranches(receiver); branches != nil {
		parts := make([]contextReceiver, 0, len(branches))
		for _, branch := range branches {
			parts = append(parts, a.contextReceiverOf(branch, depth+1))
		}
		return agreeOnCapability(parts)
	}
