	if ast.IsParenthesizedExpression(receiver) {
		return a.contextReceiverOf(receiver.Expression(), depth+1)
	}
	// `super.context()` in a static method invokes the inherited static with
	// `this` bound to the CONTAINING class, never to the superclass, so the
	// checker type of `super` (`typeof Db`) is the one key that read can never
	// have. Measured: `class S2 extends Db { static read() { return
	// super.context()... } }` recorded `Db`, a `Db` layer satisfied the row, and
	// the program panicked with `capability 'S2' was not provided`. It is
	// `this.context()` spelled differently, so it resolves the same way.
	if receiver.Kind == ast.KindSuperKeyword {
		if owner := enclosingClassLike(receiver); owner != nil {
			return a.capabilityOfClassLike(owner)
		}
		return ambiguousContextReceiver
	}
	// These are `typeOnlyWrapperOperand`'s entries (minus the parenthesis, taken
	// above) spelled out DELIBERATELY, and the one place a copy is correct.
	// This walk does not merely pass through: an operand carrying no capability
	// evidence falls through to the ASSERTED type, which is what keeps
	// `(x as typeof Db).context()` — an author's explicit claim about an opaque
	// value — recording `Db` rather than becoming a blanket refusal of every
	// cast. Folding it into the table would silently delete that fallthrough.
	// A NEW ENTRY IN THE TABLE MUST BE ADDED HERE TOO.
	switch receiver.Kind {
	case ast.KindAsExpression, ast.KindTypeAssertionExpression,
		ast.KindSatisfiesExpression, ast.KindNonNullExpression:
		if operand := a.contextReceiverOf(receiver.Expression(), depth+1); operand.kind != contextReceiverNone {
			return operand
		}
	}
	if ast.IsIdentifier(receiver) {
		// A `const` binding IS its initializer, and the initializer still spells
		// the branches the binding's declared type has already reduced away:
		// `const c = flag ? Db : Twin` has the checker type `typeof Db` when the
		// two capabilities share a shape, so reading the binding's type alone
		// recorded `Db` for a receiver that is `Twin` half the time.
		if initializer := a.constantInitializer(receiver); initializer != nil {
			if bound := a.contextReceiverOf(initializer, depth+1); bound.kind != contextReceiverNone {
				return bound
			}
		}
	}
	if fromType := a.contextReceiverOfType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(receiver), make(map[*checker.Type]bool)); fromType.kind != contextReceiverNone {
		return fromType
	}
	if ast.IsIdentifier(receiver) {
		if declaration := classDeclarationOf(a.symbolAt(receiver)); declaration != nil {
			return a.capabilityOfClassLike(declaration)
		}
	}
	return noContextReceiver
}

// enclosingClassLike is the class declaration or expression a node is lexically
// inside, if any.
func enclosingClassLike(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	for current := node.Parent; current != nil; current = current.Parent {
		if ast.IsClassLike(current) {
			return current
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// constantInitializer is the initializer of a `const` binding this identifier
// names. `let` is excluded: a reassignment makes the initializer no evidence at
// all about the value at the read.
func (a *rowAnalysis) constantInitializer(reference *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	return constantInitializerOfSymbol(a.symbolAt(reference))
}

// constantInitializerOfSymbol is the `const`-only binding step itself, shared
// with `preludeSymbols` (which has a checker but no rowAnalysis) so the two
// walks that need it cannot drift into two different readings of "a binding IS
// its initializer".
func constantInitializerOfSymbol(symbol *ast.Symbol) *ast.Node {
	if symbol == nil || len(symbol.Declarations) != 1 {
		return nil
	}
	declaration := symbol.Declarations[0]
	if declaration == nil || !ast.IsVariableDeclaration(declaration) {
		return nil
	}
	initializer := declaration.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer
	if initializer == nil {
		return nil
	}
	list := declaration.Parent
	if list == nil || !ast.IsVariableDeclarationList(list) || list.Flags&ast.NodeFlagsConst == 0 {
		return nil
	}
	return initializer
}

// contextRequirement classifies a call as a capability read.
//
// The second result is false for "not a context call". It is deliberately NOT
// the answer for a context call whose receiver is illegal — that silence was
// the fail-open this rule closes, and it is now `contextReceiverAmbiguous`.
func (a *rowAnalysis) contextRequirement(node *ast.Node) (contextReceiver, bool) {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
		return noContextReceiver, false
	}
	call := node.AsCallExpression()
	selection, selected := a.prelude.calleeSelection(node)
	if !selected || selection.text != "context" ||
		call.Arguments == nil || len(call.Arguments.Nodes) != 0 {
		return noContextReceiver, false
	}
	if resolved := a.contextReceiverOf(selection.receiver, 0); resolved.kind != contextReceiverNone {
		return resolved, true
	}
	// The receiver named nothing, but the member itself may still resolve to
	// the language's own `context` (a bare type parameter with no usable bound
	// gets here). That is a context call with no recordable key: refuse it.
	if a.prelude.isPreludeContextMember(selection) {
		return ambiguousContextReceiver, true
	}
	return noContextReceiver, false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) resolveFunctionReference(expression *ast.Node) *functionRows {
	if expression == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if fn := a.byNode[expression]; fn != nil {
		return fn
	}
	location := expression
	if ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(expression) {
		location = expression.AsPropertyAccessExpression().Name()
	}
	// `{ transform }` carries the same function value `{ transform: transform }`
	// does, but the identifier's own symbol is the object literal's PROPERTY, not
	// the local it abbreviates. Asking for the shorthand's value symbol is what
	// makes the two spellings resolve to one function.
	if location.Parent != nil && location.Parent.Kind == ast.KindShorthandPropertyAssignment &&
		location.Parent.Name() == location {
		if value := a.canonical(a.checker.GetShorthandAssignmentValueSymbol(location.Parent)); value != nil {
			if fn := a.bySymbol[value]; fn != nil {
				return fn
			}
		}
	}
	if fn := a.bySymbol[a.symbolAt(location)]; fn != nil {
		return fn
	}
	// Symbol identity alone is ONE HOP deep: it answers only when the
	// expression's symbol IS a checked function's declaration symbol, so
	// `hof(fallible)` was caught and `const alias = fallible; hof(alias)` was
	// not. Everything downstream fails OPEN when this declines — no callback is
	// found, so no Result contract is required (SMITHERS1303), no async
	// ownership is proven (SMITHERS1404), no requirement is charged, and no
	// Result.try boundary row is published. Measured on this backend: the
	// SMITHERS1303 canon written through one alias checked clean, ran to exit 0,
	// and the host observed the lifted Result as its plain success value — the
	// declared failure disappeared. Six spellings did it (a `const` alias, an
	// object property, an array element, a ternary, `.bind()`, and a
	// destructured property).
	//
	// So the checker is asked second, exactly as this function's sibling
	// resolveCallee already asks it for a CALLEE: a function value's type carries
	// call signatures, and a signature carries the declaration it came from. That
	// sees through every indirection TypeScript itself sees through, which is the
	// only way the two resolvers can agree on what `const alias = f; alias()` and
	// `hof(alias)` name.
	//
	// A value whose type offers SEVERAL distinct checked declarations (a union of
	// two different local functions, or an overload set) resolves to none of them
	// here — one row cannot stand for two — and the callers that must not accept
	// an unresolved callable say so themselves (SMITHERS2103 for Layer.provide).
	if carried := a.signatureFunctions(expression); len(carried) == 1 {
		return carried[0]
	}
	return nil
}

// signatureFunctions is every checked function this expression's call
// signatures were declared by.
//
// A MUTABLE binding is declined outright rather than read through its type. Two
// function values with the same shape have the same TYPE, so control-flow
// narrowing cannot separate them and the binding keeps the type it was
// initialized with: `let cb = usesLog; cb = usesDb; hof(cb)` reports the type of
// `usesLog`, which would charge the caller `Log` — a capability the program
// never reads — while dropping the `Db` it does. A WRONG row is worse than no
// row, and it is the same reason constantInitializer reads only `const` and
// SMITHERS1508 refuses a mutable foreign alias: a reassignment makes the
// initializer no evidence at all about the value at the use. The remaining
// mutable-binding spelling is therefore still fail-open here, deliberately, and
// belongs to the same class collectLayerBindings answers on the layer side.
func (a *rowAnalysis) signatureFunctions(expression *ast.Node) []*functionRows {
	if a.namesMutableBinding(expression) {
		return nil
	}
	found := make([]*functionRows, 0, 1)
	seen := make(map[*functionRows]bool)
	for _, signature := range a.checker.GetSignaturesOfType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(expression), checker.SignatureKindCall) {
		declaration := signature.Declaration()
		if declaration == nil {
			continue
		}
		if fn := a.byNode[declaration]; fn != nil && !seen[fn] {
			seen[fn] = true
			found = append(found, fn)
		}
	}
	return found
}

// namesMutableBinding reports whether this expression names a `let`/`var`
// binding, directly or through grouping.
func (a *rowAnalysis) namesMutableBinding(expression *ast.Node) bool {
	if expression == nil {
		return false
	}
	bare := ast.SkipParentheses(expression)
	if bare == nil || !ast.IsIdentifier(bare) {
		return false
	}
	for _, declaration := range symbolDeclarations(a.canonical(a.referencedValueSymbol(bare))) {
		if ast.IsVariableDeclaration(declaration) && declaration.Parent != nil &&
			ast.IsVariableDeclarationList(declaration.Parent) && !ast.IsVarConst(declaration) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// accessDirection reports whether a member selection reads its property, writes
// it, or both. The distinction matters because a get/set pair is two separate
// declarations with two separate rows: `box.mark = 1` executes only the setter.
func accessDirection(access *ast.Node) (read bool, write bool) {
	parent := access.Parent
	if parent == nil {
		return true, false
	}
	if ast.IsBinaryExpression(parent) && parent.AsBinaryExpression().Left == access {
		operator := parent.AsBinaryExpression().OperatorToken.Kind
		if operator == ast.KindEqualsToken {
			return false, true
		}
		if operator >= ast.KindFirstAssignment && operator <= ast.KindLastAssignment {
			return true, true
		}
	}
	if ast.IsPrefixUnaryExpression(parent) &&
		(parent.AsPrefixUnaryExpression().Operator == ast.KindPlusPlusToken ||
			parent.AsPrefixUnaryExpression().Operator == ast.KindMinusMinusToken) {
		return true, true
	}
	if ast.IsPostfixUnaryExpression(parent) {
		return true, true
	}
	if ast.IsDeleteExpression(parent) {
		return false, false
	}
	return true, false
}

// accessorInvocations returns the project accessors an ordinary property or
// element access invokes.
//
// Reading `box.size` CALLS the getter and writing `box.first = 1` CALLS the
// setter. Neither spelling can turn the accessor into a value — there is no
// syntax that names it without running it — so both are ordinary calls, and
// specification/requirements.mdx §Inference governs them: "Calling a function
// with unsatisfied requirements MUST add those capabilities to the caller's `R`
// row. ... Requirement inference MUST be transitive through ordinary calls."
// Without this edge an accessor's requirements vanished at every read on this
// backend too; SMITHERS1802 refused a cross-module get-only accessor read and
// nothing refused a same-module one, a setter, or a get/set pair.
//
// The accessor's FAILURES are deliberately not charged: a getter that yields
// `Result<A, E>` hands the reader a Result *value*, which must-consume and
// postfix propagation already govern.
func (a *rowAnalysis) accessorInvocations(node *ast.Node) []*functionRows {
	selection, selected := a.prelude.selectMember(node)
	if !selected {
		return nil
	}
	symbol := a.prelude.memberSymbol(selection)
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil
	}
	read, write := accessDirection(node)
	var invoked []*functionRows
	for _, declaration := range symbolDeclarations(symbol) {
		wanted := false
		switch {
		case ast.IsGetAccessorDeclaration(declaration):
			wanted = read
		case ast.IsSetAccessorDeclaration(declaration):
			wanted = write
		}
		if !wanted {
			continue
		}
		if accessor := a.byNode[declaration]; accessor != nil {
			invoked = append(invoked, accessor)
		}
	}
	return invoked
}

// destructuredAccessorInvocations returns the project getters an object
// destructuring pattern invokes. `const { size } = box` reads `size` exactly as
// `box.size` does. Only getters are reachable this way; a binding pattern never
// writes.
func (a *rowAnalysis) destructuredAccessorInvocations(element *ast.Node) []*functionRows {
	pattern := element.Parent
	if pattern == nil || !ast.IsObjectBindingPattern(pattern) {
		return nil
	}
	binding := element.AsBindingElement()
	// `const { ...rest } = box` names no property and reads EVERY remaining own
	// enumerable one, running each of their getters — the enumeration protocol,
	// in the declaration spelling implicitInvocationProtocol deliberately leaves
	// to this neighbouring rule.
	if binding.DotDotDotToken != nil {
		return a.ownEnumerableAccessors(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(pattern))
	}
	name := binding.PropertyName
	if name == nil {
		name = binding.Name()
	}
	if name == nil || (!ast.IsIdentifier(name) && !ast.IsStringLiteralLike(name)) {
		return nil
	}
	symbol := a.checker.GetPropertyOfType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(pattern), name.Text())
	var invoked []*functionRows
	for _, declaration := range symbolDeclarations(symbol) {
		if !ast.IsGetAccessorDeclaration(declaration) {
			continue
		}
		if accessor := a.byNode[declaration]; accessor != nil {
			invoked = append(invoked, accessor)
		}
	}
	return invoked
}

// The internal property names TypeScript gives a well-known-symbol member.
//
// `class It { *[Symbol.iterator]() {} }` declares a property whose escaped name
// is `__@iterator@13` — the trailing id is the symbol declaration's, so
// GetPropertyOfType(t, "__@iterator") finds NOTHING and the name has to be
// matched by prefix. The unescaped table key carries the "\xFE" internal
// sentinel instead of the two underscores, so the comparison is made on the
// ESCAPED form and both encodings answer alike.
const (
	iteratorMember      = "__@iterator"
	asyncIteratorMember = "__@asyncIterator"
	toPrimitiveMember   = "__@toPrimitive"
	hasInstanceMember   = "__@hasInstance"
)

func (a *rowAnalysis) typeMember(t *checker.Type, name string) *ast.Symbol {
	if t == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if direct := a.checker.GetPropertyOfType(t, name); direct != nil {
		return direct
	}
	if !strings.HasPrefix(name, "__@") {
		return nil
	}
	for _, property := range a.checker.GetPropertiesOfType(t) {
		if strings.HasPrefix(ast.EscapeAllInternalSymbolNames(property.Name), name+"@") {
			return property
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// memberInvocations is THE ONE TABLE for "which checked function is this
// protocol member?", for every position that INVOKES one.
//
// implicitInvocationProtocol made the POSITION axis of the coercion rule total.
// The MEMBER-RESOLUTION axis was still a list of one: the member's symbol was
// resolved to its DECLARATIONS and each looked up in byNode, and that single
// syntactic hop lands only when the declaration IS the function — the method
// shorthand and the accessor, and nothing else. Every other spelling puts a
// PropertyAssignment, ShorthandPropertyAssignment or PropertyDeclaration in
// between, so `{ valueOf: () => Db.context().read().length }`,
// `{ valueOf: function () {} }`, `{ valueOf: impl }`, `{ valueOf }`,
// `class C { valueOf = () => … }`, `{ ["valueOf"]: () => … }` and a member on a
// nested property all published `requirements: []` for a function that reads a
// capability, compiled clean, and panicked at run time. Measured on this
// backend before this change: 86 of 156 runnable spelling x member cells were
// fail-open, and the runtime oracle reached the member in every one of them.
//
// The checker is asked SECOND, which is the move resolveFunctionReference and
// signatureFunctions already make here: a function value's type carries call
// signatures, a signature carries the declaration it came from, and that sees
// through every indirection TypeScript itself sees through. A spelling is now
// covered BECAUSE TypeScript resolves it, not because somebody listed it.
//
// The declaration lookup is kept FIRST rather than replaced, and the order is
// load-bearing: it is the only one that answers for a GET ACCESSOR, whose
// symbol type is the getter's RETURN type, so the checker's answer there is the
// function the getter hands BACK. Both really run — `Get(obj, "valueOf")` then
// `Call` — so charging both is right, and it is what closes the
// inferred-return-type getter for free. Putting the checker path in front
// silently drops the accessor half.
//
// Only positions that INVOKE the member may use this. A property READ does not
// call what it reads (`const m = obj.valueOf` is pinned as a negative), which is
// why accessorInvocations keeps its own, deliberately narrower, getter-only
// lookup and is NOT folded in here.
func (a *rowAnalysis) memberInvocations(t *checker.Type, member string, location *ast.Node) []*functionRows {
	symbol := a.typeMember(t, member)
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil
	}
	var invoked []*functionRows
	add := func(declaration *ast.Node) {
		if declaration == nil {
			return
		}
		fn := a.byNode[declaration]
		if fn == nil {
			return
		}
		for _, already := range invoked {
			if already == fn {
				return
			}
		}
		invoked = append(invoked, fn)
	}
	for _, declaration := range symbolDeclarations(symbol) {
		add(declaration)
	}
	if location != nil {
		for _, signature := range a.checker.GetSignaturesOfType(
			a.checker.GetTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(symbol, location), checker.SignatureKindCall,
		) {
			add(signature.Declaration())
		}
	}
	return invoked
}

// coercesWithStringHint names the four coercion positions that ask for a
// STRING; every other asks for a number, or asks with no hint at all, which
// ToPrimitive treats as a number.
//
// The default is deliberately "not a string", so a coercion position nobody
// classified here charges `valueOf` as well and fails CLOSED. Totality over the
// grammar is implicitInvocationProtocol's job and is not duplicated here — this
// only refines a position that predicate has already called a coercion.
func coercesWithStringHint(node *ast.Node) bool {
	parent := node.Parent
	if parent == nil {
		return false
	}
	switch parent.Kind {
	// `{ [E]: v }`, `o[E]` and `E in o` all run ToPropertyKey, a string hint.
	case ast.KindTemplateSpan, ast.KindComputedPropertyName:
		return true
	case ast.KindElementAccessExpression:
		return parent.AsElementAccessExpression().ArgumentExpression == node
	case ast.KindBinaryExpression:
		binary := parent.AsBinaryExpression()
		return binary.OperatorToken.Kind == ast.KindInKeyword && binary.Left == node
	}
	return false
}

// primitiveCoercionFlags is the set OrdinaryToPrimitive accepts as an answer.
var primitiveCoercionFlags = checker.TypeFlagsStringLike | checker.TypeFlagsNumberLike |
	checker.TypeFlagsBigIntLike | checker.TypeFlagsBooleanLike | checker.TypeFlagsESSymbolLike |
	checker.TypeFlagsUndefined | checker.TypeFlagsNull

// isPrimitiveReturn answers false for `any`, `unknown`, an object type and a
// type variable, which is the CLOSED answer: an unclassifiable return does not
// stop the walk, so the next member is charged too.
func isPrimitiveReturn(t *checker.Type) bool {
	if t == nil {
		return false
	}
	if t.IsUnion() {
		for _, part := range t.Types() {
			if !isPrimitiveReturn(part) {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	return t.Flags()&primitiveCoercionFlags != 0
}

// coercionStopsAt answers whether OrdinaryToPrimitive STOPS at this member or
// falls through to the next.
//
// It stops as soon as one returns a primitive. That is not a detail: it is the
// whole reason the walk cannot be shortened to "the member the hint prefers".
// `Object.prototype.valueOf` returns the object ITSELF, so a number hint over an
// object with no own `valueOf` always falls through and really does run
// `toString` — measured: `+obj` on a `toString`-only object read the capability
// and panicked. `Object.prototype.toString` returns a string, so a string hint
// normally stops there and `valueOf` really is unreachable — but a project may
// declare `toString(): object`, which TypeScript accepts, and then a string hint
// falls through into `valueOf` — also measured, also a panic. Asking the
// DECLARED return type is what keeps both of those right at once, and the
// member-absent rule below is what makes a plain object behave like
// `Object.prototype`.
func (a *rowAnalysis) coercionStopsAt(t *checker.Type, member string, location *ast.Node) bool {
	symbol := a.typeMember(t, member)
	if symbol == nil {
		return member == "toString"
	}
	signatures := a.checker.GetSignaturesOfType(a.checker.GetTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(symbol, location), checker.SignatureKindCall)
	if len(signatures) == 0 {
		return false
	}
	for _, signature := range signatures {
		if !isPrimitiveReturn(a.checker.GetReturnTypeOfSignature(signature)) {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// ownEnumerableAccessors is the checked getters an ENUMERATION of a value's own
// properties runs.
//
// `{ ...box }`, `({ ...rest } = box)` and `const { ...rest } = box` copy every
// OWN ENUMERABLE property, which runs each of their getters — and only those. A
// getter declared in a CLASS body lives on the prototype, is not an own
// property, and is deliberately NOT charged: measured, `{ ...new C() }` on a
// class with `get a()` produced `{}` and never called the getter, while the
// object-literal spelling called it. Charging the class spelling would refuse a
// program that runs.
func (a *rowAnalysis) ownEnumerableAccessors(t *checker.Type) []*functionRows {
	if t == nil {
		return nil
	}
	var invoked []*functionRows
	for _, property := range a.checker.GetPropertiesOfType(t) {
		for _, declaration := range symbolDeclarations(property) {
			if !ast.IsGetAccessorDeclaration(declaration) {
				continue
			}
			if declaration.Parent == nil || !ast.IsObjectLiteralExpression(declaration.Parent) {
				continue
			}
			if accessor := a.byNode[declaration]; accessor != nil {
				invoked = append(invoked, accessor)
			}
		}
	}
	return invoked
}

// classDecorators is every decorator a CLASS DEFINITION evaluates: its own, its
// members', and its members' parameters'.
func classDecorators(declaration *ast.Node) []*ast.Node {
	found := make([]*ast.Node, 0)
	found = append(found, declaration.Decorators()...)
	for _, member := range declaration.Members() {
		found = append(found, member.Decorators()...)
		switch member.Kind {
		case ast.KindMethodDeclaration, ast.KindConstructor:
			for _, parameter := range member.Parameters() {
				found = append(found, parameter.Decorators()...)
			}
		}
	}
	return found
}

// implicitInvocations is the checked project functions an IMPLICIT protocol
// invocation runs.
//
// `[...it]`, `for (const x of it)`, `const [a] = it`, `` `${obj}` ``,
// `"v " + obj`, `String(obj)`, `JSON.stringify(obj)`, `await thenable`,
// `yield* it` and `@deco` each CALL a function the program never names, exactly
// as reading `box.size` calls its getter — and specification/requirements.mdx
// §Inference governs them for the same reason accessorInvocations exists:
// "Requirement inference MUST be transitive through ordinary calls." Without
// this edge those spellings published `requirements: []` for a function that
// reads a capability, checked `ok: true` on BOTH backends, and panicked at run
// time with `capability 'Db' was not provided`.
//
// Only REQUIREMENTS are charged, the same channel accessorInvocations charges
// and for the same reason: a protocol method cannot legally carry a failure row
// here — a Result-returning one is SMITHERS1105 and an inferred-fallible one is
// SMITHERS1101 — so there is no failure to travel, and inventing one would put a
// Result where the protocol's own contract has none.
//
// The lookup is self-limiting: it resolves a member to its DECLARATIONS and
// keeps only those the project checked, so `for (const x of [1, 2])` and
// `` `${"s"}` `` find lib.es*.d.ts declarations, match nothing, and cost
// nothing.
//
// The COERCION and ENUMERATION positions are not enumerated here: this half
// asks implicitInvocationProtocol, the same predicate the foreign half asks, and
// branches on its answer. It used to keep its own two-entry list — a template
// span and binary `+` — while the predicate beside it was already total over the
// grammar, so `-`, `~`, `++`, every arithmetic, relational, bitwise and compound
// assignment operator, `==`/`!=`, a computed property key, `o[k]`, `k in o`,
// `Number`/`Math`, an object spread and `instanceof` were all fail-open. Two
// tables for one question is the defect this file's valueBranches comment
// already records in its own words; there is only one here now.
//
// This is the AUTHORED half of the question implicitInvocationProtocol answers
// for FOREIGN values in hostrules.go: that one asks whether a position runs an
// arbitrary method on an untrusted value and charges the panic case; this one
// asks which CHECKED function that position runs and charges its row. It mirrors
// implicitInvocations in poc/src/language/semantic.ts.
func (a *rowAnalysis) implicitInvocations(node *ast.Node) []*functionRows {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	invoked := make([]*functionRows, 0)
	charge := func(expression *ast.Node, member string) {
		if expression == nil {
			return
		}
		// Parentheses and type assertions are stripped for the same reason
		// checkImplicitForeignInvocation strips them, in the same words:
		// `(obj as unknown as number) - 1` still runs `obj.valueOf()` at run
		// time, so asking the ASSERTION's type would let an author launder the
		// row with the same `as` the FOREIGN half of this protocol already
		// refuses to follow. `!` is deliberately not stripped: in `.sm` it is
		// the checked propagation boundary and it really does change the value.
		value := withoutTypeAssertions(expression)
		// Which checked function the member IS is memberInvocations' one
		// question; this stays three lines and delegates, so no position can
		// answer it differently from any other.
		invoked = append(invoked, a.memberInvocations(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(value), member, value)...)
	}
	// Coercion to a primitive runs `Symbol.toPrimitive`, `valueOf` and
	// `toString` — the three members coercionProtocol already names — and it
	// runs them in ECMAScript's own OrdinaryToPrimitive order, stopping at the
	// first that yields a primitive. `valueOf` was in that list and had never
	// been charged anywhere, so every NUMBER-hint position published
	// `requirements: []` for a function that reads a capability: `+obj` checked
	// `ok: true` on BOTH backends and panicked with `capability 'Db' was not
	// provided`.
	//
	// The walk is modelled rather than flattened to "charge all three" because
	// flattening REFUSES programs that run: an object whose only
	// capability-reading member is `valueOf`, interpolated as `` `${obj}` ``,
	// prints `[object Object]` and never reads the capability. It is not
	// shortened to "the member the hint prefers" either, because the hint's
	// first choice does not always answer; coercionStopsAt carries both
	// measurements.
	coerce := func(expression *ast.Node, stringHint bool) {
		if expression == nil {
			return
		}
		value := withoutTypeAssertions(expression)
		valueType := a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(value)
		// A value that has `Symbol.toPrimitive` runs THAT and nothing else — the
		// exotic path never consults `valueOf` or `toString` at all.
		if a.typeMember(valueType, toPrimitiveMember) != nil {
			charge(expression, toPrimitiveMember)
			return
		}
		order := []string{"valueOf", "toString"}
		if stringHint {
			order = []string{"toString", "valueOf"}
		}
		for _, member := range order {
			charge(expression, member)
			if a.coercionStopsAt(valueType, member, value) {
				return
			}
		}
	}
	switch node.Kind {
	// --- iteration protocol ------------------------------------------------
	case ast.KindSpreadElement:
		charge(node.AsSpreadElement().Expression, iteratorMember)
	case ast.KindForOfStatement:
		statement := node.AsForInOrOfStatement()
		if statement.AwaitModifier != nil {
			charge(statement.Expression, asyncIteratorMember)
		}
		charge(statement.Expression, iteratorMember)
	case ast.KindYieldExpression:
		yielded := node.AsYieldExpression()
		if yielded.AsteriskToken != nil {
			charge(yielded.Expression, iteratorMember)
		}
	case ast.KindVariableDeclaration:
		declaration := node.AsVariableDeclaration()
		if declaration.Name() != nil && ast.IsArrayBindingPattern(declaration.Name()) {
			charge(declaration.Initializer, iteratorMember)
		}
	case ast.KindParameter:
		parameter := node.AsParameterDeclaration()
		if parameter.Name() != nil && ast.IsArrayBindingPattern(parameter.Name()) {
			charge(parameter.Initializer, iteratorMember)
		}
	case ast.KindAwaitExpression:
		charge(node.AsAwaitExpression().Expression, "then")
	case ast.KindBinaryExpression:
		binary := node.AsBinaryExpression()
		if binary.OperatorToken.Kind == ast.KindEqualsToken && ast.IsArrayLiteralExpression(binary.Left) {
			// `[a] = it` — destructuring ASSIGNMENT, the expression spelling of
			// the array binding pattern above.
			charge(binary.Right, iteratorMember)
		}
	case ast.KindCallExpression:
		// `JSON.stringify(obj)` runs `obj.toJSON()` when the value declares one
		// and coerces otherwise; `String(x)` is the function spelling of a
		// template substitution and `Number(x)` the function spelling of `+x`;
		// every `Math` method coerces every argument it is handed to a number.
		// All four roots are matched by ambient identity, the same way
		// `Date["now"]()` and `Reflect.get` are, so a local binding spelled
		// `Number` is an ordinary call. They are here and not in the position
		// table because there is no operator in the source for that table to
		// classify — the coercion is inside the callee's own contract.
		arguments := node.Arguments()
		if len(arguments) == 0 {
			break
		}
		selection, selected := a.prelude.calleeSelection(node)
		if selected && selection.text == "stringify" &&
			selection.receiver != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(selection.receiver) &&
			selection.receiver.Text() == "JSON" && a.isAmbientGlobalReference(selection.receiver) {
			charge(arguments[0], "toJSON")
			coerce(arguments[0], true)
		}
		if selected && selection.receiver != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(selection.receiver) &&
			selection.receiver.Text() == "Math" && a.isAmbientGlobalReference(selection.receiver) {
			for _, argument := range arguments {
				coerce(argument, false)
			}
		}
		callee := ast.SkipParentheses(node.AsCallExpression().Expression)
		if callee != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(callee) && a.isAmbientGlobalReference(callee) {
			switch callee.Text() {
			case "String":
				coerce(arguments[0], true)
			case "Number":
				coerce(arguments[0], false)
			}
		}
	}
	// THE ONE TABLE. implicitInvocationProtocol already answers "does this
	// POSITION run an arbitrary method on the value it holds", total over the
	// grammar, for the FOREIGN half of exactly this question — and its doc
	// comment says why it must stay one predicate: the enumerated-call-site
	// shape is how this class keeps reopening. The authored half kept its own
	// two-entry list (a template span and binary `+`) and so was NOT total: `+`,
	// `-`, `~`, `++`/`--`, every arithmetic, relational, bitwise and compound
	// assignment operator, `==`/`!=`, a computed property key, `o[k]` and
	// `k in o` all published `requirements: []` for a function that reads a
	// capability, checked `ok: true`, and panicked at run time. Asking the same
	// predicate makes the answer total by construction, and the two halves can
	// no longer disagree about what a coercion position is — the
	// tagged-template carve-out is the table's, not a second copy of it.
	//
	// The ITERATION answer is deliberately not consumed here: `for await`
	// reaches `Symbol.asyncIterator` and falls back to `Symbol.iterator`, and
	// the declaration spellings (`const [a] = it`, a destructured parameter)
	// have no expression in an iteration POSITION to ask about, so those stay
	// with the node-kind cases above that charge the child.
	if ast.IsExpression(node) {
		switch implicitInvocationProtocol(node) {
		case coercionProtocol:
			// `x instanceof E` is in the position table because it INVOKES a
			// member of `E` with no call expression to see — but the member is
			// `Symbol.hasInstance`, not the coercion walk. Running the walk here
			// charged a static `toString` that `instanceof` never calls, which
			// would have refused a program that runs: measured on the reference,
			// a class with a capability-reading `static toString()` drew
			// SMITHERS2102 at `x instanceof Foo`. The foreign half needs only
			// "some member runs"; the authored half has to name the right one.
			if parent := node.Parent; parent != nil && ast.IsBinaryExpression(parent) &&
				parent.AsBinaryExpression().OperatorToken.Kind == ast.KindInstanceOfKeyword {
				charge(node, hasInstanceMember)
			} else {
				coerce(node, coercesWithStringHint(node))
			}
		// `{ ...box }` and `({ ...rest } = box)` run every own enumerable
		// getter, with no property access in the source for accessorInvocations
		// to see. Measured: `const copy = { ...box }` where `box`'s getter reads
		// a capability published `requirements: []`, checked `ok: true`, and
		// panicked.
		case enumerationProtocol:
			invoked = append(invoked, a.ownEnumerableAccessors(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(withoutTypeAssertions(node)))...)
		}
	}
	// Every decorator in a class runs when the CLASS DEFINITION is evaluated —
	// not when the decorated member is called and not when the class is
	// constructed — so they are charged at the class node, in whatever scope the
	// class statement itself sits. Charging them at the decorator node instead
	// puts a METHOD decorator inside the decorated method's own scope, where
	// nothing ever runs it: `@deco` over a capability-reading `deco` then
	// published the row on `deco` alone, everything else came out empty, and the
	// program panicked at import time.
	if ast.IsClassLike(node) {
		for _, decorator := range classDecorators(node) {
			expression := decorator.AsDecorator().Expression
			// A decorator FACTORY (`@deco("x")`) is two invocations: the factory
			// call, which is an ordinary call expression the graph already sees,
			// and the function it returns, which no declaration in the program
			// names — that half stays with the returned/stored-closure limitation
			// recorded for every other form.
			if expression == nil || ast.IsCallExpression(expression) {
				continue
			}
			for _, signature := range a.checker.GetSignaturesOfType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(expression), checker.SignatureKindCall) {
				if declaration := signature.Declaration(); declaration != nil {
					if fn := a.byNode[declaration]; fn != nil {
						invoked = append(invoked, fn)
					}
				}
			}
		}
	}
	return invoked
}

// resolveLayer is the capability closure a layer EXPRESSION provides, and
// whether that closure is completely known.
//
// The `bool` is the fail-closed half: `false` means "this POC cannot see what
// this expression provides", and its caller answers the blunt SMITHERS2104
// rather than the precise SMITHERS2101 that names a missing capability. So an
// expression this walk fails to recognise is not merely unhelpful — it turns a
// diagnostic that names the missing row into one that names nothing.
//
// THIS WALK MUST USE `typeOnlyWrapperOperand` AND NEVER RESTATE IT. It carried
// its own one-entry chain — `ast.IsParenthesizedExpression` alone — and so knew
// the parenthesis but not `as` (TypeScript 1.0), not `<T>x`, and not
// `satisfies` (TypeScript 4.9). That is the same drift `typeOnlyWrapperOperand`
// and `valueBranches` each record above, one walk at a time. Measured on this
// backend before the change: `Layer.provide(Layer.succeed(Db, db) satisfies
// Layer<typeof Db>, body)` was refused SMITHERS2104 while the identical program
// without the wrapper checked `ok: true` and RAN — and compiling both with
// `--out-dir` produced BYTE-IDENTICAL emitted modules, because a type-only
// wrapper is erased at emit. The refused program and the accepted program were
// the same program. `as`, `<T>x`, `as unknown as T` and every combination were
// refused identically at ten distinct layer positions.
//
// Why the SYNTAX and not the checker type. `Layer.succeed(Cfg, cfg) as unknown
// as Layer<typeof Db>` has the checker type `Layer<typeof Db>`, but the value
// the runtime registers is keyed on `Cfg`. Reading the type would certify a
// `Db` that is never provided; stripping the wrapper and resolving the call
// underneath answers `{Cfg}` and lets the caller report the missing `Db` with
// the precise SMITHERS2101. Measured: with this walk, that program is refused
// SMITHERS2101 naming `Db`, and `Layer.succeed(Db, db) as any` — where the type
// is gone entirely — is accepted and runs.
//
// `!` is deliberately NOT in the table (see typeOnlyWrapperOperand): in this
// language it is checked `Result` propagation, a real operation, so a layer
// under it stays on the fail-closed SMITHERS2104 path and draws SMITHERS1207 in
// its own right.
//
// The `const`-only rule this walk depends on is ORTHOGONAL and must survive:
// `a.layerBindings` is populated by `collectLayerBindings`, which records only
// `const` bindings because a reassigned `let` layer certified a `Layer.provide`
// as complete and then panicked at run time. Looking through a type-only
// wrapper says nothing about whether the binding under it can be reassigned, so
// a wrapped mutable layer is still opaque.
//
// This mirrors `resolveLayerExpression` in poc/src/language/semantic.ts; the
// two backends must answer the same question the same way.
func (a *rowAnalysis) resolveLayer(expression *ast.Node, seen map[*ast.Symbol]bool) (rowSet, bool) {
	provided := make(rowSet)
	if expression == nil {
		return provided, false
	}
	if operand := typeOnlyWrapperOperand(expression); operand != nil {
		return a.resolveLayer(operand, seen)
	}
	if ast.IsIdentifier(expression) {
		symbol := a.symbolAt(expression)
		if symbol == nil || seen[symbol] {
			return provided, false
		}
		initializer := a.layerBindings[symbol]
		if initializer == nil {
			return provided, false
		}
		seen[symbol] = true
		return a.resolveLayer(initializer, seen)
	}
	if !ast.IsCallExpression(expression) {
		return provided, false
	}
	call := expression.AsCallExpression()
	if a.prelude.layerCall(expression, "succeed") {
		if call.Arguments == nil || len(call.Arguments.Nodes) == 0 {
			return provided, false
		}
		// The capability argument is asked exactly the question
		// `contextReceiverOf` already answers — "which ONE `Context` class does
		// this expression evaluate to?" — so it is answered by that function
		// rather than by a second walk that agrees with it today. The runtime
		// registers a layer under the constructor this argument EVALUATES to
		// and resolves `.context()` by the constructor its receiver EVALUATES
		// to, so a layer's provided set and a body's required set are one fact
		// about one program; computing them with two resolvers is how they came
		// apart. It used to call `classReferenceSymbol`, which asked the
		// checker TYPE first and the syntax only as a fallback — the exact
		// ordering `contextReceiverOf`'s own comment records as producing a
		// MISATTRIBUTED row.
		//
		// Measured on this backend before the change:
		//
		//   * `Layer.succeed(Db as unknown as typeof Cfg, cfg)` — the asserted
		//     type's symbol is `Cfg`, so the layer was certified as providing
		//     `Cfg`; the program checked `ok: true`, RAN, and aborted with
		//     `Panic: unsatisfied Context requirement` at `Cfg.context`. A
		//     FAIL-OPEN, and the reference refused the same program with the
		//     precise SMITHERS2101 naming `Cfg`.
		//   * `Layer.succeed(<any>Db, db)` and `Layer.succeed(Db as any, db)` —
		//     the erased type has no class symbol, so both were refused
		//     SMITHERS2104 while the reference accepted them and RAN. Asking
		//     the OPERAND first accepts them here too.
		//   * `Layer.succeed(flag ? Db : Twin, db)` — subtype reduction leaves
		//     `typeof Db`, so the type alone records `Db` for an argument that
		//     is `Twin` half the time. `valueBranches` still spells both arms.
		//
		// An argument that pins no single class lands on this function's own
		// fail-closed `false`, which is the blunt SMITHERS2104 — the answer the
		// resolver already gives every expression it cannot see through. No new
		// code is minted: SMITHERS2106 is the refusal for an unpinned
		// `.context()` RECEIVER, where the alternative is a silent capability
		// READ; the alternative here is a layer whose closure is unproven,
		// which is what SMITHERS2104 says.
		capability := a.contextReceiverOf(call.Arguments.Nodes[0], 0)
		if capability.kind != contextReceiverCapability {
			return provided, false
		}
		addRow(provided, a.row(capability.symbol))
		return provided, true
	}
	if a.prelude.layerCall(expression, "merge") {
		complete := true
		if call.Arguments == nil {
			return provided, true
		}
		for _, argument := range call.Arguments.Nodes {
			rows, known := a.resolveLayer(argument, cloneSymbolSet(seen))
			addRows(provided, rows)
			complete = complete && known
		}
		return provided, complete
	}
	return provided, false
}

func cloneSymbolSet(values map[*ast.Symbol]bool) map[*ast.Symbol]bool {
	cloned := make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool, len(values))
	for symbol, value := range values {
		cloned[symbol] = value
	}
	return cloned
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) provideEdge(call *ast.Node) (rowProvideEdge, bool) {
	if !a.prelude.layerCall(call, "provide") {
		return rowProvideEdge{}, false
	}
	arguments := call.AsCallExpression().Arguments
	if arguments == nil || len(arguments.Nodes) < 2 {
		return rowProvideEdge{node: call}, true
	}
	layerArgument := arguments.Nodes[0]
	callbackArgument := arguments.Nodes[1]
	provided, complete := a.resolveLayer(layerArgument, make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool))
	return rowProvideEdge{
		node:      call,
		callback:  a.resolveFunctionReference(callbackArgument),
		provided:  provided,
		complete:  complete,
		argument:  layerArgument,
		callbacks: callbackArgument,
	}, true
}

// evaluatedOutsideFunction is THE ONE TABLE for the children of a function-like
// declaration that are evaluated in the scope AROUND it rather than inside it.
//
// A member's COMPUTED NAME is the whole table: `{ [key]() {} }` evaluates `key`
// where the object literal is written, before the method exists, so `key`'s
// coercion belongs to the ENCLOSING row. Every walk below stops at a function
// boundary, and a function's own walk starts at its BODY — so a computed name
// was visited by nobody, and enclosingFunction answered with the very method the
// name names, which put a TOP-LEVEL `{ [obj]() {} }` inside a function where the
// module-scope requirement pass never looked. Measured on this backend:
// `{ [obj]() { return 1 } }` over an `obj` whose `toString()` reads a capability
// compiled `ok: true` and panicked at run time. Nine spellings did it — a
// method, a getter, a setter, an `async` method and a generator method in an
// object literal, and a method, getter, static method and `async` method in a
// class — while the three member kinds that are NOT function-like
// (`{ [obj]: 1 }`, `{ [obj]: () => 1 }`, `class S { [obj]: number }`) were
// charged correctly all along. That contrast is the diagnosis: this is a
// statement about WALKS, not about computed keys.
//
// It is written once and consumed by every walk and resolver that stops at a
// function boundary, because teaching only the body walk fixes the in-function
// spelling and leaves the top-level one open — the half-fix this class keeps
// reopening as.
//
// Parameter defaults are deliberately NOT here: they are evaluated when the
// function is CALLED, so they belong to the callee's own row, and callSiteIsAnalyzed
// depends on that answer. Decorators are not here either: they are evaluated
// when the CLASS DEFINITION is, and implicitInvocations already charges them at
// the class node for that reason.
func evaluatedOutsideFunction(node *ast.Node) []*ast.Node {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if name := node.Name(); name != nil && ast.IsComputedPropertyName(name) {
		return []*ast.Node{name}
	}
	return nil
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) collectFacts(fn *functionRows) {
	body := fn.node.Body()
	if body == nil {
		return
	}
	var visit func(*ast.Node, bool)
	visit = func(node *ast.Node, caught bool) {
		if node == nil {
			return
		}
		if node != body && ast.IsFunctionLikeDeclaration(node) && !nativeInvokedWhereDefined(node) {
			// A nested function is its own row — except for the parts of it that
			// THIS scope evaluates. See evaluatedOutsideFunction.
			for _, outside := range evaluatedOutsideFunction(node) {
				visit(outside, caught)
			}
			return
		}
		if ast.IsTryStatement(node) {
			statement := node.AsTryStatement()
			visit(statement.TryBlock, caught || statement.CatchClause != nil)
			if statement.CatchClause != nil {
				visit(statement.CatchClause, caught)
			}
			if statement.FinallyBlock != nil {
				visit(statement.FinallyBlock, caught)
			}
			return
		}
		// Determinism-sensitive ambient sites that CHARGE rather than refuse.
		// specification/compatibility.mdx §Determinism-Sensitive Members rows
		// three and five. Charged HERE, in the per-function walk, because a row
		// is a property of the enclosing function; checkHostGlobals walks the
		// whole file and has no function to charge. The reference frontend
		// charges at the same point in the same walk, for the same reason.
		for _, requirement := range a.ambientRequirementCharges(node) {
			addRow(fn.directRequirements, ambientRow(requirement))
		}
		if ast.IsThrowStatement(node) && !caught {
			expression := node.AsThrowStatement().Expression
			if expression != nil {
				t := a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(expression)
				rows, known := a.rowsOfType(t)
				if !known {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS1103", "recoverable throw values must extend Error; use panic(...) for an unknown defect")
				} else {
					addRows(fn.directFailures, rows)
				}
			}
		}
		if ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
			if _, ok := a.prelude.expectCall(node); ok {
				addRow(fn.directFailures, a.row(a.prelude.panic))
			}
			// An authored `panic(...)`/`Reflect.panic(...)` exit does NOT enter
			// the recoverable failure row and therefore cannot widen this
			// function's return type, so no row is charged for one here.
			//
			// specification/failures.mdx §Panic Does Not Widen a Return Type:
			// "Calling `panic(...)` MUST NOT force a function's return type to
			// widen into `Result<A, Panic>`. ... A function that validates an
			// argument, refuses a forgery, or asserts an invariant MUST
			// therefore be able to abort with `panic(...)` while keeping a plain
			// return type." It follows from the two rules the same page already
			// carried: the panic case is "tracked separately from ordinary
			// recoverable Error variants" and "Ordinary Result recovery MUST NOT
			// swallow panic implicitly" — charging it here put it inside `E`,
			// the expected-error channel, where `unwrapOr`/`recover`/`match`
			// consume it as ordinary. docs/DECISIONS.md carries the matching
			// Locked entry.
			//
			// The panic is still tracked, just not in this set: `panicExit`
			// still recognizes it, SMITHERS1503 still constrains where it may
			// appear, and `lowerPanicExit` still materializes it when — and only
			// when — the function's published row names Panic
			// (liftShape.panics). The FOREIGN panic case, charged by
			// `foreignPolicyForCall` below, is deliberately untouched:
			// failures.mdx §Foreign Exceptions makes that a checked obligation
			// the caller must propagate, catch, or adapt.
			// A capability read whose receiver does not pin exactly one Context
			// subclass is REFUSED, not silently dropped. The `else` used to be
			// silence, and silence is what let a certified program read a
			// capability its row does not name.
			if capability, isContext := a.contextRequirement(node); isContext {
				switch capability.kind {
				case contextReceiverCapability:
					addRow(fn.directRequirements, a.row(capability.symbol))
				case contextReceiverAmbiguous:
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS2106", ambiguousContextReceiverMessage)
				}
			}
			callee := a.resolveCallee(node)
			if callee != nil {
				fn.calls = append(fn.calls, rowCallEdge{
					node:                 node,
					callee:               callee,
					propagatesFailure:    a.callPropagatesFailure(node),
					instantiatedFailures: a.instantiatedFailures(node, callee),
				})
			}
			if policy, foreign := a.foreignPolicyForCall(node); foreign && !fn.resultBoundaryBody {
				// Inside an authored `Result.try`/`Result.tryPromise` body the
				// adapter is the catch scope: the foreign call keeps its throw,
				// the boundary converts it, and neither wrapping the call nor
				// charging its failure here would be correct.
				a.foreignCalls[node] = policy
				// The ROW charge is this pass's business: a lift whose failure
				// reaches the enclosing channel adds its failures to that row.
				//
				// The MUST-CONSUME charge is not, and used to be made here as a
				// bare `else` — an unconditional SMITHERS1301 at the call. That
				// put the foreign lift outside the must-consume machinery, so it
				// could not apply the bound/unbound split (SMITHERS1301 at an
				// unbound producer, SMITHERS1302 at a binding nothing consumes)
				// that the same analysis applies to every authored producer, and
				// it over-reported at every position the ownership walk already
				// calls a consumption. boundProducedKind now reads the same
				// foreign table, so checkBoundMustConsume and
				// checkUnboundMustConsume charge the lift exactly as they charge
				// an authored Result.
				if policy.kind != "never" && a.callPropagatesFailure(node) {
					a.addForeignFailures(fn.directFailures, policy)
				}
			}
			if edge, ok := a.provideEdge(node); ok {
				fn.provides = append(fn.provides, edge)
			}
		}
		// A tagged template and a `new` name their callee in the syntax and
		// invoke it, so they are ordinary calls for row purposes; see
		// isInvocationExpression for the fail-open they closed. A CONSTRUCTOR
		// deliberately never propagates a failure: it cannot legally carry one
		// (a Result-returning constructor is SMITHERS1105, an inferred-fallible
		// one is SMITHERS1101), so the only channel that travels through `new` is
		// the requirement row.
		if ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(node) || ast.IsNewExpression(node) {
			if callee := a.resolveInvokedDeclaration(node); callee != nil {
				fn.calls = append(fn.calls, rowCallEdge{
					node:                 node,
					callee:               callee,
					propagatesFailure:    ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(node) && a.callPropagatesFailure(node),
					instantiatedFailures: a.instantiatedFailures(node, callee),
				})
			}
		}
		if isInvocationExpression(node) {
			fn.callbackValues = append(fn.callbackValues, a.crossingCallbacks(node)...)
		}
		// Every IMPLICIT protocol invocation runs a function the program never
		// names, exactly as reading `box.size` runs its getter — and
		// specification/requirements.mdx §Inference governs them for the same
		// reason accessorInvocations exists.
		fn.accessorUses = append(fn.accessorUses, a.implicitInvocations(node)...)
		if ast.IsNonNullExpression(node) {
			operand := node.AsNonNullExpression().Expression
			if _, failure, ok := a.resultArguments(a.unwidenedIndexedAccessType(operand)); ok {
				a.resultPropagations[node] = operand
				if rows, known := a.rowsOfType(failure); known {
					addRows(fn.directFailures, rows)
				}
			}
		}
		if ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(node) || ast.IsElementAccessExpression(node) {
			fn.accessorUses = append(fn.accessorUses, a.accessorInvocations(node)...)
		}
		if ast.IsBindingElement(node) {
			fn.accessorUses = append(fn.accessorUses, a.destructuredAccessorInvocations(node)...)
		}
		// Destructuring a foreign value reads its members, so it runs whatever
		// accessors that value serves them from — the same execution the property
		// rule above refuses, spelled as a pattern instead of an access. The
		// reference refuses it at the pattern (`const { k } = keyed`); this backend
		// modelled the pattern only for AUTHORED accessor rows and let the foreign
		// case through with no diagnostic at all.
		if ast.IsVariableDeclaration(node) {
			declaration := node.AsVariableDeclaration()
			name := declaration.Name()
			if name != nil && declaration.Initializer != nil &&
				(ast.IsObjectBindingPattern(name) || ast.IsArrayBindingPattern(name)) &&
				!a.namespaceObjectReference(declaration.Initializer) &&
				a.foreignValueProvenance(declaration.Initializer, make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool)) {
				a.report(name, "SMITHERS1506", "destructuring a foreign value can execute untyped accessors and has no expression-safe Result lowering; read it through an annotated getter/factory adapter instead")
			}
		}
		if ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(node) || ast.IsElementAccessExpression(node) {
			receiver := node.Expression()
			isCallTarget := node.Parent != nil && ast.IsCallExpression(node.Parent) && node.Parent.AsCallExpression().Expression == node
			// The RECEIVER's value provenance is the rule, and it is the WHOLE
			// rule. A `.sm` local whose type is an interface declared in a foreign
			// module holds an authored value, and reading its members executes no
			// foreign accessor — specification/compatibility.mdx, "Runtime
			// TypeScript Dependency": "A type-only import MUST NOT add that
			// requirement." An ESM namespace object is excluded for the same reason
			// the reference excludes it: reading through one is live-binding
			// selection, not a user accessor.
			//
			// This used to state that rule in prose and then contradict it with a
			// second gate that asked where the MEMBER was declared, and that second
			// gate is the fail-open the corpus records as
			// 09-foreign-calls/a-foreign-index-signature-read-through-a-property-access-needs-an-adapter:
			// `keyed.width` through a `Record<string, number>` index signature
			// resolves to a member with NO declarations, an empty declaration list is
			// not evidence of a trust claim, and the fork compiled, ran and exited 0
			// on a program the reference refuses. The declaring FILE was never the
			// question either — nothing stops a foreign object from serving `length`
			// or `toString` from a throwing getter while the member's only
			// declaration is in lib.es5.d.ts. Asking the receiver one question makes
			// the answer total over the spellings: dotted and element access, a
			// literal or a computed key, an index signature, an `any`-typed member
			// and a member the language library declares are one rule rather than
			// five, and the two backends report the same set at the same positions.
			if !isCallTarget && !a.namespaceObjectReference(receiver) &&
				a.foreignValueProvenance(receiver, make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool)) {
				a.foreignEvaluationReports[node] = true
				a.report(node, "SMITHERS1506", "foreign property/accessor reads need an annotated adapter before they can enter checked code")
			}
		}
		if ast.IsReturnStatement(node) {
			expression := node.AsReturnStatement().Expression
			if expression != nil {
				t := a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(expression)
				if _, failure, ok := a.resultArguments(t); ok {
					if rows, known := a.rowsOfType(failure); known {
						addRows(fn.directFailures, rows)
					}
				}
			}
		}
		node.ForEachChild(func(child *ast.Node) bool {
			visit(child, caught)
			return false
		})
	}
	visit(body, false)
}

// collectInferredResultPropagations completes the postfix table after row
// inference. Explicit Result operands were recorded during collectFacts so
// their nominal errors could seed inference. This second pass covers a direct
// call whose authored TypeScript type is still its success type but whose
// checked Smithers callee or foreign boundary is known to produce a Result.
func (a *rowAnalysis) collectInferredResultPropagations(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsNonNullExpression(node) {
				operand := node.AsNonNullExpression().Expression
				if a.resultPropagationOperand(operand) {
					a.resultPropagations[node] = operand
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		visit(file.AsNode())
	}
}

// resultPropagationOperand answers "is the value of this expression a Result",
// which is SMITHERS1207's input.
//
// It used to restate the wrapper table by hand — `Paren, As, TypeAssertion,
// Await`, no `satisfies` — 1,400 lines below typeOnlyWrapperOperand, and it had
// no identifier case at all, so a Result reached through a `const` binding was
// invisible unless the binding's authored TypeScript type was ALREADY the
// prelude Result (true for an annotated callee, false for an inferred-fallible
// one, which is why every existing `satisfies` corpus case sailed past: the
// annotated type masked the missing entries). Measured on this backend:
// `const r = inferred("ok"); const v = r!` — the most ordinary store-then-
// propagate spelling in the language — did not compile here while the reference
// ran both its paths, and it was refused by TWO diagnostics that contradict each
// other about the same value: SMITHERS1302 "Result 'r' is never consumed" from
// the must-consume walk, which saw a Result through the binding, and
// SMITHERS1207 "postfix ! requires a Result operand" from this one, which did
// not.
//
// The wrapper table is now typeOnlyWrapperOperand (THE ONE TABLE), and the
// binding edge asks boundProducedKind — the SAME answer checkBoundMustConsume
// asked about this very declaration — so 1302 and 1207 can never disagree about
// a bound value again.
// unwidenedIndexedAccessType is the type an element read would have had if
// `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` were off, when — and only when — that option is
// what added the `| undefined`.
//
// `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is mandatory (compatibility.mdx §Mandatory) and
// turning it on is what put this function here. Under it, `results[i]` over a
// `Result<A, E>[]` is `Result<A, E> | undefined`; isResultType answers false
// because isVariantUnion requires EVERY constituent to be `SmithersOk` or
// `SmithersErr` and `undefined` is neither, so `results[i]!` was refused as
// SMITHERS1207 "postfix ! requires a Result operand". The value at `results[i]`
// IS a Result — it was widened by the option the specification itself mandates,
// not by anything the author wrote.
//
// The absence axis stays intact: `!` is the error axis and `?.`/`??` are the
// absence axis, so a `Result<A, E> | undefined` the AUTHOR wrote is still not a
// `!` operand. The two are told apart by the container's INDEX type, which the
// option does not widen — only the access site is widened — so the `|
// undefined` is dropped only when the container's own element type does not
// already admit `undefined`. `(Result<A, E> | undefined)[]` therefore keeps its
// refusal, `string[]` keeps its refusal for the ordinary reason, and a
// heterogeneous tuple `[Result<A, E>, string]` accepts `pair[0]!` while
// refusing `pair[1]!` — which is why the ACCESS type is narrowed rather than
// the index type substituted for it.
//
// This is the same rule, over the same four cases, as
// `unwidenedIndexedAccessType` in poc/src/language/semantic.ts. The two
// backends must agree here: the reference is the only other implementation of
// `!`, and a divergence would be a program one backend runs and the other
// refuses.
func (a *rowAnalysis) unwidenedIndexedAccessType(expression *ast.Node) *checker.Type {
	widened := a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(expression)
	if expression == nil || !ast.IsElementAccessExpression(expression) || widened == nil || !widened.IsUnion() {
		return widened
	}
	constituents := widened.Types()
	present := make([]*checker.Type, 0, len(constituents))
	for _, member := range constituents {
		if member.Flags()&(checker.TypeFlagsUndefined|checker.TypeFlagsNull) == 0 {
			present = append(present, member)
		}
	}
	if len(present) == len(constituents) || len(present) == 0 {
		return widened
	}
	container := a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(expression.Expression())
	if container == nil {
		return widened
	}
	info := a.checker.GetIndexInfoOfType(container, a.checker.GetNumberType())
	if info == nil {
		info = a.checker.GetIndexInfoOfType(container, a.checker.GetStringType())
	}
	if info == nil || admitsUndefinedType(info.ValueType()) {
		return widened
	}
	if len(present) == 1 {
		return present[0]
	}
	return a.checker.GetUnionType(present)
}

// admitsUndefinedType reports whether `undefined` or `null` is already part of
// t, at the top level. A container whose declared element type says so was
// written that way by the author, and nothing here may take that back. The
// reference spells the same test as `getNonNullableType(indexType) !==
// indexType`, which the fork's checker does not export.
func admitsUndefinedType(t *checker.Type) bool {
	if t == nil {
		return false
	}
	nullish := checker.TypeFlagsUndefined | checker.TypeFlagsNull
	if t.IsUnion() {
		for _, member := range t.Types() {
			if member.Flags()&nullish != 0 {
				return true
			}
		}
		return false
	}
	return t.Flags()&nullish != 0
}

// isUnwidenedIndexedAccess reports whether an element read's `| undefined` was
// added by `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` and has been seen through above.
//
// The lowering asks this because it has to ASSERT in the emitted TypeScript what
// the provenance walk PROVED about the authored `.sm`: `found[0]!` over a
// `Result<A, E>[]` binds `const __smithersUnwrappedN = found[0]` and then reads
// `.ok` off it, and under the mandatory option that binding is
// `Result<A, E> | undefined`, so the lowered program was rejected with TS18048
// even though the authored program is legal. Same rule, same reason, and same
// name as isUnwidenedIndexedAccess in poc/src/language/semantic.ts.
func (a *rowAnalysis) isUnwidenedIndexedAccess(expression *ast.Node) bool {
	if expression == nil || !ast.IsElementAccessExpression(expression) {
		return false
	}
	return a.unwidenedIndexedAccessType(expression) != a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(expression)
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) resultPropagationOperand(expression *ast.Node) bool {
	return a.propagatedResultOperand(expression, make(map[*ast.Symbol]bool))
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) propagatedResultOperand(expression *ast.Node, seen map[*ast.Symbol]bool) bool {
	if expression == nil {
		return false
	}
	if a.prelude.isResultType(a.unwidenedIndexedAccessType(expression)) {
		return true
	}
	if operand := typeOnlyWrapperOperand(expression); operand != nil {
		return a.propagatedResultOperand(operand, seen)
	}
	switch expression.Kind {
	// `await` is a real change of value, so it is this caller's own branch
	// rather than an entry in the shared table; see typeOnlyWrapperOperand.
	case ast.KindAwaitExpression:
		return a.propagatedResultOperand(expression.Expression(), seen)
	// A tagged template is an invocation, so `` tag`x` `` propagates whatever
	// `tag(parts)` propagates; see isInvocationExpression.
	case ast.KindCallExpression, ast.KindTaggedTemplateExpression:
		if callee := a.resolveCallee(expression); callee != nil {
			// An async call produces a Promise. The Result becomes available only
			// after an authored await, so postfix ! on the call itself must fail
			// closed as a non-Result operand.
			return !ast.HasSyntacticModifier(callee.node, ast.ModifierFlagsAsync) &&
				(callee.declaredShape.result || len(callee.failures) != 0)
		}
		if policy, foreign := a.foreignCalls[expression]; foreign && policy.kind != "never" {
			return true
		}
	case ast.KindIdentifier:
		symbol := a.symbolAt(expression)
		if symbol == nil || seen[symbol] {
			return false
		}
		declaration := variableLikeDeclaration(symbol)
		if declaration == nil || !ast.IsVariableDeclaration(declaration) {
			return false
		}
		initializer := declaration.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer
		if initializer == nil {
			return false
		}
		seen[symbol] = true
		// A binding that holds an EXTRACTED value is not a Result: `const r =
		// lookup(k)!` binds the success value, so `r!` is the SMITHERS1207 both
		// backends already report. This is boundProducedKind's own first
		// question, asked here so the two rules read the propagation table the
		// same way; without it an annotated callee's `f()!` would answer "Result"
		// from its checker type and hand postfix `!` a plain number.
		if a.resultPropagations[stripMustConsumeWrappers(initializer)] != nil {
			return false
		}
		if a.boundProducedKind(initializer) == boundConsumeResult {
			return true
		}
		// A foreign call's Result is not a checker type — the authored success
		// type survives the boundary — so boundProducedKind cannot see it and
		// this walk's own foreign table answers instead. An alias of an alias
		// lands here too, which is what the recursion is for.
		return a.propagatedResultOperand(initializer, seen)
	}
	return false
}

func symbolDeclarations(symbol *ast.Symbol) []*ast.Node {
	if symbol == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return symbol.Declarations
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) collectTopProvides(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsFunctionLikeDeclaration(node) {
				// See evaluatedOutsideFunction.
				for _, outside := range evaluatedOutsideFunction(node) {
					visit(outside)
				}
				return false
			}
			if ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
				if edge, ok := a.provideEdge(node); ok {
					a.topProvides = append(a.topProvides, edge)
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) inferRows() {
	for _, fn := range a.functions {
		addRows(fn.bodyFailures, fn.directFailures)
		addRows(fn.requirements, fn.directRequirements)
	}
	changed := true
	for changed {
		changed = false
		for _, fn := range a.functions {
			for _, edge := range fn.calls {
				if edge.callee == nil {
					continue
				}
				if edge.propagatesFailure {
					failures := edge.callee.failures
					if edge.instantiatedFailures != nil {
						failures = edge.instantiatedFailures
					}
					changed = addRows(fn.bodyFailures, failures) || changed
				}
				changed = addRows(fn.requirements, edge.callee.requirements) || changed
			}
			// Reading or writing a property backed by an accessor CALLS it, so
			// its requirements are transitive exactly as an ordinary call's are
			// (specification/requirements.mdx §Inference). See
			// accessorInvocations for why the failure row is not charged.
			for _, accessor := range fn.accessorUses {
				changed = addRows(fn.requirements, accessor.requirements) || changed
			}
			// A callback handed across a call boundary is invoked inside that
			// call, so its capabilities are this body's capabilities. See
			// functionRows.callbackValues.
			for _, callback := range fn.callbackValues {
				changed = addRows(fn.requirements, callback.requirements) || changed
			}
			for _, provide := range fn.provides {
				if provide.callback == nil {
					continue
				}
				changed = addRows(fn.requirements, missingRowSet(provide.callback.requirements, provide.provided)) || changed
			}
			if fn.explicitReturn && fn.declaredShape.result {
				changed = addRows(fn.failures, fn.declaredFailures) || changed
			} else {
				changed = addRows(fn.failures, fn.bodyFailures) || changed
			}
		}
	}
}

// formatRowSet renders a failure row set into a diagnostic sentence. `rowSet`
// is a Go map, so the order is not merely a cosmetic choice — an unsorted walk
// would print a different sentence on different runs. The names are authored
// Error class names, and the reference renders the same set with
// `[...values].sort()` (poc/src/language/semantic.ts:3709), which is UTF-16
// code-unit order.
// formatRowSet renders a row as the set of NAMES it publishes.
//
// Deduplicated, for the reason missingRowSet subtracts by name: the reference
// frontend's row is a `Set<string>`, so an ambient `Locale` charge and a
// `Locale.context()` read render as one `Locale` there. Without the dedupe this
// backend would render `{Locale, Locale}` for the same program — a divergence
// visible in the text of SMITHERS1101, SMITHERS1806, SMITHERS2101 and
// SMITHERS2102, and one that no conformance case would catch, because the runner
// compares diagnostic codes and positions rather than message text.
func formatRowSet(rows rowSet) string {
	names := make([]string, 0, len(rows))
	seen := make(map[string]struct{}, len(rows))
	for _, row := range rows {
		if _, duplicate := seen[row.name]; duplicate {
			continue
		}
		seen[row.name] = struct{}{}
		names = append(names, row.name)
	}
	sortUTF16(names)
	return "{" + strings.Join(names, ", ") + "}"
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) reportWithCategory(node *ast.Node, code string, category string, message string) {
	file := ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(node)
	if file == nil {
		return
	}
	if a.comptime != nil && a.comptime.allowed[node] {
		// The node is inside a construct the comptime frontend owns. It has no
		// runtime existence, so a runtime rule cannot be broken by it.
		return
	}
	start := scanner.SkipTrivia(file.Text(), node.Pos())
	length := node.End() - start
	if length < 0 {
		length = 0
	}
	// Two rules — or one rule reached through two nodes that begin at the same
	// token — can render the identical diagnostic. `nested.inner.k` is two
	// foreign property reads and both start at `nested`, so both report
	// SMITHERS1506 at the same position with the same text. The reference
	// frontend collapses that in `finalizeDiagnostics` (keyed on
	// code + start + message) and prints one; without the same collapse here the
	// two backends disagree on a program neither of them is wrong about.
	for _, existing := range a.reports[file] {
		if existing.code == code && existing.start == start && existing.message == message &&
			existing.category == category {
			return
		}
	}
	a.reports[file] = append(a.reports[file], loweringReport{code: code, category: category, message: message, start: start, length: length})
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) report(node *ast.Node, code string, message string) {
	a.reportWithCategory(node, code, "error", message)
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) reportWarning(node *ast.Node, code string, message string) {
	a.reportWithCategory(node, code, "warning", message)
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) checkContracts() {
	for _, fn := range a.functions {
		if fn.explicitReturn && !fn.declaredShape.result && len(fn.bodyFailures) != 0 {
			fn.hasContractError = true
			a.report(fn.node, "SMITHERS1101", "explicit return type cannot represent recoverable failures "+formatRowSet(fn.bodyFailures)+"; use Result<A, E>")
		}
		if fn.explicitReturn && fn.declaredShape.result {
			extra := missingRowSet(fn.bodyFailures, fn.declaredFailures)
			if len(extra) != 0 {
				fn.hasContractError = true
				a.report(fn.node, "SMITHERS1104", "Result contract omits reachable failures "+formatRowSet(extra))
			}
		}
		if fn.exported && !fn.explicitReturn && len(fn.bodyFailures) != 0 {
			fn.hasContractError = true
			a.report(fn.node, "SMITHERS1102", "exported fallible functions must spell Result<A, E> (or Promise<Result<A, E>>) in their public contract")
		}
	}
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) resultParameterReferenceConsumed(node *ast.Node) bool {
	current := node
	for current.Parent != nil {
		parent := current.Parent
		switch {
		case ast.IsNonNullExpression(parent) && parent.AsNonNullExpression().Expression == current:
			return a.resultPropagations[parent] != nil
		case ast.IsParenthesizedExpression(parent), ast.IsAsExpression(parent), parent.Kind == ast.KindSatisfiesExpression:
			current = parent
			continue
		case ast.IsReturnStatement(parent):
			return true
		case func() bool {
			selection, selected := a.prelude.selectMember(parent)
			return selected && selection.receiver == current
		}():
			// Inspecting a discriminant/property or invoking a Result operation
			// consumes the value at this boundary.
			return true
		default:
			return false
		}
	}
	return false
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) checkResultParameters(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, fn := range a.functions {
		if fn.symbol == nil {
			// Anonymous callback ownership is checked at its enclosing boundary;
			// it is not a separately callable parameter contract.
			continue
		}
		for index, parameter := range fn.node.Parameters() {
			name := parameter.Name()
			if name == nil || !ast.IsIdentifier(name) || !a.prelude.isResultType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(name)) {
				continue
			}
			// Ownership is asked of the parameter's resolved SYMBOL through the
			// same per-file identifier index checkBoundMustConsume uses for a
			// binding, and for the same reason: specification/failures.mdx:190
			// names five consumption ACTS performed on the value, and no
			// sentence there conditions any of them on the function body the
			// act is written in.
			//
			// This used to be a hand-rolled walk of the body that stopped dead
			// at every nested function-like, so the fork was inconsistent with
			// ITSELF — it accepted `const entry = …; run(() => { entry! })` and
			// refused the byte-equivalent program in which `entry` is a
			// parameter. Two members of that gap were measured: a consumption
			// inside a callback body, and a consumption in a sibling
			// parameter's default (the old walk started at the BODY, so a
			// default was unreachable too). Both are references to one symbol,
			// and one index finds both. A shadowing declaration in a nested
			// scope resolves to a different symbol, so the wider search cannot
			// manufacture a consumption the narrow one would have missed.
			symbol := a.symbolAt(name)
			consumed := false
			for _, reference := range a.identifierReferences(ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(fn.node), symbol) {
				if reference != name && a.resultParameterReferenceConsumed(reference) {
					consumed = true
					break
				}
			}
			if !consumed {
				fn.unconsumedParams[index] = true
				a.report(name, "SMITHERS1302", "Result parameter must be consumed by returning, matching, transforming, inspecting, or propagating it with postfix !")
			}
		}
	}

	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
				callee := a.resolveCallee(node)
				arguments := node.AsCallExpression().Arguments
				if callee != nil && arguments != nil {
					for index := range callee.unconsumedParams {
						if index < len(arguments.Nodes) && a.prelude.isResultType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(arguments.Nodes[index])) {
							a.report(arguments.Nodes[index], "SMITHERS1301", "produced Result must be returned, matched, transformed, inspected, or propagated with postfix !")
						}
					}
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) checkOneLayer(edge rowProvideEdge) {
	if edge.callback == nil {
		a.report(edge.node, "SMITHERS2103", "Layer.provide callback must resolve to a checked local function")
		return
	}
	if !edge.complete {
		target := edge.argument
		if target == nil {
			target = edge.node
		}
		a.report(target, "SMITHERS2104", "Layer expression is opaque; the compiler cannot prove its provided capability closure")
		return
	}
	if edge.callback.inferredFallible() && !edge.callback.declaredShape.result {
		target := edge.callbacks
		if target == nil {
			target = edge.node
		}
		a.report(target, "SMITHERS2105", "fallible Layer.provide callbacks need an explicit Result (or Promise<Result>) contract so the provided computation keeps its failure channel")
	}
	if enclosingFunction(edge.node) == nil {
		missing := missingRowSet(edge.callback.requirements, edge.provided)
		if len(missing) != 0 {
			a.report(edge.node, "SMITHERS2101", "Layer.provide is missing "+formatRowSet(missing))
		}
	}
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) checkLayers() {
	for _, fn := range a.functions {
		for _, edge := range fn.provides {
			a.checkOneLayer(edge)
		}
	}
	for _, edge := range a.topProvides {
		a.checkOneLayer(edge)
	}
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) checkTopLevelRequirements(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsFunctionLikeDeclaration(node) {
				// A member's COMPUTED NAME is evaluated where the object literal
				// or class is written, so a TOP-LEVEL `{ [obj]() {} }` is module
				// evaluation and belongs to this pass. See
				// evaluatedOutsideFunction; without this the top-level spellings
				// stay open even after the body walk is taught.
				for _, outside := range evaluatedOutsideFunction(node) {
					visit(outside)
				}
				return false
			}
			if ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
				if callee := a.resolveCallee(node); callee != nil && len(callee.requirements) != 0 {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS2102", "top-level call has unsatisfied requirements "+formatRowSet(callee.requirements))
				}
				// collectFacts walks function BODIES, so a capability read
				// written DIRECTLY at module scope was charged to nobody: it
				// compiled clean and panicked at run time with `capability 'Db'
				// was not provided`, while its INDIRECT spelling — a top-level
				// call to a function whose row names the capability — was
				// already refused by the branch above and pinned by
				// 05-context-rows/unsatisfied-top-level-requirement. A one-line
				// program shape the corpus pins was escapable by inlining the
				// read.
				if capability, isContext := a.contextRequirement(node); isContext {
					switch capability.kind {
					case contextReceiverCapability:
						rows := make(rowSet)
						addRow(rows, a.row(capability.symbol))
						a.report(node, "SMITHERS2102", "top-level call has unsatisfied requirements "+formatRowSet(rows))
					case contextReceiverAmbiguous:
						a.report(node, "SMITHERS2106", ambiguousContextReceiverMessage)
					}
				}
			}
			// A callback handed to a top-level higher-order call is invoked at
			// module evaluation too, and escaped the same way before
			// callbackValues existed.
			if isInvocationExpression(node) {
				for _, callback := range a.crossingCallbacks(node) {
					if len(callback.requirements) != 0 {
						a.report(node, "SMITHERS2102", "top-level call has unsatisfied requirements "+formatRowSet(callback.requirements))
					}
				}
			}
			// Module evaluation runs a top-level `` tag`x` `` and `new C()`
			// exactly as it runs a call, and with no enclosing row to charge; see
			// isInvocationExpression.
			if ast.IsTaggedTemplateExpression(node) || ast.IsNewExpression(node) {
