				if invoked := a.resolveInvokedDeclaration(node); invoked != nil && len(invoked.requirements) != 0 {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS2102", "top-level call has unsatisfied requirements "+formatRowSet(invoked.requirements))
				}
			}
			// A top-level accessor read or write invokes the accessor at module
			// evaluation just as a call does, and there is no enclosing function
			// row to carry its capabilities either. The same is true of every
			// implicit protocol invocation — a top-level `[...it]` runs the
			// authored iterator.
			var accessors []*functionRows
			switch {
			case ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(node) || ast.IsElementAccessExpression(node):
				accessors = a.accessorInvocations(node)
			case ast.IsBindingElement(node):
				accessors = a.destructuredAccessorInvocations(node)
			}
			accessors = append(accessors, a.implicitInvocations(node)...)
			for _, accessor := range accessors {
				if len(accessor.requirements) != 0 {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS2102", "top-level call has unsatisfied requirements "+formatRowSet(accessor.requirements))
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

const detachedContextReferenceMessage = "Capability.context is a compiler-recognized capability read, not a value; a detached reference (call/apply/bind, an alias, or handing it to another function) loses the requirement row — invoke it directly as Capability.context()"

// checkContextReferences refuses every DETACHED reference to the
// compiler-recognized `Context.context` member: it may only be INVOKED.
//
// The requirement row is recorded at the call site from the receiver, so every
// spelling that separates the member from its receiver erases the row while
// keeping the capability read. Measured on this backend before the rule:
// `Reflect.apply(Db.context, Db, [])` compiled `ok: true`, ran, and returned the
// provided `Db` service with an empty row, and `Db.context.call(Db)`,
// `.apply`, `.bind`, `const m = Db.context`, and `const { context } = Db` all
// compiled clean here while the reference refused them — INCIDENTALLY, through
// the stock type check over the emitted module, because its prelude's
// `this`-parameter makes a detached receiver unresolvable. That is the review's
// Finding 3 verdict divergence: a refusal resting on a typing accident on one
// backend and on nothing at all on the other.
//
// Making it a semantic rule states the contract once, identically on both:
// the member is a call, not a value. Type positions (`typeof Db.context`) read
// nothing and stay legal.
func (a *rowAnalysis) checkContextReferences(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if selection, selected := a.prelude.selectMember(node); selected &&
				selection.text == "context" &&
				!ast.IsPartOfTypeNode(node) && !typeQueryReference(node) &&
				a.prelude.isPreludeContextMember(selection) &&
				!isZeroArgumentCallee(node) {
				a.report(node, "SMITHERS2107", detachedContextReferenceMessage)
			}
			// `Reflect.get(Db, "context")` is the reflective spelling of
			// `Db["context"]` and reaches the same member with no member-access
			// node in the program at all: measured accepted with an empty row,
			// and it RAN, returning the provided service. `Reflect` is a
			// universal global, so the spelling is reachable. A COMPUTED key
			// (`Reflect.get(Db, key)`) is deliberately not matched — it names no
			// statically known member — and reflective laundering through the
			// rest of `Object`/`Reflect` stays open; closing that by
			// construction would mean constraining where a `Context` subclass
			// may appear as a value at all, which the specification does not
			// settle. Enumerating spellings is how a class reopens, so the limit
			// is recorded here rather than pretended away.
			if ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
				arguments := node.AsCallExpression().Arguments
				if arguments != nil && len(arguments.Nodes) >= 2 && a.isReflectiveMemberRead(node) {
					key := ast.SkipParentheses(arguments.Nodes[1])
					if key != nil && ast.IsStringLiteralLike(key) && key.Text() == "context" &&
						a.contextReceiverOf(arguments.Nodes[0], 0).kind != contextReceiverNone {
						a.report(node, "SMITHERS2107", detachedContextReferenceMessage)
					}
				}
			}
			// `const { context } = Db` detaches the member with no member-access
			// node to see it, which is why the reference refused it only through
			// the emitted-TypeScript type check and this backend accepted it
			// outright.
			if ast.IsBindingElement(node) && node.Parent != nil && ast.IsObjectBindingPattern(node.Parent) &&
				node.Parent.Parent != nil && ast.IsVariableDeclaration(node.Parent.Parent) {
				binding := node.AsBindingElement()
				name := binding.PropertyName
				if name == nil {
					name = binding.Name()
				}
				initializer := node.Parent.Parent.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer
				if name != nil && initializer != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(name) && name.Text() == "context" &&
					symbolDeclaredBy(a.checker.GetPropertyOfType(a.checker.GetTypeAtLocation(initializer), "context"), a.prelude.contextDecls) {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS2107", detachedContextReferenceMessage)
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

// isZeroArgumentCallee reports whether this node is the direct callee of a call
// with no arguments — the one position in which `Context.context` may appear.
//
// GROUPING PARENTHESES ARE WALKED THROUGH. `(Db.context)()` is the direct
// spelling with parentheses around it: the same member access, the same `this`
// binding, the same emitted call, and a row calleeSelection now records.
// Requiring the member to be the IMMEDIATE callee made that program draw
// SMITHERS2107 telling the author to "invoke it directly as
// `Capability.context()`" — advice it was already following — and publish an
// empty row. That was an over-correction, not a rule: SMITHERS2107 exists for
// the spellings that genuinely separate the member from its call, and every one
// of those puts some OTHER node between the two (an argument list, an object or
// array literal, a `return`, an assignment, or `.call`/`.apply`/`.bind`).
func isZeroArgumentCallee(node *ast.Node) bool {
	current := node
	for current.Parent != nil && ast.IsParenthesizedExpression(current.Parent) {
		current = current.Parent
	}
	parent := current.Parent
	if parent == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(parent) {
		return false
	}
	call := parent.AsCallExpression()
	return call.Expression == current && (call.Arguments == nil || len(call.Arguments.Nodes) == 0)
}

// isReflectiveMemberRead recognizes a builtin that reads one named member off a
// value handed to it. Recognized by the ambient global's identity, the same way
// `Date["now"]()` is: a local binding named `Reflect` resolves elsewhere and is
// excluded.
func (a *rowAnalysis) isReflectiveMemberRead(call *ast.Node) bool {
	selection, selected := a.prelude.calleeSelection(call)
	if !selected || selection.receiver == nil || !ast.IsIdentifier(selection.receiver) {
		return false
	}
	root := selection.receiver.Text()
	member := selection.text
	reflective := (root == "Reflect" && member == "get") ||
		(root == "Object" && member == "getOwnPropertyDescriptor")
	if !reflective {
		return false
	}
	// The ambient lib publishes both roots, and the compiler-owned prelude
	// augments `Reflect` with `panic`, so the prelude's own file counts as the
	// ambient environment here exactly as a `.d.ts` does. A LOCAL binding named
	// `Reflect` declares itself somewhere else and is not this builtin.
	declarations := symbolDeclarations(a.referencedValueSymbol(selection.receiver))
	if len(declarations) == 0 {
		return true
	}
	for _, declaration := range declarations {
		file := ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(declaration)
		if file == nil {
			continue
		}
		if file.IsDeclarationFile || file.FileName() == preludeVirtualName {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// checkTopLevelCheckedChannels rejects every operation that would charge a
// failure row at module evaluation, where no enclosing function contract can
// carry it. Recognition follows the same checker identities used to build
// function rows: the prelude's Result.expect and panic declarations, analyzed
// local callees, and the resolved declaration of a foreign call. A method or
// function that merely shares one of those names remains ordinary.
//
// Top-level postfix propagation is owned by lowerPropagation (SMITHERS1202), and a
// top-level throw is owned by visitStatement (SMITHERS1511). Keeping this pass
// to the Panic-producing call family avoids duplicate or competing diagnoses.
func (a *rowAnalysis) checkTopLevelCheckedChannels(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsFunctionLikeDeclaration(node) || node.Kind == ast.KindClassStaticBlockDeclaration {
				// See evaluatedOutsideFunction.
				for _, outside := range evaluatedOutsideFunction(node) {
					visit(outside)
				}
				return false
			}
			if ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
				if _, ok := a.prelude.expectCall(node); ok {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS1505", "top-level Result.expect() panics on the error variant and cannot expose that checked panic channel; move it into a checked Result-returning function and consume that Result")
				} else if a.resolveCallee(node) != nil {
					// An authored function call exposes its Result normally. Its
					// returned value is governed by must-consume/unwrap/expect, not
					// by the foreign Panic boundary below.
					node.ForEachChild(visit)
					return false
				} else if a.prelude.isPanicCall(node) {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS1505", "top-level panic cannot be represented as a checked Result; move it into a checked Result-returning function and consume that Result")
				} else if policy, foreign := a.foreignPolicyForCall(node); foreign && policy.kind != "never" {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS1505", "an untrusted foreign call at top level cannot expose its checked panic channel; move it into a checked Result-returning function and consume that Result")
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

// checkPanicCatchBoundaries is the PANIC half of SMITHERS1205, and it was
// missing from this backend entirely.
//
// The postfix-`!` half lives in `lowerPropagation`, where `l.guardedByCatch`
// already refuses a propagation whose early return would step past an authored
// `catch`. The panic half had no implementation at all, which was not a
// difference of message but a FAIL-OPEN, measured against the reference on
//
//	try { if (value < 0) panic("negative"); return "ok" } catch { return "caught" }
//
// The reference refuses it SMITHERS1205; this backend compiled it and printed
// `caught` — the ordinary `catch` swallowing an abort that must reach an
// explicit panic boundary. `Reflect.panic(...)` measured the same way, and
// `Result.expect()` — which lowers to the same panic exit — measured the same
// way through `lowerExpect`'s silent `leave()`.
//
// The MESSAGE is deliberately not the postfix-`!` sentence. A panic exit is
// lowered two ways — a completion value where the enclosing contract names
// `Panic` (`lowerPanicExit`), an unwinding `throw` where it does not
// (`lowerPanicThrow`) — so a sentence asserting either one would be false for
// the other half of the programs this arm fires on. What is true of both is
// that an ordinary `catch` is the wrong observer.
//
// Scope is measured against the reference, not assumed. A call to a HELPER that
// panics is not reported on either backend — the rule is about the intrinsic
// written inside the guarded block, not about a transitively aborting callee —
// and a `try` with only a `finally` guards nothing, so neither backend reports
// there. A nested function is its own exit owner and resets the flag, exactly as
// `lowerer.visit` does for `guardedByCatch`.
func (a *rowAnalysis) checkPanicCatchBoundaries(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node, bool)
		visit = func(node *ast.Node, caught bool) {
			if ast.IsFunctionLikeDeclaration(node) {
				node.ForEachChild(func(child *ast.Node) bool {
					visit(child, false)
					return false
				})
				return
			}
			if node.Kind == ast.KindTryStatement {
				try := node.AsTryStatement()
				visit(try.TryBlock, caught || try.CatchClause != nil)
				if try.CatchClause != nil {
					visit(try.CatchClause.AsCatchClause().Block, caught)
				}
				if try.FinallyBlock != nil {
					visit(try.FinallyBlock, caught)
				}
				return
			}
			if caught && ast.IsCallExpression(node) {
				construct := ""
				if a.prelude.isPanicCall(node) {
					construct = "panic(...)"
				} else if _, isExpect := a.prelude.expectCall(node); isExpect {
					construct = "Result.expect()"
				}
				if construct != "" {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS1205", construct+" inside a JavaScript try statement with a catch clause is not lowered because a panic exit is not a recoverable failure this catch may observe: where the enclosing contract materializes the panic the exit is a completion value the catch never sees, and where it does not the catch would swallow an abort that must reach an explicit panic boundary; move the panic point outside the try or handle it at that boundary")
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(func(child *ast.Node) bool {
				visit(child, caught)
				return false
			})
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(func(child *ast.Node) bool {
			visit(child, false)
			return false
		})
	}
}

// directCallReference reports whether an identifier is the callee of an
// ordinary call — the shape whose row this analysis already attributes.
// Parentheses are transparent: `(plain)(1, 2)` resolves and charges exactly what
// `plain(1, 2)` does. An `as`-cast callee is NOT transparent, because there the
// resolved signature — and with it the row — genuinely stops.
func directCallReference(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if node == nil || node.Parent == nil {
		return false
	}
	expression := node
	if ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(node.Parent) && node.Parent.AsPropertyAccessExpression().Name() == node {
		expression = node.Parent
	}
	for expression.Parent != nil && ast.IsParenthesizedExpression(expression.Parent) {
		expression = expression.Parent
	}
	parent := expression.Parent
	if parent == nil || !ast.IsCallExpression(parent) || parent.AsCallExpression().Expression != expression {
		return false
	}
	return callSiteIsAnalyzed(parent)
}

// callSiteIsAnalyzed reports whether a call's callee row was attributed
// anywhere. collectFacts walks function BODIES, and the top-level passes
// (SMITHERS2102 requirements, SMITHERS1301/1302 must-consume, SMITHERS1505
// channels) cover module evaluation and anything else outside a function, such
// as a class property initializer. A call in a PARAMETER DEFAULT is in neither:
// its callee's row is charged nowhere, so a cross-module callee there is a
// genuine fail-closed case and must keep SMITHERS1802. The reference backend
// reaches the same answer through its call-edge table, which is likewise built
// only from bodies.
func callSiteIsAnalyzed(node *ast.Node) bool {
	for current := node; current != nil && current.Parent != nil; current = current.Parent {
		// A member's computed NAME is analyzed by the ENCLOSING scope, not by the
		// member; see evaluatedOutsideFunction.
		if ast.IsFunctionLikeDeclaration(current.Parent) && !nodeIn(evaluatedOutsideFunction(current.Parent), current) {
			return current.Parent.Body() == current
		}
	}
	return true
}

func nodeIn(nodes []*ast.Node, node *ast.Node) bool {
	for _, candidate := range nodes {
		if candidate == node {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// typeQueryReference reports a `typeof f` entity name. It names the function's
// TYPE, never its value, so it can no more escape than an annotation can. The
// reference backend excludes it through its own type-position walk; this
// backend's ast.IsPartOfTypeNode does not reach an entity name, which made a
// plain `export type Sig = typeof plain` a fork-only refusal.
func typeQueryReference(node *ast.Node) bool {
	for current := node; current != nil && current.Parent != nil; current = current.Parent {
		if ast.IsTypeQueryNode(current.Parent) {
			return true
		}
		if !ast.IsIdentifier(current) && current.Kind != ast.KindQualifiedName {
			return false
		}
	}
	return false
}

// crossModuleEscapeSymbol resolves the function an identifier names.
//
// `{ plain }` carries the same function value `{ plain: plain }` does, but the
// identifier's own symbol is the object literal's PROPERTY, not the local it
// abbreviates — the shorthand escaped while the explicit spelling was refused,
// the same divergence resolveFunctionReference already had to close for
// SMITHERS1303.
func (a *rowAnalysis) crossModuleEscapeSymbol(node *ast.Node) *ast.Symbol {
	if node.Parent != nil && node.Parent.Kind == ast.KindShorthandPropertyAssignment &&
		node.Parent.Name() == node {
		if value := a.canonical(a.checker.GetShorthandAssignmentValueSymbol(node.Parent)); value != nil {
			return value
		}
	}
	return a.symbolAt(node)
}

// checkCrossModuleEscapes is the fail-closed backstop for a cross-module
// function that becomes a VALUE — specification/compatibility.mdx: a build "MUST
// fail closed on any construct whose lowering depends on information the file
// alone does not carry, rather than emitting a guess". It must not fire on an
// ordinary call, which is exactly what specification/requirements.mdx §Inference
// and specification/type-system.mdx §Fallibility Inference require rows to
// travel through: a direct callee (including through parentheses), an accessor
// access (reading it runs it), a `typeof` entity name, and a re-exported
// binding are all cases where nothing escapes.
func (a *rowAnalysis) checkCrossModuleEscapes(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			// A shorthand property NAME is a declaration name, but it is also a
			// use of the local it abbreviates, so it must not be skipped here.
			shorthand := node.Parent != nil && node.Parent.Kind == ast.KindShorthandPropertyAssignment &&
				node.Parent.Name() == node
			if ast.IsIdentifier(node) && !ast.IsPartOfTypeNode(node) && !typeQueryReference(node) &&
				(shorthand || !ast.IsDeclarationNameOrImportPropertyName(node)) {
				target := a.bySymbol[a.crossModuleEscapeSymbol(node)]
				if target != nil && target.file != file && !directCallReference(node) &&
					!a.accessorReference(node, target) && !a.destructuredAccessorReference(node, target) {
					a.report(node, "SMITHERS1802", "cross-module function '"+target.name+"' escapes direct static call analysis; wrap the higher-order use in an explicitly checked local function")
				}
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

// accessorReference reports whether an identifier is the member name of a
// property access that invokes `target` as an accessor.
func (a *rowAnalysis) accessorReference(node *ast.Node, target *functionRows) bool {
	parent := node.Parent
	if parent == nil || !ast.IsPropertyAccessExpression(parent) || parent.AsPropertyAccessExpression().Name() != node {
		return false
	}
	for _, accessor := range a.accessorInvocations(parent) {
		if accessor == target {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// destructuredAccessorReference reports whether an identifier is the explicit
// property name of a binding element that invokes `target` as a getter.
// `const { value: read } = source` reads `value` exactly as `source.value` does.
func (a *rowAnalysis) destructuredAccessorReference(node *ast.Node, target *functionRows) bool {
	parent := node.Parent
	if parent == nil || !ast.IsBindingElement(parent) || parent.AsBindingElement().PropertyName != node {
		return false
	}
	for _, accessor := range a.destructuredAccessorInvocations(parent) {
		if accessor == target {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

func trustedForeignModule(file *ast.SourceFile) bool {
	for _, comment := range leadingJSDocComments(file) {
		if exactModuleMarker(comment) && exactThrowsNeverMarker(comment) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// leadingJSDocComments enumerates the JSDoc comments in a file's leading
// trivia, as the SCANNER classifies them.
//
// The marker used to be found by searching the raw leading text for a `/** … */`
// SUBSTRING, which asks no one whether a JSDoc exists at all. Measured on this
// backend, with a runtime oracle: a `//` line comment whose body happens to
// contain the marker text, and a plain `/* … */` block containing it, both
// conferred module-initialization trust and the untrusted module's initializer
// RAN. That is the same defect this bridge already closed for `@throws {never}`
// on constructors — honouring a marker the parser attaches to nothing.
//
// The comment KIND is now the scanner's own answer, and so is the JSDoc test:
// the fork's scanner calls a multi-line comment a JSDoc exactly when it opens
// `/**` and is not the empty `/**\/`, which is what the two tests below spell.
// Note the direction this preserves: `/* @module @throws {never} */` (one
// asterisk) was already correctly refused, so the rule always meant to
// distinguish JSDoc from a block comment — it just did it by substring.
//
// The parser's own attached JSDoc nodes are deliberately NOT the source here,
// even though they would supply parsed tags. Measured by the reference lane over
// the 75 marker-carrying files in this repository, a tag-based implementation
// disagrees in 21 places and in BOTH directions: TypeScript attaches only the
// LAST JSDoc block before a statement, so the ordinary shape "module header,
// then the first export's own doc comment" loses the header (15 files, including
// `conformance/support/foreign.ts`, the corpus's own trusted module); and the
// JSDoc parser strips a `*` decoration inside a tag, so `@throws\n * {never}`
// parses as a `{never}` type expression and `conformance/support/split-trust-marker.ts`
// — a near-miss the corpus exists to keep refused — would have been GRANTED
// trust. A second lane measured the same fix losing the header of
// `decorated-trust-header.ts`, `capitalized-never.ts` and `upper-throws-tag.ts`.
func leadingJSDocComments(file *ast.SourceFile) []string {
	if file == nil || len(file.Statements.Nodes) == 0 {
		return nil
	}
	text := file.Text()
	limit := scanner.SkipTrivia(text, file.Statements.Nodes[0].Pos())
	if limit < 0 || limit > len(text) {
		return nil
	}
	s := scanner.NewScanner()
	s.SetText(text)
	s.SetScriptTarget(core.ScriptTargetLatest)
	s.SetLanguageVariant(core.LanguageVariantStandard)
	s.SetSkipTrivia(false)
	comments := make([]string, 0, 2)
	for {
		kind := s.Scan()
		if kind == ast.KindEndOfFile || s.TokenStart() >= limit {
			break
		}
		if kind != ast.KindMultiLineCommentTrivia {
			continue
		}
		comment := text[s.TokenStart():s.TokenEnd()]
		if !strings.HasPrefix(comment, "/**") || strings.HasPrefix(comment, "/**/") {
			continue
		}
		comments = append(comments, comment)
	}
	return comments
}

// exactModuleMarker and exactThrowsNeverMarker both compare with the exact case
// the specification prints. Neither folds case, and that is the rule, not an
// oversight.
//
// specification/failures.mdx, Foreign Exceptions (Locked): "`@throws {never}`
// removes the default panic case; `@throws {T}` declares the stated foreign
// error channel." Those are two productions of one syntax and only the spelling
// inside the braces separates them. `T` is a TypeScript type name and
// TypeScript type identity is case-sensitive, so `Never` is the second
// production — a declared channel whose Error constructor must resolve — and
// never the first. Folding case merges the productions and turns a channel the
// compiler could not reify into the trusted opt-out, which is the fail-open
// direction. The call boundary already reads the marker exactly
// (exactThrowsAnnotation, and the `annotation == "never"` comparison), pinned by
// 09-foreign-calls/the-never-annotation-is-case-sensitive; the module boundary
// is the same marker and takes the same rule. A JSDoc tag name is not
// case-folded by the fork's own JSDoc parser either, so `@THROWS` and `@MODULE`
// are not the tags the specification names.
//
// The boundary after `@module` stays: `@moduleResolution` is a tag people
// really write and it claims nothing.
func exactModuleMarker(comment string) bool {
	const marker = "@module"
	for offset := 0; offset < len(comment); {
		index := strings.Index(comment[offset:], marker)
		if index < 0 {
			return false
		}
		index += offset
		next := index + len(marker)
		if next == len(comment) || comment[next] == '*' || isJSDocWhitespace(comment[next]) {
			return true
		}
		offset = next
	}
	return false
}

func exactThrowsNeverMarker(comment string) bool {
	const marker = "@throws"
	for offset := 0; offset < len(comment); {
		index := strings.Index(comment[offset:], marker)
		if index < 0 {
			return false
		}
		index += offset
		position := index + len(marker)
		for position < len(comment) && isJSDocWhitespace(comment[position]) {
			position++
		}
		if position >= len(comment) || comment[position] != '{' {
			offset = index + len(marker)
			continue
		}
		position++
		for position < len(comment) && isJSDocWhitespace(comment[position]) {
			position++
		}
		if !strings.HasPrefix(comment[position:], "never") {
			offset = index + len(marker)
			continue
		}
		position += len("never")
		for position < len(comment) && isJSDocWhitespace(comment[position]) {
			position++
		}
		if position < len(comment) && comment[position] == '}' {
			return true
		}
		offset = index + len(marker)
	}
	return false
}

// isJSDocWhitespace is the exact JSDoc whitespace class, and the narrowness is
// the rule rather than an oversight.
//
// The reference frontend used to build these markers out of JavaScript's `\s`,
// which also matches U+00A0, U+000B, U+000C, U+FEFF and every Unicode space
// separator, so it granted trust where this side refused. Eight spellings
// diverged: NBSP inside the braces, NBSP after `@module`, form feed, vertical
// tab, BOM, EN SPACE, IDEOGRAPHIC SPACE and LINE SEPARATOR. THIS side was the
// correct one, by the same argument miscased-trust-markers-do-not-confer-module-trust
// makes: the marker is the exact text the specification prints, and
// `{<NBSP>never<NBSP>}` names a type whose spelling is not `never` — the
// `@throws {T}` production, not the trusted opt-out. Widening the class merges
// the two productions exactly as folding case does. The reference moved to this
// class; all eight spellings were re-measured refusing on both backends.
func isJSDocWhitespace(value byte) bool {
	return value == ' ' || value == '\t' || value == '\r' || value == '\n'
}

// staticRuntimeModuleEdge returns the module specifier whose initializer runs
// for an authored import or re-export. Type-only, compiler-owned, and admitted
// compile-time asset edges are erased and therefore have no trust boundary.
//
// `import x = require("./y")` is one of these edges and used not to be counted.
// It is a THIRD spelling of the same static module load — the value-position
// external-module reference is evaluated exactly when an `import … from` in the
// same slot would be — so leaving it out let an authored `.sm` load an unmarked
// foreign module with no SMITHERS1510 at all, which is the same fail-open shape
// the closure walk closes at depth two. Measured: `import sneaky = require("./sneaky.ts")`
// against an unmarked `sneaky.ts` reported nothing from this rule while the
// reference reported SMITHERS1510 at the specifier. The type-only spelling
// (`import type x = require(…)`) and the namespace-alias form (`import x = A.B`)
// load nothing and are excluded, exactly as the type-only import spellings are.
func (a *rowAnalysis) staticRuntimeModuleEdge(statement *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if statement == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if _, asset := a.assetImports[statement]; asset {
		return nil
	}
	var specifier *ast.Node
	switch {
	case ast.IsImportDeclaration(statement):
		declaration := statement.AsImportDeclaration()
		if importIsTypeOnly(declaration) {
			return nil
		}
		specifier = declaration.ModuleSpecifier
	case ast.IsExportDeclaration(statement):
		declaration := statement.AsExportDeclaration()
		if declaration.ModuleSpecifier == nil || nativeExportIsTypeOnly(declaration) {
			return nil
		}
		specifier = declaration.ModuleSpecifier
	case ast.IsImportEqualsDeclaration(statement):
		specifier = importEqualsModuleSpecifier(statement)
		if specifier == nil {
			return nil
		}
	default:
		return nil
	}
	if a.prelude.isCompilerModuleSpecifier(specifier) && !isPathModuleSpecifier(specifier) {
		return nil
	}
	return specifier
}

// isPathModuleSpecifier reports whether an authored module specifier names a
// FILE — a relative or rooted path — rather than one of the compiler-owned
// module names the language publishes (`smthrs/context`, `smthrs/provider`,
// `smithers:exceptions`, `smithers:comptime`, `smithers:flows`).
//
// The distinction is the form of the specifier, not its spelling: every
// sanctioned compiler-owned name is a bare specifier, and every path is a
// filesystem reference. isCompilerModuleSpecifier resolves by module SYMBOL, and
// that is right for deciding what the lowering must rewrite — but it makes
// `import { SmithersOk } from "./__smithers_prelude.ts"` indistinguishable from
// `import { Context } from "smthrs/context"`, because both resolve to the same
// module. One of those is the language's own name for a compiler-owned module;
// the other is an author reaching into the compiler's emitted file, which is an
// ordinary foreign TypeScript module edge and is owned by the module
// initialization trust rule exactly like any other.
func isPathModuleSpecifier(specifier *ast.Node) bool {
	if specifier == nil || !ast.IsStringLiteral(specifier) {
		return false
	}
	text := specifier.Text()
	return strings.HasPrefix(text, ".") || strings.HasPrefix(text, "/")
}

// compilerOwnedModuleSourceByPath returns the compiler-owned virtual source
// file an authored PATH specifier resolves to, and nil for every other edge.
//
// isForeignDeclaration excludes the compiler-owned virtual files, and it has to:
// the lowering rewrites `smthrs/context` and its siblings onto the prelude, and
// the analysis must not then charge its own rewritten edge. But that exclusion
// also let an author name the prelude by a relative path — the compiler's own
// emitted file — and reach the Result variant constructors it exports, which
// specification/failures.mdx, "Compiler Lifting" (Locked), forbids: "Authors
// MUST NOT need to write Result.ok(...) or Result.err(...). Those constructors
// MUST NOT be part of the ordinary Smithers authoring API."
//
// The compiler's own rewritten edges are already excluded one layer up, by
// SPECIFIER FORM rather than by module identity, so nothing here can charge
// them. What is left is exactly an author's path into a compiler-owned file,
// which is an untrusted TypeScript module edge like any other: the prelude
// carries no leading `@module`/`@throws {never}` claim and cannot be given one.
func (a *rowAnalysis) compilerOwnedModuleSourceByPath(specifier *ast.Node) *ast.SourceFile {
	if !isPathModuleSpecifier(specifier) || !a.prelude.isCompilerModuleSpecifier(specifier) {
		return nil
	}
	module := a.checker.ResolveExternalModuleName(specifier)
	if module == nil {
		return nil
	}
	for _, declaration := range module.Declarations {
		if file := ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(declaration); file != nil {
			return file
		}
	}
	return nil
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) foreignModuleSource(specifier *ast.Node) *ast.SourceFile {
	if specifier == nil {
		return nil
	}
	module := a.checker.ResolveExternalModuleName(specifier)
	if module == nil {
		return nil
	}
	for _, declaration := range module.Declarations {
		if a.isForeignDeclaration(declaration) {
			return ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(declaration)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// checkDynamicModuleImports applies the module-initialization trust rule to the
// dynamic spelling of a FOREIGN module edge.
//
// The rule the diagnostic names is about foreign module INITIALIZATION — an ESM
// initializer can panic before any checked call boundary exists — and that
// hazard is identical whether the edge is spelled `import ... from "./x.ts"` or
// `import("./x.ts")`. A rule about module initialization may not be escapable by
// re-spelling the edge.
//
// It is equally not a licence to refuse dynamic import as such.
// docs/DECISIONS.md:266 is Locked that "arbitrary dynamic import expressions
// remain available", and a `.sm` module in the same project crosses no foreign
// boundary: there is no untrusted initializer, so there is nothing here for this
// rule to be about. Compiler-owned dynamic asset imports are likewise excluded,
// because checkAssetImports already owns and erases those edges before runtime.
//
// A specifier this compiler cannot resolve to a project module fails CLOSED: an
// unresolved or computed target could be any foreign module, and the safe answer
// for an edge whose destination is unknown is the refusal.
func (a *rowAnalysis) checkDynamicModuleImports(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
		visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
			if ast.IsImportCall(node) {
				if _, asset := a.assetImports[node]; asset {
					return false
				}
				target := node
				call := node.AsCallExpression()
				if call.Arguments != nil && len(call.Arguments.Nodes) != 0 {
					target = call.Arguments.Nodes[0]
					if moduleFile := a.foreignModuleSource(target); moduleFile != nil {
						if trustedForeignModule(moduleFile) {
							// A trusted foreign module carries the same initialization
							// claim through either spelling. Whether the dynamic edge may
							// use it at all is the unresolved ledger question
							// (DECISIONS.md:266 versus the SMITHERS1510 model); refusing a
							// module that HAS the claim would answer it here, which this
							// rule may not do.
							//
							// Awaiting this import evaluates the target module and
							// everything ITS evaluation reaches, so the claim it carries is
							// a claim about that whole subgraph exactly as a static edge's
							// is. Remember it as a trust root for the closure walk.
							a.rememberTrustRoot(a.dynamicTrustRoots, file, target, moduleFile)
							return false
						}
						a.refusedForeignModules[moduleFile] = true
					} else if a.projectModuleSource(target) != nil {
						return false
					}
				}
				a.report(target, "SMITHERS1510", "foreign module initialization can panic before a checked call boundary; a dynamically imported module must resolve to a project module or to a declaration carrying a leading @module and @throws {never} trust claim")
				return false
			}
			node.ForEachChild(visit)
			return false
		}
		file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	}
}

// projectModuleSource returns the authored `.sm` source file a specifier
// resolves to, or nil when it resolves to anything else. A project module is
// checked Smithers code: it has no foreign initializer, so the module-trust rule
// has no subject there.
func (a *rowAnalysis) projectModuleSource(specifier *ast.Node) *ast.SourceFile {
	if specifier == nil {
		return nil
	}
	module := a.checker.ResolveExternalModuleName(specifier)
	if module == nil {
		return nil
	}
	for _, declaration := range module.Declarations {
		file := ast.GetSourceFileOfNode(declaration)
		if file == nil {
			continue
		}
		// The same file-identity test isForeignDeclaration uses, read the other
		// way round: an authored project module under the staged root whose name
		// ends in `.sm`.
		name := strings.ToLower(file.FileName())
		if strings.HasPrefix(name, "/src/") && strings.HasSuffix(name, ".sm") {
			return file
		}
	}
	return nil
}

func (a *rowAnalysis) checkForeignModuleTrust(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	for _, file := range files {
		for _, statement := range file.Statements.Nodes {
			specifier := a.staticRuntimeModuleEdge(statement)
			if specifier == nil {
				continue
			}
			module := a.checker.ResolveExternalModuleName(specifier)
			if module == nil {
				// An unresolved runtime import cannot carry the leading module
				// initialization trust declaration. Compiler modules were already
				// excluded by resolved module identity above. A missing relative
				// Smithers module is instead owned by SMITHERS1801, because it is
				// a missing checked-closure edge rather than a foreign package.
				if ast.IsStringLiteral(specifier) {
					text := specifier.Text()
					if strings.HasPrefix(text, ".") && strings.HasSuffix(text, ".sm") {
						continue
					}
				}
				a.report(specifier, "SMITHERS1510", "foreign module initialization can panic before a checked call boundary; the module could not be resolved to a declaration carrying a leading @module and @throws {never} trust claim")
				continue
			}
			target := a.foreignModuleSource(specifier)
			if target == nil {
				target = a.compilerOwnedModuleSourceByPath(specifier)
			}
			if target != nil && !trustedForeignModule(target) {
				a.refusedForeignModules[target] = true
				a.report(specifier, "SMITHERS1510", "statically loaded TypeScript/JavaScript module does not declare a leading @module and @throws {never} initialization trust claim")
				continue
			}
			if target != nil {
				// The claim this module just made is a claim about its own
				// initialization, and its initialization runs everything it reaches.
				// Remember the edge so walkReachedForeignModules can ask the rest of
				// that closure for the same claim.
				a.rememberTrustRoot(a.staticTrustRoots, file, specifier, target)
			}
		}
	}
}

// moduleTrustRoot is one TRUSTED foreign module a `.sm` loads directly, paired
// with the authored specifier that reached it. Every transitive refusal is
// reported against `site`: it is the only text in the authored file its author
// can change, and it is the position the corpus's other SMITHERS1510 cases
// already declare.
type moduleTrustRoot struct {
	site   *ast.Node
	named  string
	target *ast.SourceFile
}

// rememberTrustRoot seeds the closure walk, and ONLY on a relative specifier.
//
// The closure IS the relative runtime graph. A specifier that is not relative
// names code this compilation did not resolve, place or emit, and applying the
// rule to it makes this compiler responsible for a whole foreign subtree it
// does not own. The reference lane measured what happens when the seed is wider:
// seeding from every trusted depth-one target turned `capability-seams.test.ts`
// SEAM 3 red, because a `.sm` that imports the compiler's own
// `runtime/introspection.ts` — which carries the module claim precisely so
// authored `.sm` may reach the brand seam — reaches `runtime/result.ts` and
// `runtime/panic.ts`, which deliberately carry NO claim. Their unmarkedness is
// the forgery guarantee recorded in `introspection.ts`'s own header: marking
// them would put a `Result` variant constructor one import away from authored
// `.sm`. The runtime seam is reached by an ABSOLUTE path, so narrowing the seed
// to relative specifiers is what keeps that guarantee intact.
//
// The depth-one marker check above is untouched by this narrowing and still
// interrogates every resolvable target, relative or not; what a non-relative
// edge does not do is seed a closure.
//
// `isPathModuleSpecifier` is deliberately NOT the test: it also admits a rooted
// `/…` path, and the reference seeds only on a `.`-prefixed specifier.
func (a *rowAnalysis) rememberTrustRoot(
	into map[*ast.SourceFile][]moduleTrustRoot,
	file *ast.SourceFile,
	specifier *ast.Node,
	target *ast.SourceFile,
) {
	if file == nil || specifier == nil || target == nil || !ast.IsStringLiteral(specifier) {
		return
	}
	text := specifier.Text()
	if !strings.HasPrefix(text, ".") {
		return
	}
	into[file] = append(into[file], moduleTrustRoot{site: specifier, named: "'" + text + "'", target: target})
}

// walkReachedForeignModules asks every module the trusted roots REACH for the
// same marker the roots carried, and refuses the ones that do not have it.
//
// The defect this closes was measured with a runtime oracle on both backends: a
// properly marked `relay.ts` doing `export { danger } from "./sneaky.ts"`, where
// `sneaky.ts` carries no marker at all, compiled clean and RAN the untrusted
// initializer at depth 2 and at depth 3, because checkForeignModuleTrust only
// ever iterated the authored `.sm`'s own statements. A rule about module
// INITIALIZATION cannot stop at depth one: module evaluation does not.
//
// `answered` spans all of one file's roots, so a module reached through two
// paths (a diamond) is decided once and reported once, against the first
// authored edge that reaches it, and a cycle terminates for the same reason. The
// walk is breadth-first in source order at every level, so the diagnostics a
// program produces do not depend on map iteration order.
//
// Three edges deliberately END the walk rather than continue it, and each
// matches a boundary the relative runtime graph already draws:
//
//   - a COMPILER-OWNED specifier is compiler-owned and has no foreign
//     initializer — and every sanctioned compiler-owned name is bare, so the
//     relative-only filter below already excludes it;
//   - a `.sm` module is a project module this same pass checks in its own right,
//     and `foreignModuleSource` already declines to name one;
//   - a NON-RELATIVE specifier (`node:fs`, a package, a bare name) is outside
//     the relative runtime graph entirely, for the reason `rememberTrustRoot`
//     records.
//
// A RELATIVE initialization edge whose destination this compiler cannot resolve
// is REFUSED, not skipped. That is not a new rule: it is the fork's own
// "unresolved runtime import cannot carry the trust claim" branch above, applied
// at the depth the author cannot see.
//
// One thing this deliberately does NOT do: an untrusted module found at depth
// one is refused and not walked into. Only trusted targets become roots, so the
// program is already rejected at the edge the author wrote and cascading that
// module's own dependencies would bury the one fixable line.
//
// This is a port of `walkReachedForeignModules` in `poc/src/language/semantic.ts`,
// which is in turn the rule `src/relative-runtime-graph.ts` already had. Keep
// the three structurally identical; if one moves, move the others.
func (a *rowAnalysis) walkReachedForeignModules(files []*ast.SourceFile) {
	type frame struct {
		file *ast.SourceFile
		via  []*ast.SourceFile
	}
	for _, file := range files {
		roots := append(append([]moduleTrustRoot(nil), a.staticTrustRoots[file]...), a.dynamicTrustRoots[file]...)
		if len(roots) == 0 {
			continue
		}
		answered := make(map[*ast.SourceFile]bool, len(roots))
		for _, root := range roots {
			answered[root.target] = true
		}
		for _, root := range roots {
			refuseReached := func(reached string, fault string, via []*ast.SourceFile) {
				through := ""
				if len(via) != 0 {
					names := make([]string, 0, len(via))
					for _, item := range via {
						names = append(names, item.FileName())
					}
					through = " (through " + strings.Join(names, " -> ") + ")"
				}
				a.report(root.site, "SMITHERS1510",
					"foreign module initialization can panic before a checked call boundary; "+
						root.named+" loads "+reached+" during module initialization"+through+", and "+fault+
						"; use a type-only import, add the trusted marker, or put dynamic import behind a checked async foreign adapter")
			}
			queue := []frame{{file: root.target}}
			for len(queue) != 0 {
				current := queue[0]
				queue = queue[1:]
				nextVia := append(append([]*ast.SourceFile(nil), current.via...), current.file)
				for _, specifier := range a.foreignModuleInitializationEdges(current.file) {
					text := specifier.Text()
					if !strings.HasPrefix(text, ".") {
						continue
					}
					if a.checker.ResolveExternalModuleName(specifier) == nil {
						refuseReached("'"+text+"'",
							"that specifier could not be resolved to a declaration carrying a leading @module and @throws {never} trust claim",
							current.via)
						continue
					}
					target := a.foreignModuleSource(specifier)
					if target == nil {
						target = a.compilerOwnedModuleSourceByPath(specifier)
					}
					if target == nil {
						// Resolved, but not a foreign module this rule owns: a `.sm`
						// project module, which this pass checks in its own right.
						continue
					}
					if answered[target] {
						continue
					}
					answered[target] = true
					if !trustedForeignModule(target) {
						a.refusedForeignModules[target] = true
						refuseReached(target.FileName(),
							"that module does not declare a leading @module and @throws {never} initialization trust claim",
							current.via)
						continue
					}
					queue = append(queue, frame{file: target, via: nextVia})
				}
			}
		}
	}
}

// foreignModuleInitializationEdges are the specifiers ONE FOREIGN MODULE's own
// evaluation reaches.
//
// The static forms are the three `staticRuntimeModuleEdge` recognizes, minus its
// restriction to top-level statements: a foreign module may write
// `import`/`export … from` only at module scope anyway, but an
// `import x = require(…)` inside a namespace is still evaluated on load. The
// type-only spellings are excluded for the reason they are excluded there — an
// erased edge has no runtime at all.
//
// `import()` and `require()` are governed by moduleInitializationClassifier,
// whose default is "initialization" and whose "deferred" answer must be proven.
// An import call carrying import attributes is an asset request and belongs to
// the source-asset pass.
func (a *rowAnalysis) foreignModuleInitializationEdges(file *ast.SourceFile) []*ast.Node {
	if file == nil {
		return nil
	}
	classifier := newModuleInitializationClassifier(file)
	specifiers := make([]*ast.Node, 0, 4)
	appendStringLiteral := func(node *ast.Node) {
		if node != nil && ast.IsStringLiteral(node) {
			specifiers = append(specifiers, node)
		}
	}
	var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
	visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
		switch {
		case ast.IsImportDeclaration(node):
			declaration := node.AsImportDeclaration()
			if !importIsTypeOnly(declaration) {
				appendStringLiteral(declaration.ModuleSpecifier)
			}
		case ast.IsExportDeclaration(node):
			declaration := node.AsExportDeclaration()
			if declaration.ModuleSpecifier != nil && !nativeExportIsTypeOnly(declaration) {
				appendStringLiteral(declaration.ModuleSpecifier)
			}
		case ast.IsImportEqualsDeclaration(node):
			appendStringLiteral(importEqualsModuleSpecifier(node))
		case ast.IsImportCall(node):
			call := node.AsCallExpression()
			if call.Arguments != nil && len(call.Arguments.Nodes) == 1 &&
				classifier.isModuleInitializationEdge(node) {
				appendStringLiteral(call.Arguments.Nodes[0])
			}
		case ast.IsRequireCall(node, false):
			call := node.AsCallExpression()
			if classifier.isModuleInitializationEdge(node) {
				appendStringLiteral(call.Arguments.Nodes[0])
			}
		}
		node.ForEachChild(visit)
		return false
	}
	file.AsNode().ForEachChild(visit)
	sort.SliceStable(specifiers, func(left, right int) bool {
		return specifiers[left].Pos() < specifiers[right].Pos()
	})
	return specifiers
}

// importEqualsModuleSpecifier returns the string literal of a value-position
// `import x = require("…")`, and nil for the type-only spelling or for the
// `import x = A.B` namespace-alias form, neither of which loads a module.
func importEqualsModuleSpecifier(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if node == nil || !ast.IsImportEqualsDeclaration(node) {
		return nil
	}
	declaration := node.AsImportEqualsDeclaration()
	if declaration.IsTypeOnly || declaration.ModuleReference == nil ||
		!ast.IsExternalModuleReference(declaration.ModuleReference) {
		return nil
	}
	return declaration.ModuleReference.AsExternalModuleReference().Expression
}

// moduleInitializationClassifier decides which `import()`/`require()` edges of
// ONE foreign module are evaluated while that module is being evaluated. The
// answer defaults to YES, and "no" has to be proven.
//
// This flag decides which reached modules must carry the initialization trust
// marker, so an edge misclassified as deferred is an untrusted foreign
// initializer running with no diagnostic. "Lexically inside a function" is not
// the question; "can module evaluation reach this" is —
// `const l = (() => require(…))()`, a named local function called at module
// scope, and a module-scope getter read were all measured checking clean and
// running the untrusted initializer when the question was asked the other way.
//
// The proof accepted here is deliberately narrow, because the walk has no
// cross-module information — a function defers its body only when module-scope
// code cannot get hold of the function value at all:
//
//   - the function literal must BE a module-scope `function` declaration, a
//     member of a module-scope `class` declaration, or the entire initializer of
//     a module-scope `const`/`let`/`var` binding — anything else (an IIFE, an
//     array element, an object property, an argument) hands the value to
//     module-scope code, which may call it;
//   - that declaration must be exported, so the module is written to be driven
//     from outside; and
//   - none of the names it declares may be MENTIONED by module-scope code, since
//     a mention is enough to call it.
//
// Everything else is an initialization edge. That deliberately refuses some
// genuinely deferred shapes — a method reached only through a non-exported
// factory, `module.exports = { load: () => require(…) }`, a lazily read property
// of an exported object literal — and the escape hatch for each is the one the
// diagnostic already names: mark the reached module, or load it through a
// checked async foreign adapter. Refusing a deferred edge asks for a marker;
// admitting an initialization edge runs untrusted code, so the asymmetry is the
// whole point.
//
// Note also that only a function's BODY and its parameter defaults are evaluated
// when it is called. Its decorators, computed member name and type annotations
// are evaluated where the function is written, so an edge sitting in one of those
// is at module scope even though it is lexically "inside a function", and the
// climb below keeps walking outward for exactly that case.
//
// This is a deliberate mirror of `moduleInitializationClassifier` in
// `poc/src/language/semantic.ts`, which is itself a mirror of the one in
// `src/relative-runtime-graph.ts`, kept structurally identical so the three diff
// cleanly. If one side moves, move the others in the same shape.
type moduleInitializationClassifier struct {
	file             *ast.SourceFile
	exportedByClause map[string]bool
	moduleScopeUses  map[string]bool
	deferred         map[*ast.Node]bool
}

func newModuleInitializationClassifier(file *ast.SourceFile) *moduleInitializationClassifier {
	classifier := &moduleInitializationClassifier{
		file:             file,
		exportedByClause: make(map[string]bool),
		deferred:         make(map[*ast.Node]bool),
	}
	for _, statement := range file.Statements.Nodes {
		if !ast.IsExportDeclaration(statement) {
			continue
		}
		declaration := statement.AsExportDeclaration()
		if declaration.IsTypeOnly || declaration.ModuleSpecifier != nil || declaration.ExportClause == nil ||
			!ast.IsNamedExports(declaration.ExportClause) {
			continue
		}
		elements := declaration.ExportClause.AsNamedExports().Elements
		if elements == nil {
			continue
		}
		for _, element := range elements.Nodes {
			specifier := element.AsExportSpecifier()
			if specifier.IsTypeOnly {
				continue
			}
			name := specifier.PropertyName
			if name == nil {
				name = specifier.Name()
			}
			if name != nil {
				classifier.exportedByClause[name.Text()] = true
			}
		}
	}
	return classifier
}

// collectModuleScopeUses names every identifier module-scope code MENTIONS.
// Function bodies are skipped — they are the thing being deferred — but nothing
// else is, so a decorator, a computed member name or a type annotation written
// on a function still counts as module scope.
func (c *moduleInitializationClassifier) collectModuleScopeUses() map[string]bool {
	names := make(map[string]bool)
	var visit func(*ast.Node) bool
	visit = func(node *ast.Node) bool {
		// A type position is erased before anything runs, and an import or export
		// clause names a binding rather than calling it.
		if ast.IsTypeNode(node) || ast.IsTypeAliasDeclaration(node) || ast.IsInterfaceDeclaration(node) ||
			ast.IsImportDeclaration(node) || ast.IsImportEqualsDeclaration(node) || ast.IsExportDeclaration(node) {
			return false
		}
		if ast.IsIdentifier(node) {
			parent := node.Parent
			// `{ load }` is a reference written in name position; every other
			// `name`/`propertyName` slot is a declaration or a member label.
			if parent == nil || ast.IsShorthandPropertyAssignment(parent) ||
				(parent.Name() != node && declarationPropertyName(parent) != node) {
				names[node.Text()] = true
			}
			return false
		}
		body := node.Body()
		functionLike := ast.IsFunctionLike(node)
		node.ForEachChild(func(child *ast.Node) bool {
			if functionLike && child == body {
				return false
			}
			visit(child)
			return false
		})
		return false
	}
	visit(c.file.AsNode())
	return names
}

func (c *moduleInitializationClassifier) isProvablyDeferred(fn *ast.Node) bool {
	if cached, known := c.deferred[fn]; known {
		return cached
	}
	c.deferred[fn] = false
	parent := fn.Parent
	var statement *ast.Node
	switch {
	case ast.IsFunctionDeclaration(fn):
		statement = fn
	case parent != nil && ast.IsClassDeclaration(parent):
		statement = parent
	case parent != nil && ast.IsVariableDeclaration(parent) &&
		parent.AsVariableDeclaration().Initializer == fn &&
		parent.AsVariableDeclaration().Name() != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(parent.AsVariableDeclaration().Name()):
		if parent.Parent != nil {
			statement = parent.Parent.Parent
		}
	}
	if statement == nil || statement.Parent == nil || !ast.IsSourceFile(statement.Parent) {
		return false
	}
	names := declaredModuleScopeValueNames(statement)
	exported := ast.HasSyntacticModifier(statement, ast.ModifierFlagsExport)
	if !exported {
		for _, name := range names {
			if c.exportedByClause[name] {
				exported = true
				break
			}
		}
	}
	if !exported {
		return false
	}
	if c.moduleScopeUses == nil {
		c.moduleScopeUses = c.collectModuleScopeUses()
	}
	for _, name := range names {
		if c.moduleScopeUses[name] {
			return false
		}
	}
	c.deferred[fn] = true
	return true
}

func (c *moduleInitializationClassifier) isModuleInitializationEdge(node *ast.Node) bool {
	child := node
	current := node.Parent
	for current != nil {
		if ast.IsFunctionLike(current) {
			evaluatedOnCall := child == current.Body()
			if !evaluatedOnCall && ast.IsParameterDeclaration(child) {
				if data := current.FunctionLikeData(); data != nil && data.Parameters != nil {
					for _, parameter := range data.Parameters.Nodes {
						if parameter == child {
							evaluatedOnCall = true
							break
						}
					}
				}
			}
			if evaluatedOnCall && c.isProvablyDeferred(current) {
				return false
			}
		}
		child = current
		current = current.Parent
	}
	return true
}

// declaredModuleScopeValueNames are the value names one module-scope statement
// introduces, when they are plain identifiers.
func declaredModuleScopeValueNames(statement *ast.Node) []string {
	if ast.IsFunctionDeclaration(statement) || ast.IsClassDeclaration(statement) {
		if name := statement.Name(); name != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(name) {
			return []string{name.Text()}
		}
		return nil
	}
	if ast.IsVariableStatement(statement) {
		list := statement.AsVariableStatement().DeclarationList
		if list == nil || !ast.IsVariableDeclarationList(list) {
			return nil
		}
		declarations := list.AsVariableDeclarationList().Declarations
		if declarations == nil {
			return nil
		}
		names := make([]string, 0, len(declarations.Nodes))
		for _, declaration := range declarations.Nodes {
			if name := declaration.AsVariableDeclaration().Name(); name != nil && ast.IsIdentifier(name) {
				names = append(names, name.Text())
			}
		}
		return names
	}
	return nil
}

// declarationPropertyName is the `propertyName` slot of the three node kinds
// that have one, for the "is this identifier a reference or a label" test above.
func declarationPropertyName(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	switch node.Kind {
	case ast.KindBindingElement:
		return node.AsBindingElement().PropertyName
	case ast.KindImportSpecifier:
		return node.AsImportSpecifier().PropertyName
	case ast.KindExportSpecifier:
		return node.AsExportSpecifier().PropertyName
	}
	return nil
}

// lowerer rewrites one authored `.sm` source file.
type lowerer struct {
	emitContext *printer.EmitContext
	factory     *printer.NodeFactory
	visitor     *ast.NodeVisitor
	checker     *checker.Checker
	prelude     *preludeSymbols
	analysis    *rowAnalysis
	comptime    *comptimeAnalysis
	file        *ast.SourceFile

	shape               liftShape
	canHoist            bool
	impure              bool
	guardedByCatch      bool
	pending             []*ast.Node
	nextTemp            int
	used                map[string]bool
	reports             []loweringReport
	needsPreludeRuntime bool

	// identities is the COMPILE-WIDE nominal Error identity claim map: minted
	// identity -> "<logical file>:<class name>" of the declaration holding it.
	// One map is shared by every lowerer in a compilation, because one of the two
	// collisions this guards spans two modules. See claimNominalErrorIdentity.
	identities map[string]string
}

func newLowerer(typeChecker *checker.Checker, prelude *preludeSymbols, analysis *rowAnalysis, comptime *comptimeAnalysis, identities map[string]string) *lowerer {
	low := &lowerer{
		emitContext: printer.NewEmitContext(),
		checker:     typeChecker,
		prelude:     prelude,
		analysis:    analysis,
		comptime:    comptime,
		used:        make(map[string]bool),
		identities:  identities,
	}
	low.factory = low.emitContext.Factory
	low.visitor = low.emitContext.NewNodeVisitor(low.visit)
	return low
}

// hoistableStatements are the statement kinds where a statement introduced
// before the statement runs exactly once, in the same order, as the expression
// it was extracted from.
//
// This is NO LONGER the propagation placement rule. specification/failures.mdx
// §Refusal Conditions withdrew the statement-walk that consulted this table, and
// `conditionallyEvaluatedPosition`/`repeatedlyEvaluatedPosition`/
// `precededByUnhoistedEffect` replaced it; a propagation's guard is flushed by
// `visitStatement` in front of whatever statement encloses it, including the
// kinds absent here. What remains keyed on this table is `l.canHoist`, and the
// only reader of `l.canHoist` is `matchScrutinee`, which binds a NEW temporary
// for a computed `Error.match` receiver and needs a statement it may legally
// precede. Widening it would widen that rule and nothing else.
//
//   - A `switch` discriminant is evaluated exactly once, before any clause, so a
//     statement hoisted in front of the `switch` preserves the authored order.
//   - A `for` INITIALIZER runs exactly once, before the loop; its condition and
//     incrementor run once per iteration and are refused separately by
//     `repeatedlyEvaluatedPosition` (SMITHERS1703), so admitting the statement
//     kind here cannot admit a repeated header.
var hoistableStatements = map[ast.Kind]struct{}{
	ast.KindVariableStatement:   {},
	ast.KindReturnStatement:     {},
	ast.KindExpressionStatement: {},
	ast.KindThrowStatement:      {},
	ast.KindIfStatement:         {},
	ast.KindSwitchStatement:     {},
	ast.KindForStatement:        {},
}

func (l *lowerer) transform(file *ast.SourceFile) *ast.SourceFile {
	l.file = file
	lowered := l.visitor.VisitSourceFile(file)
	if len(l.used) == 0 && !l.needsPreludeRuntime {
		return lowered
	}
	importDeclaration := l.newPreludeImport(file.FileName())
	statements := append([]*ast.Node{importDeclaration}, lowered.Statements.Nodes...)
	list := l.factory.NewNodeList(statements)
	list.Loc = lowered.Statements.Loc
	return l.factory.UpdateSourceFile(lowered, list, lowered.EndOfFileToken).AsSourceFile()
}

// newPreludeImport builds `import { SmithersOk as __smithersOk, … } from
// "<relative>/__smithers_prelude.js"` over exactly the exports this file needed.
// The node is synthesized, so it carries no authored position and stays
// unmapped in the emitted source map.
func (l *lowerer) newPreludeImport(authoredName string) *ast.Node {
	specifiers := make([]*ast.Node, 0, len(preludeImports))
	for _, entry := range preludeImports {
		if !l.used[entry.exported] {
			continue
		}
		specifiers = append(specifiers, l.factory.NewImportSpecifier(
			entry.typeOnly,
			l.factory.NewIdentifier(entry.exported),
			l.factory.NewIdentifier(entry.local),
		))
	}
	specifier := l.factory.NewStringLiteral(preludeSpecifierFor(authoredName), ast.TokenFlagsNone)
	if len(specifiers) == 0 {
		return l.factory.NewImportDeclaration(nil, nil, specifier, nil)
	}
	namedImports := l.factory.NewNamedImports(l.factory.NewNodeList(specifiers))
	clause := l.factory.NewImportClause(ast.KindUnknown, nil, namedImports)
	return l.factory.NewImportDeclaration(nil, clause, specifier, nil)
}

// preludeSpecifierFor computes the relative module specifier from one virtual
// project file to the injected prelude.
func preludeSpecifierFor(virtualName string) string {
	directory := path.Dir(virtualName)
	rest := strings.Trim(strings.TrimPrefix(directory, "/src"), "/")
	if rest == "" {
		return "./" + preludeModuleBase
	}
	return strings.Repeat("../", strings.Count(rest, "/")+1) + preludeModuleBase
}

// stableErrorIdentity mints the compiler-owned nominal identity of one Error
// class: "smithers:<module path>:<class name>".
//
// specification/failures.mdx, "Error Classes", puts this on the compiler —
// "Authors MUST NOT need a 'TaggedError("Name")' factory, '_tag' declaration, or
// separate error-declaration syntax. The compiler MUST provide stable nominal
// identity" — and "Error Prototype" says why it may not be an author string:
// "Handler selection MUST use compiler-stable nominal identity, not a forgeable
// user '_tag' or minifier-sensitive constructor name in compiled artifacts."
// There is deliberately no author-facing spelling.
//
// sourceName is the module's project-relative path, so two same-named classes
// in two modules mint two different identities and cannot collapse, while one
// class keeps the same identity no matter where the compiler was invoked from.
//
// Every step is defined on UTF-16 code units because the reference frontend's
// nominalErrorIdentity is JavaScript string surgery, and the two identities must
// be the same string for the same class or the wire is not interoperable. A
// non-BMP character therefore contributes two escapes rather than one, and the
// bound is 256 units rather than 256 runes. The two implementations are pinned
// against each other by the shared vectors in
// conformance/identity/nominal-error-identity.json, which both backends read;
// they are NOT maintained by reading one another.
//
// THE INJECTIVITY RULE. Until 2026-08-28 this function normalized the path by
// rewriting every unit outside [A-Za-z0-9._/@:+-] to '_', prefixed "source_"
// when the result did not start alphanumerically, and then truncated the whole
// identity — class name included — to 256 units. All three steps are many-to-one
// and all three were measured minting ONE identity for TWO distinct classes on
// this backend, with zero diagnostics:
//
//	"a".repeat(250)+".sm" / Left  and  .../Right  -> one 256-unit identity
//	a b.sm/Boom           and  a_b.sm/Boom        -> smithers:a_b.sm:Boom
//	.a.sm/Boom            and  source_.a.sm/Boom  -> smithers:source_.a.sm:Boom
//
// That is a fail-OPEN: the compiler accepted the program, emitted a plausible
// artifact, and the artifact threw "stable Error identity ... is already
// registered" out of smithersRegisterError while it was still loading. So no
// step here may destroy information. The path is escaped REVERSIBLY (a unit
// outside the alphabet, and any unit at index 0 that is not alphanumeric,
// becomes '+' followed by exactly four upper-case hex digits; '+' is withheld
// from the alphabet so it can introduce an escape and nothing else), and the
// bound is honoured by DIGESTING the exact spelling rather than by cutting it.
//
// "smithers.digest:" cannot be confused with the ordinary "smithers:" spelling —
// the ninth unit is '.' in one and ':' in the other — and the ordinary spelling
// is itself injective over the pair, because a class name is a TypeScript
// identifier and so contains no ':', which makes the last ':' an unambiguous
// separator. A surrogate is outside the alphabet, so no lone surrogate can reach
// the output and the old trailing-surrogate trim is no longer reachable.
func stableErrorIdentity(sourceName string, name string) string {
	units := utf16.Encode([]rune("smithers:"))
	for index, unit := range utf16.Encode([]rune(sourceName)) {
		verbatim := isStableIdentityAlphanumeric(unit)
		if index > 0 {
			verbatim = isEscapedIdentityPathUnit(unit)
		}
		if verbatim {
			units = append(units, unit)
			continue
		}
		units = append(units, utf16.Encode([]rune(fmt.Sprintf("+%04X", unit)))...)
	}
	units = append(units, utf16.Encode([]rune(":"+name))...)
	spelled := string(utf16.Decode(units))
	if len(units) <= 256 {
		return spelled
	}
	// Digesting the SPELLING rather than the pair is what makes the fallback
	// injective for free: the spelling is already injective over (file, name), so
	// the digest inherits that up to SHA-256 collision resistance. []byte(spelled)
	// is the same byte sequence Node's update(..., "utf8") hashes.
	return fmt.Sprintf("smithers.digest:%x", sha256.Sum256([]byte(spelled)))
}

// isEscapedIdentityPathUnit is [A-Za-z0-9._/@:-] — the identity alphabet MINUS
// '+'. Withholding '+' is what makes stableErrorIdentity's encoding reversible:
// an unescaped '+' can never occur, so every '+' in the output starts exactly
// one five-unit escape.
//
// It is deliberately NOT isDurableIdentityUnit (durable.go.txt), which withholds
// '@' as well because the CONTRACT spelling separates its two components with
// '@' rather than with ':'.
//
// isStableIdentityPathUnit, the /[^A-Za-z0-9._\/@:+-]/ complement this file used
// to carry beside these two, is gone: durableFailureIdentity was its last caller
// and it no longer folds against an alphabet.
func isEscapedIdentityPathUnit(unit uint16) bool {
	if isStableIdentityAlphanumeric(unit) {
		return true
	}
	switch unit {
	case '.', '_', '/', '@', ':', '-':
		return true
	}
	return false
}

func isStableIdentityAlphanumeric(unit uint16) bool {
	return (unit >= 'A' && unit <= 'Z') || (unit >= 'a' && unit <= 'z') || (unit >= '0' && unit <= '9')
}

func (l *lowerer) report(node *ast.Node, code string, message string) {
	start := node.Pos()
	if l.file != nil {
		start = scanner.SkipTrivia(l.file.Text(), start)
	}
	length := node.End() - start
	if length < 0 {
		length = 0
	}
	l.reports = append(l.reports, loweringReport{code: code, message: message, start: start, length: length})
}

type lowererScope struct {
	shape          liftShape
	canHoist       bool
	impure         bool
	guardedByCatch bool
	pending        []*ast.Node
}

func (l *lowerer) saveScope() lowererScope {
	return lowererScope{
		shape:          l.shape,
		canHoist:       l.canHoist,
		impure:         l.impure,
		guardedByCatch: l.guardedByCatch,
		pending:        l.pending,
	}
}

func (l *lowerer) restoreScope(saved lowererScope) {
	l.shape = saved.shape
	l.canHoist = saved.canHoist
	l.impure = saved.impure
	l.guardedByCatch = saved.guardedByCatch
	l.pending = saved.pending
}

func (l *lowerer) visit(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if node == nil {
		return nil
	}
	// The comptime frontend decided everything about this node before the
	// visitor ran. Substituting here — and returning without descending —
	// is what makes an unselected `comptime.target` branch, an erased
	// compile-time function body, and the compiler-owned import all disappear
	// from the emitted artifact rather than being visited as runtime code.
	if replacement, owned := l.comptimeSubstitution(node); owned {
		return replacement
	}
	if ast.IsFunctionLike(node) {
		saved := l.saveScope()
		l.shape = l.shapeOf(node)
		l.canHoist = false
		l.impure = false
		l.guardedByCatch = false
		l.pending = nil
		var result *ast.Node
		conciseArrow := ast.IsArrowFunction(node) && node.Body() != nil && node.Body().Kind != ast.KindBlock
		switch {
		case conciseArrow && !(l.shape.result && l.shape.panics) && l.prelude.isPanicCall(node.Body()):
			// `(m: string): never => panic(m)` — an abort written as the whole
			// body of an arrow. It is one of the exit positions the reference
			// frontend accepts (`isSimplePanicExit`), and with a plain return
			// type there is no Result value to build, so it becomes a throwing
			// block body rather than a SMITHERS1503 placement refusal.
			result = l.lowerConcisePanicBody(node)
		case l.shape.lifts() && conciseArrow:
			result = l.lowerConciseBody(node)
		default:
			result = l.visitor.VisitEachChild(node)
		}
		l.restoreScope(saved)
		return result
	}
	if node.Kind == ast.KindBlock {
		return l.visitBlock(node)
	}
	if ast.IsStatement(node) {
		return l.visitStatement(node)
	}
	return l.visitExpression(node)
}

func (l *lowerer) visitBlock(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	block := node.AsBlock()
	statements := l.visitStatementSequence(block.Statements.Nodes)
	list := l.factory.NewNodeList(statements)
	list.Loc = block.Statements.Loc
	return l.factory.UpdateBlock(block, list, block.MultiLine)
}

// visitStatementSequence lowers each statement in source order.
func (l *lowerer) visitStatementSequence(statements []*ast.Node) []*ast.Node {
	result := make([]*ast.Node, 0, len(statements))
	for _, statement := range statements {
		result = appendVisitedStatement(result, l.visit(statement))
	}
	return result
}

func appendVisitedStatement(result []*ast.Node, visited *ast.Node) []*ast.Node {
	if visited == nil {
		return result
	}
	if visited.Kind == ast.KindSyntaxList {
		return append(result, visited.AsSyntaxList().Children...)
	}
	return append(result, visited)
}

// shapeOf derives the checked return contract from the declared return type
// annotation. `Promise` is only looked through for an `async` function, so a
// plain function that hands back a promise value is never lifted.
func (l *lowerer) shapeOf(node *ast.Node) liftShape {
	if l.analysis != nil {
		if fn := l.analysis.byNode[node]; fn != nil && fn.inferredFallible() {
			return liftShape{
				async:  ast.HasSyntacticModifier(node, ast.ModifierFlagsAsync),
				result: true,
				panics: l.rowNamesPanic(node),
			}
		}
	}
	typeNode := node.Type()
	if typeNode == nil {
		return liftShape{}
	}
	shape := liftShape{}
	if ast.HasSyntacticModifier(node, ast.ModifierFlagsAsync) {
		inner := l.promiseArgument(typeNode)
		if inner == nil {
			return liftShape{}
		}
		typeNode = inner
		shape.async = true
	}
	shape.result = l.classifyTypeNode(typeNode)
	if !shape.lifts() {
		return liftShape{}
	}
	shape.panics = l.rowNamesPanic(node)
	return shape
}

// rowNamesPanic reports whether this function's published failure row contains
// the distinguished `Panic` case. See liftShape.panics.
func (l *lowerer) rowNamesPanic(node *ast.Node) bool {
	if l.analysis == nil || l.prelude == nil || l.prelude.panic == nil {
		return false
	}
	fn := l.analysis.byNode[node]
	if fn == nil {
		return false
	}
	_, named := fn.failures[rowKey{symbol: l.analysis.canonical(l.prelude.panic)}]
	return named
}

// promiseArgument returns the awaited type node of a `Promise<T>` annotation.
// Promise identity comes from the checker's own definition of a promise, not
// from the spelling of the type name.
func (l *lowerer) promiseArgument(typeNode *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	if !ast.IsTypeReferenceNode(typeNode) {
		return nil
	}
	reference := typeNode.AsTypeReferenceNode()
	if reference.TypeArguments == nil || len(reference.TypeArguments.Nodes) != 1 {
		return nil
	}
	if l.checker.GetPromisedTypeOfPromise(l.checker.GetTypeFromTypeNode(typeNode)) == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return reference.TypeArguments.Nodes[0]
}

// classifyTypeNode resolves an annotation to the compiler-owned Result by
// symbol identity.
func (l *lowerer) classifyTypeNode(typeNode *ast.Node) bool {
	if typeNode == nil || !ast.IsTypeReferenceNode(typeNode) {
		return false
	}
	reference := typeNode.AsTypeReferenceNode()
	symbol := l.checker.GetSymbolAtLocation(reference.TypeName)
	return l.prelude.isResultSymbol(symbol)
}

// lowerConciseBody wraps the expression body of a Result-returning
// arrow function, so `(): Result<A, E> => value` becomes `=> new __smithersOk(value)`.
// A propagation point in that body hoists its guard into a block body
// SYNTHESISED here — `=> { const t = …; if (!t.ok) return t; return Ok(t.value) }`
// — which is the whole of what "the guard is hoisted into the nearest statement
// or arrow function" costs to implement, and what the withdrawn placement walk
// used to refuse on its behalf.
//
// lowerConcisePanicBody replaces `(...) => panic(cause)` with a block body that
// throws the constructed Panic. See lowerPanicThrow for why the call cannot
// simply be left in place.
func (l *lowerer) lowerConcisePanicBody(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	arrow := node.AsArrowFunction()
	original := arrow.Body
	thrown := l.factory.NewThrowStatement(l.panicValue(original))
	l.emitContext.AssignCommentAndSourceMapRanges(thrown, original)
	l.emitContext.SetOriginal(thrown, original)
	body := l.factory.NewBlock(l.factory.NewNodeList([]*ast.Node{thrown}), true)
	l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(body, original)
	return l.factory.UpdateArrowFunction(
		arrow,
		l.visitor.VisitModifiers(arrow.Modifiers()),
		arrow.TypeParameters,
		l.visitor.VisitNodes(arrow.Parameters),
		arrow.Type,
		arrow.FullSignature,
		arrow.EqualsGreaterThanToken,
		body,
	)
}

func (l *lowerer) lowerConciseBody(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	arrow := node.AsArrowFunction()
	original := arrow.Body
	body := l.visitor.VisitNode(original)
	lifted := l.liftValue(original, body)
	if lifted != body {
		l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(lifted, original)
	}
	loweredBody := lifted
	if len(l.pending) != 0 {
		// The guards a propagation point in this body hoisted have nowhere to go
		// in a concise body, so the concise body becomes a block one. The
		// authored expression stays the returned value, which keeps the arrow's
		// declared channel — the one condition 3 asks about — the channel the
		// early return exits through.
		returned := l.factory.NewReturnStatement(lifted)
		l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(returned, original)
		l.emitContext.SetOriginal(returned, original)
		statements := append(append([]*ast.Node{}, l.pending...), returned)
		loweredBody = l.factory.NewBlock(l.factory.NewNodeList(statements), true)
		l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(loweredBody, original)
		l.pending = nil
	}
	return l.factory.UpdateArrowFunction(
		arrow,
		l.visitor.VisitModifiers(arrow.Modifiers()),
		arrow.TypeParameters,
		l.visitor.VisitNodes(arrow.Parameters),
		arrow.Type,
		arrow.FullSignature,
		arrow.EqualsGreaterThanToken,
		loweredBody,
	)
}

// visitStatement gives every statement its own hoist scope so that temporaries
// introduced by propagation land immediately before the statement that needed
// them, and so evaluation-order accounting restarts at every statement.
func (l *lowerer) visitStatement(node *ast.Node) *ast.Node {
	saved := l.saveScope()
	_, hoistable := hoistableStatements[node.Kind]
	l.canHoist = hoistable && !declaresSeveralNames(node)
	l.impure = false
	l.pending = nil

	var lowered *ast.Node
	switch {
	case ast.IsImportDeclaration(node) && l.assetImportModule(node) != nil:
		lowered = l.lowerAssetImport(node, l.assetImportModule(node))
	case ast.IsExportDeclaration(node) && l.assetImportModule(node) != nil:
		lowered = l.lowerAssetExport(node, l.assetImportModule(node))
	case ast.IsImportDeclaration(node) && durableResolvedModule(l.checker, node.AsImportDeclaration().ModuleSpecifier):
		declaration := node.AsImportDeclaration()
		specifier := l.factory.NewStringLiteral(durablePreludeSpecifierFor(l.file.FileName()), ast.TokenFlagsNone)
		l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(specifier, declaration.ModuleSpecifier)
		lowered = l.factory.UpdateImportDeclaration(
			declaration,
			l.visitor.VisitModifiers(declaration.Modifiers()),
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.ImportClause),
			specifier,
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.Attributes),
		)
	case ast.IsExportDeclaration(node) && durableResolvedModule(l.checker, node.AsExportDeclaration().ModuleSpecifier):
		declaration := node.AsExportDeclaration()
		specifier := l.factory.NewStringLiteral(durablePreludeSpecifierFor(l.file.FileName()), ast.TokenFlagsNone)
		l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(specifier, declaration.ModuleSpecifier)
		lowered = l.factory.UpdateExportDeclaration(
			declaration,
			l.visitor.VisitModifiers(declaration.Modifiers()),
			declaration.IsTypeOnly,
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.ExportClause),
			specifier,
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.Attributes),
		)
	case ast.IsImportDeclaration(node) && l.prelude.isCompilerModuleSpecifier(node.AsImportDeclaration().ModuleSpecifier):
		declaration := node.AsImportDeclaration()
		specifier := l.factory.NewStringLiteral(preludeSpecifierFor(l.file.FileName()), ast.TokenFlagsNone)
		l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(specifier, declaration.ModuleSpecifier)
		lowered = l.factory.UpdateImportDeclaration(
			declaration,
			l.visitor.VisitModifiers(declaration.Modifiers()),
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.ImportClause),
			specifier,
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.Attributes),
		)
	case ast.IsExportDeclaration(node) && l.prelude.isCompilerModuleSpecifier(node.AsExportDeclaration().ModuleSpecifier):
		declaration := node.AsExportDeclaration()
		specifier := l.factory.NewStringLiteral(preludeSpecifierFor(l.file.FileName()), ast.TokenFlagsNone)
		l.emitContext.AssignSourceMapRange(specifier, declaration.ModuleSpecifier)
		lowered = l.factory.UpdateExportDeclaration(
			declaration,
			l.visitor.VisitModifiers(declaration.Modifiers()),
			declaration.IsTypeOnly,
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.ExportClause),
			specifier,
			l.visitor.VisitNode(declaration.Attributes),
		)
	case l.analysis != nil && l.analysis.isNominalErrorClass(node) &&
		!ast.HasSyntacticModifier(node, ast.ModifierFlagsAmbient):
		// 'declare class X extends Error {}' declares a foreign class; it emits
		// no binding, so a registration naming it would be a reference to
		// nothing. The reference frontend does emit one there, and the program
		// it accepts dies on load with "ReferenceError: X is not defined" — a
		// clean compile that cannot run, which is the exact failure the runtime
		// identity validator's own reasoning refuses to create. The identity
		// itself is still reserved: isNominalErrorClass, and so SMITHERS1150,
		// is unchanged, so two ambient Error classes of one name still collide.
		lowered = l.lowerNominalErrorClass(node)
	case node.Kind == ast.KindTryStatement:
